# Backlog

Source of truth for what to build next. The `next-story` skill reads and updates this file through `scripts/backlog.py`; edit it by hand freely, but keep the table shape.

- **Pri:** P0 blocks V0.1, P1 ships if time allows, P2 deferred (skipped unless asked).
- **Status:** `todo`, `in-progress`, `review`, `done`, `blocked`.
- **Depends:** story IDs that must be `done` first, comma-separated, or `-`.
- A milestone's stories unlock only when the previous milestone's gate is checked.

## Gates

- [x] S0: decision records DEC-1 to DEC-5 accepted; CI green on every PR
- [x] M0: a 1 GB CSV opens, scrolls smoothly, takes one edit, and saves safely
- [x] M1: viewer passes the LibreOffice navigation checklist; first rows of 1 GB in under 500 ms
- [x] M2: editor round-trips edits, paste, inserts, and undo on 1 GB without full rewrites before save
- [x] M3: all V0.1 benchmarks in apps/bricks/docs/BENCHMARKS.md pass on reference hardware (V0.1 release)
- [x] M4: `curl -fsSL https://bricks.cronkite.cc/install.sh | sh` installs per user on clean Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora VMs, and double-clicking a CSV opens it
- [x] M5: bricks opens the LibreOffice fixture workbook and a real-world .xlsx with formulas on 3+ sheets; after edits and Save, LibreOffice reopens it with the same values, formulas, and sheet names; styles, merged cells, charts, comments, and conditional formats survive Save (LibreOffice dump), and Bricks shows fonts, colours, fills, borders, alignment, and number formats; the XLSX, formula, and styled-XLSX rows in apps/bricks/docs/BENCHMARKS.md pass on the reference box
- [x] SL0: slate opens the fixture .txt, .md, and .docx files and a real-world .docx and shows their paragraphs, headings, emphasis, lists, tables, links, and pictures as LibreOffice reads them (dump comparison), read-only, reading off the UI thread, in a pageless column at the text width the user picks
- [x] SL1: slate edits and saves .txt, .md, and .docx with undo; LibreOffice reopens a saved .docx with the edits and everything else unchanged; an edited Markdown file changes only the edited blocks
- [x] SL2: slate finds and replaces text across paragraphs and table cells with undo, and after a crash offers back the unsaved edits of every open document without touching its file
- [x] SL3: slate draws each .docx in its own fonts and paragraph styles (LibreOffice's dump matches), the format bar sets style, font, size, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, colour, and alignment with undo, saved .docx files read back in LibreOffice with them, and re-wrap and typing stay within SL1's targets on a multi-font Word document
- [x] SL4: a .docx shaped like a Google Docs résumé (tab-aligned lines on a 7.5″ page, an embedded font, a bordered title, direct paragraph spacing and indents, "●" bullets) opens at its page width and draws its tab stops, embedded font, borders and shading, direct spacing and indents, and list markers as the file defines them (measured in the GUI check, and LibreOffice's dump matching the model), and saves them back unchanged
- [x] FR0: frame opens the fixture .pptx files and a real-world PowerPoint deck and shows every slide's shapes, text, and pictures where LibreOffice reads them (dump comparison); F5 plays the deck full screen
- [x] FR1: frame edits slide text on the slide and in the outline view, moves and resizes shapes with snapping, formats through the inspector, inserts text, shapes, pictures, and tables, and adds, deletes, and reorders slides, all with undo; File → New starts a deck; Save writes .pptx that LibreOffice reopens with the edits and everything else unchanged; the spec's reference task takes fewer input actions than in PowerPoint
- [x] ET0: etch opens a blank canvas and draws with the pencil and eraser in the chosen colours and sizes, zooms from 10% to 3200% around the pointer and pans, and undoes and redoes each stroke; a stroke on a 7680×4320 image draws each segment and frame in under 8 ms p99 (`--bench-stroke`); `scripts/ship.sh etch build` and `verify` pass
- [x] ET1: etch opens PNG, JPEG, and BMP files and draws on them with the brush, fill, colour picker, line, rectangle, ellipse, and text, each step undoable; Save and Save As write PNG, JPEG, and BMP that read back with the same pixels (JPEG within its loss, alpha composited onto white), atomically; closing an edited image asks Save, Discard, or Cancel
- [x] ET2: etch selects a rectangle and moves its pixels, cuts, copies, and pastes through the system clipboard (to and from another app), crops to the selection, resizes the image and the canvas, rotates and flips, all with undo: the spec's V0.1 definition of done
- [x] ET3: etch opens dropped files, lists recent files, picks colours by chooser and hex, switches every tool by its key, and after a crash offers back unsaved images without touching their files
- [x] XP0: a decision record (ADR, accepted by the user) says how the suite builds, packages, and tests on macOS and Windows, from a spike that built and started every app there and listed what breaks
- [x] XP1: Brick, Slate, Frame, and Etch install on macOS (Apple Silicon, macOS 14 or later) from a curl installer or a DMG as unsigned previews, start, open and save their files, keep their data in `~/Library`, use Command shortcuts and the macOS menu bar with native title bars, and open files from Finder; CI builds, tests, and smoke-tests them on macOS
- [x] XP2: Brick, Slate, Frame, and Etch install per user on Windows 10 and 11 (x64) from a Setup.exe or a PowerShell one-liner as unsigned previews, start without a console window, open and save their files, keep their data in `%APPDATA%` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, have native Windows title bars with a menu bar and follow Windows' light and dark mode, open files from Explorer, and uninstall from Settings → Apps; CI builds, tests, and smoke-tests them on Windows
- [ ] UP0: an installed release of Brick, Slate, Frame, and Etch updates itself to the next release on Linux, macOS, and Windows: checked at startup off the UI thread, downloaded and verified against an Ed25519-signed `latest.json`, applied for the next start without a prompt, "Updated to <App> X" shown once; tampered, mis-signed, other apps', and older manifests are refused with the installed app untouched; "Update Automatically" turns it off (ADR 0012)

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DEC-1 | S0 | P0 | done | - | Choose UI toolkit (GTK4 vs Slint vs egui vs Iced) | Spike renders a 1M-row virtual grid in the top two; ADR 0001 records frame time and input latency |
| DEC-2 | S0 | P0 | done | INFRA-2 | Choose source storage (mmap + sparse index vs chunked arena vs columnar) | Spike indexes 1 GB; ADR 0002 records index time, index RAM, random row fetch latency |
| DEC-3 | S0 | P0 | done | DEC-2 | Choose edit model (overlay + row map vs piece table) | Spike applies 10k random edits and an insert at row 500k; ADR 0003 shows O(1) or O(log n) per edit |
| DEC-4 | S0 | P0 | done | DEC-2 | Choose sort/filter representation | Spike sorts a 2M-row numeric column in under 3 s; ADR 0004 |
| DEC-5 | S0 | P0 | done | - | Choose concurrency model (Rayon + channels + cancel tokens vs async) | Spike runs a search while a timer simulates 60 fps; frame budget held; ADR 0005 |
| INFRA-1 | S0 | P0 | done | - | CI: fmt, clippy -D warnings, test on every PR | Workflow in .github/workflows/ci.yml passes on main |

| UPD-1 | UP0 | P0 | done | DEC-12 | Signed release manifests | `scripts/release_json.py write` adds `"app"` to `latest.json` and writes `latest.json.sig` (base64 Ed25519 over the file's exact bytes) with the key at `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY`; a key file inside the work tree is refused; `scripts/release_key.py new` makes a key pair, and the two release public keys are in the repo; `release_json.py check` and `ship.sh verify` fail on a missing or bad signature (script tests: good, tampered byte, other key, missing `.sig`); `ship.sh upload --dry-run` sends `latest.json.sig` beside `latest.json`; older installers still read the new `latest.json` (`install.sh` and `install.ps1` tests unchanged and green) |
| UPD-2 | UP0 | P0 | review | UPD-1 | `suite-update`: check, verify, download, and stage at startup in every app | A new toolkit-free crate `suite-update` (depends on suite-core, suite-platform, suite-file only; CLAUDE.md's suite rules and crate table updated) fetches `latest.json` and its `.sig` with `ureq`/`rustls`/`ring` on a background thread started after the first window shows, and stages a newer release in the app's cache folder's `updates/`; tests against a local HTTP server (signed with a `test-key`-feature key) prove: a newer release downloaded, sha256- and size-checked, and staged; refused with nothing staged: a bad signature, another app's manifest, an equal or older version, a missing platform, a checksum or size mismatch, a dropped connection; no request at all when `CRONKITE_NO_UPDATE=1`, the setting is off, or the binary isn't an installer's install (dev builds); all four apps start it; `rust_licenses.py` passes for every app and target with the new crates; Linux, macOS, and Windows CI green |
| UPD-3 | UP0 | P0 | todo | UPD-2 | The setting and the "updated" notice in all four apps | Each app has "Update Automatically" (a check item next to About: the app menu on Linux and macOS, Help on Windows), on by default, kept across restarts in the app's settings folder (test); the first start after an update shows "Updated to <App> X" once, without blocking or stealing focus, and never again (test of the recorded version; GUI checks screenshot it in Brick, Slate, Frame, and Etch); each app's spec and download page, and `llms.txt`, say the apps update themselves and how to turn it off |
| UPD-4 | UP0 | P0 | todo | UPD-2 | Apply updates on Linux | A staged release replaces `~/.local/bin/<app>`, its desktop entry, icon, and licence files by write-beside-and-rename while the app runs, never touching `mimeapps.list`; `scripts/check_update.sh` in Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora containers installs an old build of each app with its `install.sh`, serves a newer signed release locally (`CRONKITE_UPDATE_URL`), starts and quits the app, and the next start's `--version` is the new one with the notice shown and default apps unchanged; a tampered tarball and a mis-signed manifest leave the old version installed and working |
| UPD-5 | UP0 | P0 | todo | UPD-2 | Apply updates on macOS | A staged release is unpacked beside `<App>.app` and swapped in when the last running instance exits (owner locks in `updates/`), or before the window at the next start with a re-exec; `scripts/check_mac_update.sh` on a macOS runner (Homebrew moved away) installs an old build of each app with `install.sh`, serves a newer signed release, and proves: quitting then starting runs the new version with the notice; a second running instance defers the swap until both have quit; a staged update left by a killed process is applied at the next start; the bundle's ad-hoc signature verifies (`codesign --verify`) and it carries no quarantine attribute; a tampered release is refused |
| UPD-6 | UP0 | P0 | todo | UPD-2 | Apply updates on Windows | When the last running instance exits it starts the staged `Setup.exe` detached with `/VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /CURRENTUSER`; `scripts/check_win_update.ps1` on a Windows runner (MSYS2 moved away) installs an old build of each app with `install.ps1`, serves a newer signed release, and proves: after quitting, Settings → Apps shows the new version and the next start reports it with the notice; a second running instance defers the update until both have quit; Setup closes no running instance; a tampered release is refused and the old version keeps working |
| UPD-7 | UP0 | P0 | todo | UPD-3, UPD-4, UPD-5, UPD-6 | Ship the updater, then update it in the wild | One release of all four apps carries the updater (its notes and download pages say to reinstall once); a following release, signed and uploaded by `ship.sh`, is picked up by an installed copy of the first on Linux, macOS, and Windows (recorded `--version` before and after, screenshots of the notice); the ship-it skill covers signing with `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY` and the offline backup key |

## Notes

Story notes, decisions made mid-story, and follow-ups go here, newest first.

- **2026-10-21 UPD-2:** `suite-update`: check, verify, download, and stage at startup. Branch `story/upd-2-suite-update`, stacked on UPD-1's.
  - New crate `crates/suite-update` (depends on suite-core and suite-platform only; toolkit-free; CLAUDE.md's suite rules and crate table): `start(app, CARGO_PKG_VERSION)`, called by each app from its first window's `map` (`connect_window_added` in each `main.rs`), runs one check on a thread named `update`, once per process. `Gate::skip`: compiled out (`CRONKITE_NO_SELF_UPDATE` at build time, for packages others update), `CRONKITE_NO_UPDATE` set (not empty or `0`), "Update Automatically" off (`automatic`/`set_automatic`: `automatic=false` in `update` in the app's settings folder; UPD-3 puts it in the menus), or not an installer's install (`install`: Linux `~/.local/bin/<command>` resolving to the running program with `<data>/applications/<app id>.desktop`; macOS `suite_platform::bundle::install_root`, new, in a folder a probe file can be made in; Windows the install folder). Then `latest.json` (64 KiB at most) and `latest.json.sig` (1 KiB) from `AppKind::release_url` (new in suite-core) or `CRONKITE_UPDATE_URL`; `manifest::verify`: the Ed25519 signature over the bytes as fetched with a trusted key (`keys::trusted`: the two release keys compiled in from `packaging/release-keys`; with the `test-key` feature also `crates/suite-update/test-key/test.pub`, whose private half is committed there on purpose), `"app"` this app's command, `version` X.Y.Z, this platform's entry with a 64-hex sha256; only a newer version is fetched. `stage`: under `updates/.lock` (`File::try_lock`; a second instance gets `Busy` and fetches nothing), into `updates/.download`, refused past the manifest's `bytes`, then by size and SHA-256 (ring), synced, renamed to the URL's file name (letters, digits, `._-` only), then `updates/staged` (`version=`, `file=`, `sha256=`) written by temp and rename, and older files removed (`owner-*.lock` kept for UPD-5). `staged(dir)` reads it back for the apply step. Failures: one `WARN update: …` line, nothing left but `.lock`.
  - Dependencies: `ureq` 3.4 (`rustls-no-provider`, `platform-verifier`), `rustls` 0.23 (ring), `ring` 0.17, `base64` 0.23 (already in the tree through ureq), `serde`/`serde_json` (the workspace's). No `webpki-roots`. ADR 0012 item 3 amended: the system's verifier (`rustls-platform-verifier`) rather than `rustls-native-certs` alone. `rust_licenses.py` now reads a licence text written as source comments (`untrusted`'s ISC `LICENSE.txt`), with a test; all four apps pass for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, `aarch64-apple-darwin`, and `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` (Brick 134/135/134 crates).
  - Found while smoking it: ureq's connection pool reused a connection Python's `http.server` had closed, failing the `.sig` request with "Peer disconnected" about one run in eight. The agent now keeps no idle connections (`max_idle_connections(0)`); test `a_connection_the_server_closed_is_never_reused` (a server that answers keep-alive, then hangs up on reuse) failed with the pool and passes without it.
  - Proof: 19 tests in `suite-update` against a real HTTP server on 127.0.0.1 with manifests signed by the test key: a newer release downloaded, checked, staged, and not downloaded again; a later one replacing it; refused with nothing staged and the file never requested: another key's signature, a changed manifest byte, a garbled or missing `.sig`, another app's manifest and one naming no app, equal and older versions, no entry for this platform; refused with nothing left: same-size changed bytes, longer, shorter, a dropped connection (and the next check then succeeds), no server, a file name escaping the folder; `Busy` with zero requests while another holds the lock; zero requests when compiled out, `CRONKITE_NO_UPDATE=1`/`yes`, setting off, or not installed, and requests again with it empty or `0`; the setting's round trip; install.sh's layout counting and others not; only the two release keys trusted without `test-key`; version order. Live, a throwaway example: `https://dl.cronkite.cc/latest.json` fetched over HTTPS through the platform verifier (its `.sig` 404s until UPD-7's release), self-signed.badssl.com and expired.badssl.com refused ("UnknownIssuer", "certificate expired"); a `latest.json` signed with the real release key (`release-1`) by `release_json.py`, served locally, made it download Brick 0.5.0's live Linux tarball and stage it with sha256 `499ae385…`, equal to `dist/`'s. All four apps (debug builds) copied into a scratch home as install.sh lays them out and run under Broadway, with `CRONKITE_UPDATE_URL` at a local server holding each app's 9.9.9 signed with the release key: each logged "update: <App> 9.9.9 downloaded for the next start" and staged it (3 requests each); the same binary with `CRONKITE_NO_UPDATE=1`, and the build in `target/debug`, made 0 requests. `cargo fmt`, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, script tests.
  - Not yet run: Linux, macOS, and Windows CI need this branch pushed (ask). No `rustup` here to build the macOS or Windows targets locally; their dependency graphs pass the licence check.
  - For later: `ship.sh verify` should check a release binary trusts only the release keys (built without `test-key`), as ADR 0012 item 8 says (UPD-7). The apply step (UPD-4 to UPD-6) must check the staged file's sha256 against `staged` again before installing it.
- **2026-10-21 UPD-1:** signed release manifests. Branch `story/upd-1-signed-manifests`, stacked on `story/dec-12-self-updating-apps`.
  - `scripts/release_key.py` (new): Ed25519 through the `openssl` command (OpenSSL 3; no Python package needed, standard PEM key files): `new KEY_DIR NAME` writes `KEY_DIR/NAME.key` (0600 in a 0700 folder, encrypted with `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY_PASS` when set) and `packaging/release-keys/NAME.pub`; `sign`, `verifies`. A key path inside the work tree is refused by `new` and `sign` (resolved, so a link into the repo counts).
  - The release keys: `packaging/release-keys/release-1.pub` and `release-2.pub`, made 2026-10-21; their private halves are in `~/.config/cronkite/release-keys/` on the release box, unencrypted. `.env.ship` points `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY` at release-1. For the user: move `release-2.key` offline (and encrypt both: `openssl pkey -in K -aes-256-cbc -out K.enc`, then `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY_PASS`).
  - `scripts/release_json.py`: `latest.json` starts with `"app"` (the command); `write APP URL VERSION … OUT` writes OUT and, with `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY`, `OUT.sig` (base64 of the 64-byte signature over OUT's exact bytes, newline included), refusing a key whose public half isn't in `packaging/release-keys`; without the key it writes OUT unsigned, drops a stale `.sig`, and says so. `check` also wants `"app"`, each file named `<app>-…`, and `LATEST.sig` verifying with a repository key.
  - `scripts/ship.sh`: `latest` passes the app and signs when the key is set (the runners' `build --macos`/`--windows` never have `DOWNLOAD_BASE_URL`, so CI never writes or signs); `upload` writes and checks `latest.json` (signature included) before any `r2 object put`, then puts `latest.json` and `latest.json.sig` last; `verify` fails unsigned through `check`. `.env.ship.example` names `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY`; the ship-it skill says how signing stops an upload.
  - Proof: `scripts/test_release_json.py` (13 tests, 8 new): either release key signs and verifies; a tampered byte fails; a key not in the keys folder can't sign and its signature fails `check`; unsigned drops a stale `.sig` and `check` fails; a key inside the repository is refused; an encrypted key signs with its passphrase, not without; the CLI refuses a non-release key, writes unsigned without one, and `check` then fails; `check` wants `"app"` and the app's own file names; the old installers' greedy sed still finds Linux's URL with `"app"` first. `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts`: 67 OK, 1 skipped. `scripts/ship.sh brick upload --dry-run` with the release key: "dist/latest.json: brick 0.5.0 for linux-x86_64, macos-arm64, windows-x86_64, signed", `check` ok, the eight release files, then `latest.json` and `latest.json.sig`; with `CRONKITE_RELEASE_KEY` empty: "latest.json.sig missing: unsigned", exit 1 before any put; with a missing key file: exit 1. The real `sites/brick/install.sh`, fed the new signed `latest.json` from a local server (`BRICK_DOWNLOAD_URL`), installed Brick 0.5.0 into a scratch home (`brick --version`: `brick 0.5.0`). `install.ps1` reads only `version` and `platforms.'windows-x86_64'` by name (no pwsh here to run it). `dist/latest.json` was put back to the published one afterwards.
  - For UPD-2: the apps compile in `packaging/release-keys/*.pub` (SPKI PEM: the raw 32-byte key is the last 32 bytes of the base64 body) and verify `latest.json.sig` over the bytes as fetched, then check `"app"`.
- **2026-10-21 DEC-12:** the apps update themselves (user decisions: download silently and apply on the next start; Ed25519-signed `latest.json` with an offline key; on by default with a setting). Design in `docs/adr/0012-self-updating-apps.md`, accepted by the user the same day. Today's releases have no updater, so their users reinstall once (UPD-7).
- **2026-10-17 PKG-7:** one installer for the whole suite, and cronkite.cc for three platforms. Branch `story/pkg-7-suite-installer`.
  - `sites/cronkite/install.sh` (POSIX sh, Linux and macOS): `curl -fsSL https://cronkite.cc/install.sh | sh [-s -- [--uninstall] [APP…]]` fetches `https://<app>.cronkite.cc/install.sh` for each app chosen (all four by default; names case-insensitive, run in the suite's order, an unknown name refused before anything runs) and runs it with its own `sh` (`--uninstall` passed on), stdin from `/dev/null` so a child can't eat the piped script (the whole script is a `main` called on its last line). It prints each app's installer output under `== Brick (1 of 4): URL`, then a result per app and `Cronkite: 4 of 4 apps installed` (or `…; failed: Frame, Etch`, `…; needs GTK 4: Brick`). Exit 1 if any failed, else 2 if any app's installer exited 2 (installed, GTK 4 missing), else 0. Only an OS other than Linux or macOS is refused here (pointing Windows users at install.ps1); x86_64, GTK 4, Apple Silicon, and macOS 14 stay the app installers' checks. `--help` prints usage.
  - `sites/cronkite/install.ps1` (Windows PowerShell 5.1 and 7): `irm https://cronkite.cc/install.ps1 | iex`; `-Apps slate,frame` or `$env:CRONKITE_APPS = "slate frame"` (commas or spaces) picks apps, `-Uninstall` removes, through `& ([scriptblock]::Create((irm …))) -Apps …/-Uninstall` as the app installers take `-Uninstall`. Each app's `install.ps1` is downloaded as text (bytes decoded, a BOM dropped) and run as a scriptblock in its own scope, its output to the host; what it throws is that app's failure, printed in red, and the next app runs. Same summary lines; then it throws `cronkite install: Etch failed (see above)` if any app failed (the app installers fail by throwing too, never `exit`, which would close the user's window under `iex`).
  - Tests' hooks: `CRONKITE_SITE_BASE=URL` (both) takes `URL/<app>/install.{sh,ps1}` instead of each app's site; the app installers' own `<APP>_DOWNLOAD_URL` pass through the environment untouched.
  - `scripts/ship.sh suite-site` already copied `sites/cronkite` whole; it now checks both installers are there and prints the files it deploys (`cronkite.cc: index.html install.ps1 install.sh llms.txt shots`, also in `--dry-run`). No placeholders: the app sites' addresses are fixed, so an app release needs no suite deploy.
  - cronkite.cc (`index.html`, `llms.txt`): title, description, tagline, footer, the Install intro, Requirements (now one labelled paragraph per platform), and the screenshots caption say Linux, macOS, and Windows (preview). Install leads with the suite: "Linux and macOS" (`curl … | sh`) and "Windows (preview)" (`irm … | iex`), each in the page's primary command box with Copy and how to pick apps; the per-app commands follow under "Or one app at a time" (h4 per app, so the cards' `#install-brick` links still land); Uninstall leads with the two suite commands, one app's uninstall in a note. The visitor's platform first, detected as the app pages do (`userAgentData.platform`, `navigator.platform`, "Windows NT"): Windows puts Windows' suite command, per-app commands, uninstall, and requirements first; a Mac titles the curl block "macOS and Linux" and keeps macOS's requirements first; Linux moves Linux's requirements first. Checked in headless Chromium on `dist/cronkite-site` after `ship.sh suite-site --dry-run`, with Windows, Mac, and Linux user agents and platforms: the install blocks' order per platform as above, Copy puts `curl -fsSL https://cronkite.cc/install.sh | sh` on the clipboard, screenshots of the masthead, Install, Requirements, Uninstall, and footer at 1280 px and Install at 390 px looked at.
  - Proof: `scripts/check_install_suite.sh` (new) in Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora containers as a normal user, the repo's installers served locally and the live releases (Brick 0.5.0, Slate 0.5.0, Frame 0.3.0, Etch 0.4.0): `slate paint` refused with nothing installed; without GTK 4, `brick` installed with "needs GTK 4: Brick", exit 2; `slate frame` installs exactly those; a site without Frame's installer and Etch's download URL pointing nowhere, `brick frame etch`: Brick installed, Frame's and Etch's failures reported, "1 of 3 apps installed; failed: Frame, Etch", exit 1; all four installed, each `--version` the live version, menu entries; `--uninstall`: "4 of 4 apps removed", no binary, menu entry, icon, licence folder, or file default left. All three `ok`, "SUITE INSTALL OK" (locally and in CI). New workflow `install-suite.yml` (push touching `sites/*/install.{sh,ps1}` or the checks, or dispatch; no build) runs it on ubuntu-latest, `scripts/check_mac_install_suite.sh` on macos-14 with Homebrew moved away (`slate frame` → exactly Slate.app and Frame.app in `~/Applications`; Etch's download failing beside Brick's install, exit 1; all four, each starting at its live version; uninstall leaves none), and `scripts/check_win_install_suite.ps1` on windows-latest in Windows PowerShell 5.1 (`$env:CRONKITE_APPS="slate paint"` refused; `-Apps slate,frame` → only Slate's and Frame's HKCU Uninstall keys; Etch failing beside Brick, a failing exit; `irm | iex` → all four keys at the live versions with `bin\<app>.exe`, a screenshot of Settings > Apps listing Brick 0.5.0 by Cronkite; `-Uninstall` → no key, no folder). Run https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/39025964456: linux, macos, windows green.
  - Decisions: the macOS and Windows checks live in a new narrowly triggered workflow rather than `release-macos.yml`'s verify job, which needs all four apps built first; it is the cheapest clean runner (Homebrew moved away on the Mac). `release-macos.yml` and `release-windows.yml` no longer trigger on `sites/cronkite/install.*` (their `sites/*/install.*` paths matched the suite's). GTK 4 missing counts as installed (the binary is in place) but exits 2 like the app installers. App names on the Linux page stay lowercase (`slate frame`), as the commands are.
  - Not deployed: `scripts/ship.sh suite-site` after the merge puts the page and both installers on cronkite.cc; until then `https://cronkite.cc/install.sh` is a 404. For later: the Settings > Apps screenshot shows the list sorted by name, so only Brick is on screen; the four keys are what's checked.

- **2026-10-16 WIN-5:** Windows bundles and per-user installers. `.github/workflows/release-windows.yml` (manual with an `apps` input, release tags, pushes that change the bundling) builds each app on `windows-latest` with MSYS2's UCRT64 GTK and `scripts/win_bundle.sh`, uploads `windows-<app>` (Setup.exe and `.sha256`), and its `verify` job, a fresh runner with `C:\msys64` moved away and a PATH of Windows' own folders, runs `scripts/check_win_install.ps1`. WIN-6 wires `ship.sh`, `latest.json`, and the pages around it.
  - Bundle (`<App>\`, MSYS2's prefix layout, which GLib, GTK, gdk-pixbuf, and fontconfig find from their DLLs, so no variables are needed): `bin\<app>.exe` stripped and the 59 DLLs it and the 14 gdk-pixbuf loaders import, found by `objdump -p` recursively (delay-loaded ones too: `vulkan-1.dll`), Windows' own left out; MSYS2's `loaders.cache` (relative paths); GTK's `org.gtk.gtk4.*` schemas compiled; Adwaita's index and symbolic icons; hicolor with the app's icon as PNGs and a written `index.theme` (hicolor-icon-theme's is GPL); MSYS2's `fonts.conf` with only `WINDOWSFONTDIR`/`WINDOWSUSERFONTDIR` and `<cachedir prefix="xdg">fontconfig</cachedir>`, `conf.d`; `fonts\` (Brick, Slate, Frame: WIN-4 finds `..\fonts`); `<app>.ico` (rsvg-convert PNGs 16–256 in a PNG-entry .ico, `scripts/win_app.py ico`); LICENSE files; `licenses\`. Folder 64–73 MB.
  - `suite_platform::bundle::init` on Windows: an install folder is `<Name>\bin\<app>.exe` beside `etc\fonts\fonts.conf` and `licenses\THIRD-PARTY.txt`; it sets only `XDG_CACHE_HOME` (also through `_wputenv_s`, fontconfig reads `getenv`) to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<Name>`, so fontconfig's cache is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<Name>\fontconfig`, not the shared `%LOCALAPPDATA%\fontconfig`. Tests `only_a_windows_install_folder_with_its_files_counts`, `a_variable_reaches_the_c_librarys_environment` (Windows).
  - No D-Bus (decision): `gdbus.exe` isn't bundled. GLib then starts no session bus (DEC-11 R13's `gdbus.exe` child, `%USERPROFILE%\.dbus-keyrings` left after uninstall), and GApplication goes on without one (`gapplicationimpl-dbus.c`: "proceed as a normal non-unique application"; the apps are NON_UNIQUE, WIN-4). Checked: no child process, nothing left in the profile.
  - No GPL code (as MAC-7): MSYS2's cairo links lzo2 (GPL-2.0-or-later) into `libcairo-script-interpreter-2.dll`, and its libtiff links jbigkit (GPL-2.0). `scripts/win_libs.sh` rebuilds both from MSYS2's own source packages of the installed versions (cairo 1.18.6-2 with MSYS2's two patches and meson options plus `-Dlzo=disabled`; libtiff 4.7.2-2 with its configure options plus `--disable-jbig`), pinned with the source packages' sha256; it fails when MSYS2's package moves on (update the pin). Cached as `target/win-libs`. The bundle takes them first; no DLL named lzo or jbig ends up in it.
  - Licences: `scripts/win_licenses.py` maps every bundled file to its MSYS2 package through pacman's local database, copies each package's `share/licenses` texts (gstreamer, gst-plugins-base, and gst-plugins-bad-libs install none: taken from the upstream tarball inside MSYS2's source package), and writes `licenses\THIRD-PARTY.txt` (version, licence, MSYS2 source package as the LGPL source offer, recipe). It fails on GPL-only licences; GCC's runtime (`libgcc_s_seh-1.dll`, `libstdc++-6.dll`, GPL-3.0-or-later WITH GCC-exception-3.1) passes by its exception, noted; libintl (gettext-runtime) and liblzma (xz) count by their library's licence as on macOS; libiconv's GPL is its documentation's. 38 packages. `THIRD-PARTY-RUST.txt` for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`: Brick 112 crates, Slate 95, Frame 82, Etch 87. Tests: `scripts/test_win_licenses.py`, `scripts/test_win_app.py`.
  - Installer (`packaging/windows/setup.iss`, one template; `scripts/win_app.py iss APP` gives each app's defines and `[Registry]` from its desktop entry's types): `PrivilegesRequired=lowest`, `{autopf}\<Name>` = `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\<Name>`, AppId `dev.cronkite.<Name>` (`HKCU\…\Uninstall\dev.cronkite.<Name>_is1`), Start menu shortcut, version info, Windows 10+, x64; an update first deletes the old bin/lib/share/etc/fonts/licenses. Types under HKCU: ProgID `Cronkite.<Name>.<ext>` per extension in the extension's `OpenWithProgids` (a string value: Inno's `ValueType: none` writes no value), `Applications\<app>.exe` (FriendlyAppName, SupportedTypes), `Software\Cronkite\<Name>\Capabilities` and `RegisteredApplications`; never an extension's default. Every entry is undone at uninstall (shared keys only when left empty, incl. `Classes\Applications`, `RegisteredApplications`, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs` when Setup made it). Name `<app>-<version>-x86_64-windows-setup.exe`. Inno Setup 6.7.1 is on `windows-latest`.
  - Uninstall and user data (decision): Windows' convention, as the Linux and macOS installers: `%APPDATA%\Cronkite\<Name>` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<Name>` stay; a silent uninstall keeps them; by hand (Settings → Apps) it asks "Also remove your <Name> settings, recent files, and recovered work?", No by default. The fontconfig cache always goes.
  - Defaults (observed): where a type had no default (the runner's `.csv .tsv .xlsx .xlsm .docx .md .markdown .pptx .pptm`), Windows now opens it with the one program registered for it (`AssocQueryString`: the app instead of `OpenWith.exe`), a Windows choice, no default written; types with one (`.txt` Notepad, `.png .jpg .jpeg .bmp` with a UserChoice) keep it.
  - `install.ps1` (each `sites/<app>/`): reads `latest.json`'s `platforms."windows-x86_64"` = `{"url", "sha256", "bytes"}` of the Setup.exe (flat, as `linux-x86_64`; WIN-6 writes it), checks the sha256, runs `/VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /CURRENTUSER /CLOSEAPPLICATIONS /LOG`, prints `Installed <Name> <version>` (or `Updated a -> b`) from the Uninstall key; `<APP>_DOWNLOAD_URL` overrides the base (test hook, as the curl installers); `& ([scriptblock]::Create((irm …/install.ps1))) -Uninstall` runs the uninstaller silently and waits for it (Scoop's pattern; `irm | iex` can't take arguments). Setup.exe sizes: Brick 21529198 bytes, Slate 21317346, Frame 21021675, Etch 18571991. - **2026-10-16 WIN-4 follow-up:** Every app's file drop answers COPY on Windows. WIN-3 found GDK's Win32 drop keeps the actions of GTK's last status, which is none for the move that crosses into another widget, so a drag that stopped just past a widget edge was refused; Brick got the fix. `suite_ui::launch::file_drop_target()` now makes the FileList/COPY drop target with, on Windows only, `enter` and `motion` answering COPY; Brick's start window, Slate's window, Frame's window and canvas (pictures), and Etch's window use it (Frame's slide list's row move isn't a file drop). `check_win_open.ps1`'s drags now rest, then make their last move across a widget edge, from the window's top-left corner: Brick into the "Recent" heading, Slate over the Open button's top edge, Frame from the canvas into the slide list, Etch from the tool column into the canvas. Without the helper in Slate, Frame, and Etch, all three refused (effect 0; [run 38971324914](https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38971324914)); with it every drop opened, `check_win_open: OK`, `check_win_ui: OK`, every job green ([run 38971361145](https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38971361145)). Linux: `check_drop.sh` DROP OK, `check_slate.sh` SLATE OK, `check_frame.sh` FRAME OK, `check_etch.sh` ETCH OK.
- **2026-10-16 WIN-3:** Windows-like windows. Each app is a GUI-subsystem program (`#![cfg_attr(windows, windows_subsystem = "windows")]`), `suite_platform::console::attach()` runs first in every `main` (AttachConsole(ATTACH_PARENT_PROCESS); std handles the starter redirected are put back, so pipes and files keep the output); `suite_ui::chrome` gains Windows: `MENU_BAR` is true on macOS and Windows, `chrome::init()` (after `renderer::init`) sets `GTK_CSD=0` unless set (GTK draws its own title bar on win32 otherwise, even without a header bar: `gtk_window_should_use_csd`), `menu_bar(kind, menus)` ends with Help → About <App> on Windows (Window stays macOS's), `exit_section()` (File → Exit, `app.quit`) on Windows, and `install` on Windows sets `show-menubar` on every `ApplicationWindow` (GTK's default is FALSE) and titles untitled windows (GTK's alerts) with the app's name (GDK shows "." otherwise). The toolbar row is macOS's. Shortcuts stay Control (`Primary::HERE`).
  - Dark mode: `suite_platform::appearance` (now `appearance/{macos,windows}.rs`) on Windows reads `HKCU\…\Themes\Personalize\AppsUseLightTheme` (0 dark; absent light) and a thread waits on `…\Themes` (subtree, `RegNotifyChangeKeyValue`) and calls `suite_ui::appearance::set_dark` on a change; that also sets `DWMWA_USE_IMMERSIVE_DARK_MODE` (20, else 19) on every toplevel, and on each new one at realize (`appearance/title_bars.rs`, HWND through `gdk_win32_surface_get_handle`). `windows-sys = "0.61.2"` in the workspace (Console, Registry, Dwm features). Brick's portal reader is off on Windows (it autolaunched a D-Bus bus, DEC-11 R13).
  - Frame: its window's capture-phase key handler leaves keys to the menu bar while the focus is in it (F10, then Down opened nothing: Down changed slides).
  - CI: the `windows` job runs `scripts/check_win_ui.ps1` (Windows PowerShell 5.1) after WIN-4's open check on the same debug build, uploading `windows-ui`. Per app: PE subsystem 2, no console process, `--version` through a pipe, a `cmd` redirect, and a new console window (read back with `GetBufferContents`), WS_CAPTION/SYSMENU/THICKFRAME/MINIMIZEBOX/MAXIMIZEBOX and a 31 px system title bar, F10+Down opens File, Left wraps to Help, Enter opens "About <App>", Win+Left snaps, File → Exit ends the process, and light/dark live and at start by brightness (title bar 243 light, 32 dark; menu bar row 216–245 light, 47–59 dark).

The first green run, merged with WIN-2: https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38953303779. Screenshots looked at: native title bars with min/max/close; every app's menu bar row (Brick File, Edit, Sheet, Format, Data, Help; Slate File, Edit, Format, View, Help; Frame File, Edit, Insert, Slide, View, Help; Etch File, Edit, View, Image, Help); File menus with Ctrl keys and Exit; the About Brick, Slate, Frame, and Etch dialogs with icons (MSYS2 decodes the SVG); dark title bars, rows, and menus, live and at start; Slate's page staying paper; snapped windows. Linux on the merge (c44e6ac): fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (171 targets, 842 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored), script tests (36 OK); release builds: SLATE OK, FRAME OK, ETCH OK, LOOK OK, FORMAT OK, and DROP OK after the drop fix.
  - Decisions and deviations: Help holds only About (its popover is taller than its one item, GTK's sizing); Exit shows no key except where Linux binds Ctrl+Q (Slate, Etch); Alt+F4 closes one window, as Windows does. About uses GtkAboutDialog's own header bar (client-drawn), and modal alerts get the system frame with a disabled maximize. Output from a GUI program lands after the shell's next prompt (the shell doesn't wait).
  - For later: Brick's natural width (about 1166 px, its format bar) exceeds a 1024 px screen, so Win+Left snaps it to the left at full height but not half width.
  - Brick's drop after merging WIN-4: `check_win_open` failed to drop on Brick's start window at its centre (660,340) in run 38954731930 and its re-run. Slate, Frame, and Etch accepted their drops. Cause, from `GDK_DEBUG=dnd` and an instrumented Brick on the runner (throwaway branch `spike/win-3-drop`, since deleted): GDK's Win32 drop sets the drop's actions to those of each status GTK reports (`gdk_win32_drop_status`). Where the pointer crosses into another widget, GTK reports none for one move. GDK's `dragover` runs the motion again only when the point, keys, or offered effects change. So when the drag's last move crossed into the "Recent" heading and the pointer stopped, the actions stayed empty (logged `actions=0x0`) and the drop was refused. The new menu bar row had moved that heading under the drop point. With the actions logged, the full check reproduced the refusal. When the target answered COPY, every drop opened in all 14 tries (both frames, with and without the menu bar, three heights, with recent files). The fix: on Windows, Brick's file drop target answers COPY on enter and on every move, which sets the actions again. Slate's, Frame's, and Etch's drop targets would refuse in the same case and may need the same.
- **2026-10-16 WIN-4:** Fonts, renderer, and opening files on Windows. The `windows` job of `ci.yml` now fetches the fonts before testing and, after the tests, builds the four apps (debug) and runs `scripts/check_win_open.ps1` (Windows PowerShell 5.1, C# through Add-Type), uploading `windows-open` (shots, logs, summary, env).
  - Opening (decision): `suite_ui::launch::run` gives `g_application_run` the program alone and opens the files from `handle-local-options` (register, `open`, exit 0), the same on every OS. GLib on Windows ignores the argv it's given and keeps as many of the real command line's last arguments as it was given (`gapplication.c`), so before this `brick FILE --bench-open` handed `--bench-open` to GApplication and `etch a.png -- -b.png` lost `a.png`. Now GApplication never parses the command line; each app's own parser (Rust's `args_os`, UTF-16 on Windows) is the only one. `--version` already exits before GTK (WIN-3 owns its console). The apps stay `NON_UNIQUE`: each Windows launch is its own process. Test `launch::tests::files_reach_open_and_none_activates` now runs on Windows too, with `ü 文件.docx`, a `-`-name, and a child process whose command line ends in libtest's own options.
  - Fonts (decision, on evidence): Pango on fontconfig on Windows as on macOS (`PANGOCAIRO_BACKEND=fc`, also set through UCRT's `_putenv_s`: Pango reads it with `getenv`, whose copy of the environment `SetEnvironmentVariableW` doesn't touch), the bundled folder found as MAC-6 does (`../fonts` beside the exe's folder: `target\fonts`, and `<App>\fonts` for WIN-5's `<App>\bin\<app>.exe`). Evidence (run 38950422644 shots): Pango's Win32 font map drew Slate's Markdown (Liberation Sans) in a fallback face with other line breaks and logged "couldn't load font"; AddFontResourceExW doesn't reach Pango's DirectWrite map since Pango 1.50.11; Slate's embedded fonts need fontconfig. Windows' own Calibri, Calibri Light, Cambria, Arial, Times New Roman, Courier New, and Segoe UI draw as themselves (fontconfig prefers installed families; `env.txt` fc-match); the stand-ins draw where a family is missing or named.
  - Line breaks: GDK on Windows forces `gtk-hint-font-metrics` TRUE (`gdkwin32misc.c`), which rounded Courier New's widths and broke its lines elsewhere than on Linux. `suite_ui::fonts::unhinted_metrics` (hint metrics off, unrounded positions, slight hinting) is used by Slate's `Page::document_context` (a Windows variant, as macOS has) and Frame's slide context; the UI keeps GTK's. After it, office-fonts.docx (Arch reference: Carlito, Caladea, Liberation) breaks every Calibri, Arial, Times New Roman, and Courier New line as on Linux; real Cambria wraps one word earlier than Caladea in the Cambria body paragraph (a font difference: not Caladea). `stand-ins.docx` and `stand-ins.pptx` (`scripts/office_fonts.py --stand-ins`, naming Carlito, Caladea, Liberation) draw in the bundled fonts with Linux's breaks in every line.
  - Paths for fontconfig: fontconfig (`GetFileAttributesExA`, `opendir`) and FreeType (`CreateFileA`) open files by ANSI names on Windows, so `suite_ui::fonts::fontconfig_path` gives the ANSI code page's bytes, else the short 8.3 name; Slate's embedded faces use it too (was UTF-8). Test `pango_takes_fontconfig_which_reads_windows_fonts_and_the_bundled_ones` (Windows): the font map is `PangoCairoFcFontMap`, Liberation from `…\suite-ui fonts é`, Carlito and Caladea from `…\suite-ui fonts 文件` (short name), Segoe UI, Arial, Times, Courier New as themselves. For WIN-5: a user name outside the ANSI code page on a volume without short names leaves the fonts unread (a warning, fallbacks); an `activeCodePage` UTF-8 manifest would end that. A bundle needs fontconfig's `etc\fonts` beside `bin` (fontconfig finds it from its DLL).
  - Renderer (decision): GTK's choice on Windows (`suite_ui::renderer::WINDOWS_DEFAULT = None`; ADR 0011 item 5 amended). On the runner GTK tries WGL (only GL 1.1) and EGL (no libEGL in MSYS2) and draws with cairo; `GSK_RENDERER=gl`, `ngl`, and `vulkan` fail to realize and fall back to cairo, drawn right, never black. Real-hardware GL through WGL is a hand check; flip the constant if it draws black.
  - Drag and drop: GTK 4 reads a drop's files from the shell's "Shell IDList Array", not CF_HDROP (`gdkclipdrop-win32.c`), so the check drags the shell's own data object (`SHCreateDataObject`, as Explorer does) with `DoDragDrop` and SendInput; a WinForms CF_HDROP-only drag was refused. All four apps open the dropped file (effect copy).
  - Proof: https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38950422644 (all five jobs green; `check_win_open: OK`): command line (relative names in `Cronkite résumé 文件`), `cmd /c start "" app.exe "…\people é文件.csv"` (Open with's command line), a registered HKCU ProgID opened by type through ShellExecute (by class for `.png`, which has a UserChoice), benches with the option after the file (`brick FILE --bench-open`, `slate FILE --bench-flow`, `frame FILE --bench-draw 10`, `etch --bench-stroke 10`, exit 0), drops into all four, first start with a cold fontconfig cache 1.2 s (later 0.26 s), bundled fonts added in 20 ms. Screenshots looked at: Slate office-fonts, stand-ins, Markdown (fontconfig vs Win32), Frame decks, Brick grid, drops. Linux: fmt, clippy, `cargo test -p suite-ui`, script tests (OK); `check_slate` SLATE OK, `check_frame` FRAME OK, `check_etch` ETCH OK, `check_drop` DROP OK, `check_malformed` MALFORMED OK, `check_recovery` RECOVERY OK, `check_xlsx` XLSX OK (before the slight-hinting commit, which changes Windows only). macOS jobs green on every run.
  - For later: Brick's default window is wider than the runner's 1024 px screen. `scripts/check_mac_look.sh` now writes office-fonts.docx through `scripts/office_fonts.py`.
- **2026-10-16 WIN-2:** Per-user folders and process checks on Windows. Every app's files go through `suite_platform::dirs`; Linux and macOS paths are unchanged.
  - Folders: `dirs` gains `recent_dir` (a `Folder::Recent`): the state folder on Linux (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/<command>`, XDG's place for recently used files) and macOS (`~/Library/Application Support/<app id>`), and on Windows `%APPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>`, beside the settings, as ADR 0011 says (settings and recent files roam with the profile; journals, which name this machine's processes, and caches stay in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>`). Brick's `state::recent()` (after the Linux folder adoption has run) and Etch's `recent::store()` use it. Slate's and Etch's settings already went through `dirs::config_dir` (MAC-2), so on Windows they're `%APPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>\settings` with no change; nothing in the apps uses `glib::user_config_dir` any more. Audit: every per-user path is `dirs`' (Brick's scratch copies and Frame's and Slate's package copies `cache_dir`, journals `state_dir`, settings `config_dir`, recent lists `recent_dir`, Slate's embedded fonts `runtime_dir` else `cache_dir`, so `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\Slate\fonts` on Windows); the Save dialogs' starting folders are GLib's `user_special_dir` (Documents, Pictures), which are Windows' known folders there. GTK's own files (its recently-used list, file chooser settings) are GTK's business.
  - Recent list labels: `recent::label` shortens the home folder to `~` on Unix only; on Windows the folder shows in full (`C:\Users\me\Documents`), as Windows' own recent lists do, ignoring the `HOME` an MSYS2 or Git Bash shell sets. Decision taken (no `%USERPROFILE%`-relative form, which Windows doesn't show either). Tests: `labels_show_the_whole_folder_on_windows` (drive root `D:\`, UNC `\\server\share\`), `the_home_folder_is_shortened_when_given`; Etch's `items_show_the_name_and_folder_keeping_underscores` has a Windows twin.
  - Owner locks work on Windows unchanged: `File::lock` is `LockFileEx` there, which another process's `try_lock` sees (`ERROR_LOCK_VIOLATION` → `WouldBlock`) and which Windows drops when a killed process ends (soon after, not always at once: the tests poll up to 10 s). Fixed for Windows, which hands out process ids again soon: `suite_file::recovery::Store` remembers the journals this process named, so a journal named for this process's id that it didn't name (an earlier crashed process with the same id) is offered back instead of being taken for its own, and `new_journal` skips names a file already has, so it can't overwrite that journal. Slate's package copies with a reused id are only garbage and go at a later sweep.
  - Tests, each with a real second process (the test binary run again as a child, killed with `Child::kill`, `TerminateProcess` on Windows): `suite_file`'s `a_killed_processs_journal_is_offered` (a running process's journal is left alone, a killed one's offered, its lock file going with it) and `an_earlier_process_with_this_id_left_its_journals`; per app, `a_killed_bricks_journal_is_offered_back`, `a_killed_slates_journal_is_offered_back`, `a_killed_etchs_journal_is_offered_back`: the child, its `HOME`, `%APPDATA%`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, and XDG variables pointed into a temporary folder, opens a file (Brick and Etch: adds it to the recent list), edits it, and writes its journal through the app's own `recovery::store()`; the test checks each file's folder for this system (Windows `Local\Cronkite\<App>\recovery`, `Roaming\Cronkite\<App>\recent` and `settings`, Slate's package copy in `Local\Cronkite\Slate`; macOS's and Linux's as before), that the journal isn't offered while the child runs, then kills it and gets the journal offered and its edits back onto the file (Brick: the restore plan saves `1,pear`; Slate: `Recovered::read` gives the blocks; Etch: the pixels). Slate's `a_sweep_keeps_the_copies_of_running_slates` (the test is the second Slate, a child process the first, whose copy survives the sweep and goes once it's killed) already passed on Windows in WIN-1; it now waits for the killed child's lock to drop. Also `a_mapped_scratch_copy_is_removed_and_keeps_its_bytes` (csv-engine: removing a mapped scratch copy works on Windows, so none stays in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\Brick`), `package_copies_are_in_frames_cache_folder` (pptx), `the_list_lives_in_etchs_recent_files_folder`, and `dirs`' tests with the recent folder in every system's mapping and in `this_platforms_folders`' child process.
  - Specs: Brick's SPEC, `docs/specs/slate.md`, and `docs/specs/etch.md` say where Windows keeps each file and that recovery works there; CLAUDE.md's suite-platform row names which folder holds what.
  - For later WIN stories: WIN-5's uninstaller decides what happens to `%APPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>` (the user's settings, recent files, and unsaved journals). On Windows state and caches share `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>`, so Slate's sweep reads a folder that also holds `recovery\` and `fonts\`; it touches only `original-*` files and their owners' lock files.
  - Proof: https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38945109192 (commit db663d6), all five jobs green: `windows` (clippy 17 s, tests 2 min 31 s; 171 test targets, 834 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored; every test named above `ok`), `check`, `macos`, `macos-look`, `bench`; again on the story's last code commit, merged with main: https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38951016616 (commit d391262), all five green, `windows` 171 targets, 834 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored. - **2026-10-16 WIN-1:** `ci.yml` has a `windows` job: `windows-latest`, `msys2/setup-msys2` UCRT64 (`update: true`, its package cache on) with MSYS2's `gtk4`, `pkgconf`, `gcc`, and `rust` (rustc 1.99.0 "Built by MSYS2 project", host `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`, clippy included), then clippy `-D warnings` on all targets and features and `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` (every failing target reports, not just the first). `actions/cache` keeps `D:\cargo-home\registry` and `target`, keyed by the toolchain and Cargo.lock, a run restoring the newest and saving its own. No `core.autocrlf` setting: the new root `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`; `**/tests/data/**` and `apps/*/crates/*/data/**` `-text`) checks out every file's committed bytes, so the 2 CRLF failures (data-model's `a_v1_journal_still_restores` and `a_v2_journal_still_restores`, whose CSV fixtures came out CRLF) pass on a default Windows checkout.
  - The spike's failures, each fixed or gated with a Windows test of its own: **41 save failures** (file-format 7, xlsx 7, docx 19, pptx 8): `save_atomic` syncs the folder only on Unix (`#[cfg(unix)]`); Windows can't open a folder as a file, and NTFS journals the rename. `std::fs::rename` there is `SetFileInformationByHandle` with POSIX semantics, so it replaces a file that's open or mapped. New tests: `replaces_a_file_that_is_open` (every OS: the open handle still reads the old bytes) and `read_only_file_on_windows_stays_and_no_temp`; the permission-bits half of `replaces_contents_and_keeps_permissions` is Unix-only. **4 recent-list failures:** the list keeps lines that are `Path::is_absolute` (was: start with `/`) and stores paths through `suite_file::canonical`, which strips `\\?\` (and `\\?\UNC\`) when the plain path means the same file (under 260 UTF-16 units; no CON/PRN/AUX/NUL/COM1-9/LPT1-9 part; no trailing dot or space), so lists and menus show `C:\…`. Tests: `files_are_listed_by_their_canonical_names`, `canonical_names_a_file_one_way` (every OS), `verbatim_prefixes_go_only_when_the_path_means_the_same_without` (Windows). **2 mapped-file truncation failures:** the SIGBUS guard and its two tests (`source::tests::truncation_while_mapped_*`, `index::tests::row_spans_report_a_file_changed_underneath`) are `cfg(unix)`, `libc` is a Unix-only dependency of csv-engine, and `Source::changed` is always false on Windows. Windows test `source::tests::another_program_cannot_truncate_a_mapped_file`: `set_len(0)` and `File::create` over Brick's mapped CSV fail with `ERROR_USER_MAPPED_FILE` (1224), the mapping keeps every byte and indexes 200,000 rows, a same-length write in place goes through and shows in the mapping, and a rename over it (a save) replaces the file while the mapping reads the old bytes. **7 Unix-only targets:** suite-file's `atomic` and image-io's `save` (Unix: read-only folder; Windows: read-only file, `a_save_that_fails_leaves_the_old_file`, error text matched on "denied"), the etch bin's journal test (`odd_path`: raw non-UTF-8 bytes on Unix, a Unicode name on Windows), and the four `evict` targets (MAC-1 had already gated them to Linux). The timing test `kill_9_mid_save_leaves_the_original_intact` (R10) reads the temp file's size from the file, not the folder listing, and its child pauses 1 ms per 256 KiB (was per MiB): green on Windows.
  - Paths in the suite's own files: `suite_file::{path_bytes, path_from_bytes}`: a path's own bytes on Unix (unchanged on disk); on Windows UTF-8, `None` for a name that isn't Unicode (GLib's file names are UTF-8 there, so no path from GTK is refused). Used by the recent list, Brick's journal header (data-model now depends on suite-file; a name that can't be kept fails the journal write, one that can't be read is `Corrupt`), Etch's journal header, and Slate's fontconfig call. Slate's private font folder gets mode 0700 on Unix only.
  - `suite_file::canonical(path)` replaces the apps' own `canonical` helpers (Brick's `main.rs`, Slate's `main.rs`, Frame's and Etch's `window.rs`, Etch's `recent::remove`), so a window lookup, a journal's path, and the recent list name a file the same way on Windows.
  - `suite_platform::Platform` gains `Windows`; `current()` accepts it (Slate, Frame, and Etch start), and only other systems are `Unsupported` ("only Linux, macOS, and Windows are supported"). Tests: `linux_macos_and_windows_are_supported`, `this_build_is_supported`.
  - Folders: `dirs` must answer on Windows, so it implements ADR 0011's folders now: `config_dir` is `%APPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>`, `state_dir` and `cache_dir` both `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Cronkite\<App>` (`<App>` = `AppKind::display_name`), each variable counting when absolute, else `AppData\Roaming` / `AppData\Local` under `%USERPROFILE%` (then `std::env::home_dir`, then the temp folder); no runtime folder. Tests: `windows_uses_the_application_data_folders_by_app_name`, `windows_without_the_variables_uses_the_profile_folder`, and `this_platforms_folders` (a child process with `APPDATA`/`LOCALAPPDATA` set) green on Windows. Left for WIN-2: the recent lists still live in `state_dir`, so on Windows they're in `%LOCALAPPDATA%` until WIN-2 moves recent files to `%APPDATA%` as the ADR says (Etch's `the_list_lives_in_etchs_state_folder` expects `Cronkite/Etch/recent` on Windows meanwhile); Slate's settings still use `glib::user_config_dir`; Slate's package-copy sweep and each app's crash recovery on Windows are untested; `recent::label` still shortens with `$HOME` and `~/`.
  - Gated as Unix tests (their paths are Unix paths, which Windows' `Path` doesn't take as absolute): `dirs`' Linux and macOS mapping tests and `bundle`'s two macOS bundle-path tests (`variables` and `loaders_list` compile for tests on Unix only). `suite_ui::launch`'s `files_reach_open_and_none_activates` checks `activate` everywhere and the files only off Windows, where GLib ignores the arguments `g_application_run` gets and reads the process's command line (`g_win32_get_command_line`), so `launch::run` can't hand GApplication files: WIN-4 (opening from the command line and Explorer) owns that, and there GApplication will also see the apps' own options (`--version`, `--bench-*`). (Split into two tests, the two `g_application_run`s ran at once and failed Linux CI in run 38936534083: it stays one test.)

The first green Windows run, cold: https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38936534083 (setup-msys2 84 s, clippy 60 s, tests 3 min 44 s). Earlier runs on the branch: 38933915944 (Etch's state-folder test), 38935385453 (suite-platform's Unix-path tests and suite-ui's command-line test; every other target already green). Linux: `cargo fmt --all`, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (171 targets, 828 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored), `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts` (34 OK, 1 skipped); clippy for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` in the spike's Fedora MinGW image (`cronkite-xwin`) clean. GUI checks on the release builds, each ending OK: `check_recovery`, `check_xlsx`, `check_drop`, `check_malformed`, `check_format`, `check_formula_bar`, `check_formulas`, `check_look`, `check_reach`, `check_sheets`, `check_structure`, `check_styles`, `check_slate`, `check_frame`, `check_etch` (the install and release checks need release tarballs and weren't run: nothing they cover changed).
- **2026-10-16 PKG-6:** Every release carries `THIRD-PARTY-RUST.txt`, a notice of the Rust crates compiled into the app for that platform: per crate its version, its declared SPDX licence, the licence it's used under here, its source (crates.io page, project repository), and its licence files, whose texts follow the list, each identical text once with the crates that carry it.
  - Generator: `python3 scripts/rust_licenses.py APP TARGET OUT` (stdlib Python, no cargo-about): the crates are `cargo tree --locked -p APP --target TARGET -e normal`, the graph `cargo build --release -p APP` compiles for that target with its features, looked up in `cargo metadata --filter-platform TARGET` for their sources. It reads only Cargo.lock and the crates' sources, so it runs on the host for the Linux tarball and on the runner for the bundle, and gives the same file on both (Brick's macOS notice, 215938 bytes, matched byte for byte). cargo-about wasn't needed: every crate in the four graphs ships its licence files, so no SPDX template text is filled in, and a crate that someday ships none fails the build instead of getting a generic text.
  - What counts: normal dependencies, proc-macros included (their expansions are compiled into the binary: glib-macros, gtk4-macros, futures-macro, serde_derive where used), with their own dependencies (syn, quote, proc-macro2, proc-macro-crate, toml_edit). Build dependencies (system-deps, pkg-config, cc and their trees) and dev-dependencies are left out: they run at build time and leave no code in the binary. The suite's own crates are listed too, under the repository's LICENSE and LICENSE-APACHE.
  - Licence files: the crate root's `LICEN[CS]E*`, `COPYING*`, `COPYRIGHT*`, `NOTICE*`, `UNLICENSE`, `LICENSES/*`, and its `license-file`. All of them go in (Apache-2.0's NOTICE rule; COPYRIGHT files carry the authors).
  - The gate, in `ALLOWED` (also the preference order for a choice): MIT, Apache-2.0 (also WITH LLVM-exception), BSD-3-Clause, BSD-2-Clause, ISC, Zlib, 0BSD, MIT-0, Unicode-3.0, Unicode-DFS-2016, BSL-1.0, CC0-1.0, Unlicense, MPL-2.0 (file-level copyleft; the notice names each crate's source). No GPL, AGPL, or LGPL: an LGPL crate is statically linked, so it fails until someone decides. The build fails when a crate declares no SPDX licence, has no licence file, can't be used under the allowlist, or ships no text of the licence it's used under: each licence is recognised in the files by its wording (`TEXT`), so a COPYRIGHT file that only names "MIT" doesn't count. Of an OR it takes the first allowlisted alternative whose texts are all there. Shown on the real graph: dropping Zlib from the list fails Brick on `zlib-rs 0.6.8: Zlib is outside the allowlist`; breaking MIT's wording fails Frame on cairo-rs and the other gtk-rs crates (`no text of MIT (COPYRIGHT, LICENSE)`). `scripts/test_rust_licenses.py` (9 tests) covers expressions, wording, the choice, the failures, file discovery, and the notice.
  - Crates (Linux x86_64 / macOS arm64; third-party + the suite's own): Brick 110 / 109 (97 + 13 on Linux), Slate 93 / 92 (84 + 9), Frame 80 / 80 (72 + 8), Etch 85 / 85 (78 + 7). `core_detect` is Linux-only (Brick and Slate). Used under: Brick MIT 106, Apache-2.0 1, MIT AND BSD-3-Clause 1, MIT AND Unicode-3.0 1, Zlib 1; Slate MIT 89 and the same four; Frame MIT 77, Apache-2.0 1, MIT AND Unicode-3.0 1, Zlib 1; Etch MIT 82, Apache-2.0 2, MIT AND Unicode-3.0 1 (macOS one MIT fewer for Brick and Slate). Notices are 150–217 KB.
  - Special cases: `core_detect` writes `MIT/Apache-2.0` (crates.io's old syntax, read as OR); `encoding_rs` is `(Apache-2.0 OR MIT) AND BSD-3-Clause` (the WHATWG text in LICENSE-WHATWG) and `unicode-ident` `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND Unicode-3.0`, both used under MIT and the other; `moxcms` and `pxfm` (Etch) are `BSD-3-Clause OR Apache-2.0`, used under Apache-2.0 by the order; `zopfli` is Apache-2.0 alone with its text in COPYING; `zlib-rs` is Zlib. No crate is GPL, AGPL, or LGPL.
  - Linux: `scripts/ship.sh APP build` writes it into the tarball beside the binary and the LICENSE files, and fails the build on the gate. Each installer puts LICENSE, LICENSE-APACHE, and THIRD-PARTY-RUST.txt in `~/.local/share/doc/APP/` (replaced on update; `--uninstall` removes the folder). `check_install*.sh` assert the three files are there, the notice is at least 10 KB and lists gtk4, and the folder is gone after uninstall.
  - macOS: `scripts/mac_bundle.sh` writes `Contents/Resources/licenses/THIRD-PARTY-RUST.txt` after `mac_licenses.py` (which writes that folder afresh) and before signing; THIRD-PARTY.txt points to it. `check_mac_install.sh` asserts the installed app's notice is for arm64 and lists gtk4; `release-macos.yml` also runs when `scripts/rust_licenses.py` changes.
  - Not done: the About dialogs don't link the notice (optional in the story).
  - Proof: release-macos.yml on the branch, https://github.com/avdrav1/cronkite/actions/runs/38899027986 (bundle and verify green; `ok   brick: THIRD-PARTY-RUST.txt: 109 crates in 215938 bytes, among them gtk4 0.11.5`, the same for Slate 92, Frame 80, and Etch 85; `check_mac_install: OK`). Linux, `scripts/ship.sh APP build` then `verify` for all four apps: RELEASE OK and INSTALL OK on Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora each ("installs its licences, uninstalls"). Tarballs: Brick 2113563 bytes, Slate 1758171, Frame 1123395, Etch 880965. `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts`: 34 tests OK.
- **2026-10-16 MAC-7:** Each app ships for macOS as a self-contained, ad-hoc-signed `<App>.app` (Apple Silicon, macOS 14 or later): a DMG for the browser and a tarball for the curl installer, which installs it into `~/Applications` and registers it with Launch Services. `latest.json` lists each platform and every download page offers the visitor's first.
  - Bundle (`scripts/mac_bundle.sh APP [BIN_DIR [OUT]]`, arm64 Mac with Homebrew's `gtk4 adwaita-icon-theme librsvg dylibbundler meson ninja`):
    - skeleton and Info.plist from `scripts/mac_app.py`, which gains `CFBundleIconFile`, `LSApplicationCategoryType`, and `UTImportedTypeDeclarations` for the types macOS 14 may not declare (Markdown, `.xlsm`, `.pptm`), and copies the fonts only for Brick, Slate, and Frame (MAC-6: Etch uses the Mac's);
    - the binary `strip -x`ed; dylibbundler copies the 42 Homebrew dylibs it and the loaders need into `Contents/Frameworks`, linked by `@executable_path/../Frameworks`;
    - in `Contents/Resources`: GTK's GSettings schemas compiled; Adwaita's symbolic icons (copied without links: some point outside the copy, and codesign then fails with "No such file or directory") and hicolor with the app's icon as PNGs, so About shows it without SVG decoding; gdk-pixbuf's 12 loaders (SVG through librsvg) with a `loaders.cache` whose paths are relative to `Resources`; Homebrew's `fonts.conf` without Homebrew's paths, its cache first in `~/Library/Caches/<app id>/fontconfig`, and `conf.d` (the metric aliases); the `.icns` (rsvg-convert, iconutil); the licence files;
    - it fails when any Mach-O names a library outside the bundle and the system, or a link is broken; signs each dylib and loader ad hoc, then the app (`codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes);
    - DMG: `hdiutil` ULMO (LZMA; macOS 10.15+ reads it) with the app and an `/Applications` link. Tarball: the `.app` alone (`COPYFILE_DISABLE`). Both with `.sha256`.
    - Third-party licence notices (`scripts/mac_licenses.py`, before signing): it maps every dylib in `Frameworks` (by its copy under `$(brew --prefix)/opt/*/lib`, resolved into the Cellar; cairo's are ours, below) and every loader (by its source path) to its Homebrew formula and version. It adds the formulas whose data the bundle carries: gtk4's schemas, adwaita-icon-theme, and fontconfig's `conf.d`. Each formula's licence files go to `Contents/Resources/licenses/<formula>/`. They come from its keg (root, `share/doc`, `share/licenses`); GLib's keg has none, so its come from the source tarball, top level and REUSE `LICENSES/` (`brew fetch --build-from-source`). `licenses/THIRD-PARTY.txt` gives per formula the version, SPDX licence (`brew info --json=v2`), upstream source tarball (the release Homebrew built: LGPL's source offer), the recipe, the licence files, and what the app carries of it; then the fonts' OFL texts. It says the libraries are dynamically linked and replaceable (sign the app again afterwards). The script fails when a library maps to no formula or a formula has no licence text (`scripts/test_mac_licenses.py`, 5 tests). Not linked from About (optional; not done).
    - No GPL code (user decision, 2026-10-16). Homebrew's cairo links its script interpreter (`libcairo-script-interpreter`, which GTK loads) to `liblzo2`, GPL-2.0-or-later. `scripts/mac_cairo.sh PREFIX` builds the same release, cairo 1.18.4 (pinned with its sha256; it fails if Homebrew's cairo is another version), with the formula's meson options plus `-Dlzo=disabled -Dtests=disabled`, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0`, and `-headerpad_max_install_names`. It takes 20 s on macos-14, and `release-macos.yml` caches `target/mac-cairo`. lzo only decompresses compressed cairo scripts, so nothing the apps do changes. After dylibbundler, `mac_bundle.sh` puts our `libcairo.2`, `libcairo-gobject.2`, and `libcairo-script-interpreter.2` in place of Homebrew's under the same names (same source and options, so the same ABI; GTK, Pango, and librsvg are unchanged). It relinks their dependencies to the copies in `Frameworks`, found under dylibbundler's real-file names (`libxcb.1.1.0.dylib`). Then it keeps in `Frameworks` only what the app and the loaders reach, which drops `liblzo2.2.dylib` (41 dylibs, was 42). It fails if anything still names lzo.
    - hicolor's `index.theme` (hicolor-icon-theme, GPL-2.0-only) is no longer copied: `mac_bundle.sh` writes a minimal one for the app icon's size folders.
    - `mac_licenses.py` fails when a formula's SPDX licence can't be met without the GPL (`without_gpl`: GPL or AGPL fails a term, LGPL passes; AND needs all, OR one; tested). Three formulas name the GPL only for programs the bundle leaves out, so they count by their library's licence, each with a note in THIRD-PARTY.txt saying so, and only while that library is all the bundle carries of them: fribidi (`libfribidi`, LGPL-2.1-or-later; the GPL is the `fribidi` program's getopt), gettext (`libintl`, LGPL-2.1-or-later), and xz (`liblzma`, 0BSD; the GPL is the xzgrep and xzdiff scripts'). cairo's entry names our build: the source tarball and its sha256, the meson options, and `scripts/mac_cairo.sh` as its recipe.
  - At launch, `suite_platform::bundle::init(APP)`, first in each app's `main` (before `renderer` and `fonts`, which loads fontconfig): only for `<Name>.app/Contents/MacOS/<app>` beside `Contents/Frameworks`, so MAC-5's minimal bundles and `target/` builds are untouched, and never on Linux. It sets `XDG_DATA_DIRS` (prepended), `GSETTINGS_SCHEMA_DIR`, `GTK_DATA_PREFIX`, `GTK_EXE_PREFIX`, `GIO_MODULE_DIR` (an empty folder, so a Mac with Homebrew's GLib never loads its modules into the bundle's), `FONTCONFIG_FILE`, `FONTCONFIG_PATH`, `GDK_PIXBUF_MODULE_FILE`, and `GDK_PIXBUF_MODULEDIR`. gdk-pixbuf wants absolute module paths and the bundle can be anywhere, so the loader list is written with this bundle's paths to `~/Library/Caches/<app id>/gdk-pixbuf-loaders.cache` when it differs (temp file, rename). Tests: `suite_platform::bundle::tests` (3, on both systems).
  - Sizes (actions/runs/38842348548): `.app` Brick 46 MB, Slate 46 MB, Frame 45 MB, Etch 37 MB (31 MB of it the 42 dylibs). DMG Brick 14.4 MB, Slate 14.2 MB, Frame 13.8 MB, Etch 10.4 MB; tarball 17.4, 17.1, 16.5, and 12.6 MB. UDZO DMGs were 20.3, 20.0, 19.4, and 14.9 MB (run 38836872439), hence ULMO. Minimum macOS (`otool -l`): 168 bundled libraries 14.0, the four binaries 11.0.
  - Release: `.github/workflows/release-macos.yml` (manual with an `apps` input, release tags, and pushes that change the bundling). Job `bundle` (macos-14) runs `scripts/ship.sh APP build --macos` per app and uploads `macos-<app>` (DMG, tarball, checksums); job `verify`, a fresh macos-14 with `/opt/homebrew` moved away, runs `scripts/check_mac_install.sh`. `ship.sh` gains:
    - `build --macos`: on a Mac, cargo and `mac_bundle.sh`; elsewhere it dispatches the workflow at HEAD (committed and pushed, or it says so), waits for it, and fetches. Proven from Linux: `scripts/ship.sh etch build --macos` dispatched actions/runs/38843180753 (both jobs green) and put Etch's DMG and tarball in `dist/` with `latest.json` naming both platforms;
    - `fetch-macos RUN_ID`: only a green run built at HEAD; checks the files against their sha256;
    - `upload` puts the macOS tarball, DMG, and checksums beside the Linux tarball (`releases/vX.Y.Z/<app>-X.Y.Z-arm64-macos.{tar.gz,dmg}`), then `latest.json`; `--dry-run` prints them. Without macOS files it says "Linux only" and ships Linux.
    - The version is read with a sed BSD sed takes too (`p;}`).
  - `latest.json` is one line: `platforms` first (`linux-x86_64`: url, sha256, bytes; `macos-arm64`: url, sha256, and bytes of the tarball, and dmg, dmg_sha256, and dmg_bytes; each a flat object), then Linux's version, date, url, sha256, and bytes at the top level, last. Installers from before MAC-7 read a key with a sed that takes its last match on the line, so they keep reading Linux's (checked with their `field()` on the new file). The new installers cut out the `macos-arm64` object and read it the same way.
  - Installers (all four `sites/<app>/install.sh`), on Darwin: refuse Intel Macs (`uname -m`, and `hw.optional.arm64` for a shell under Rosetta) and macOS before 14; check the macOS tarball's sha256; replace `~/Applications/<App>.app`; `lsregister -f`; check its `--version` runs. `--uninstall` runs `lsregister -u` and removes the app, keeping `~/Library/Application Support/<app id>` as Linux keeps its state. `<APP>_DOWNLOAD_URL` still overrides. Linux is unchanged.
  - Default apps (decision): on macOS the installer doesn't change them, and says how (Finder, Get Info, Open with, Change All). Linux's installers make each app the default, but macOS has no supported command-line way: it needs `duti` (not on Macs) or a compiled LaunchServices call. Silently taking over CSV, `.docx`, `.pptx`, or PNG from Numbers, Pages, Keynote, Preview, or Office would surprise Mac users. `LSHandlerRank` stays Alternate (MAC-5). On a Mac without another app for a type, Finder should open it with ours [INFERENCE: Launch Services falls back to Alternate handlers; not run]; otherwise it's under Open With (checked: `urlsForApplications(toOpen:)` lists it).
  - Pages: every download page's button offers the visitor's platform (`navigator.userAgentData.platform`, else `navigator.platform`, else the user agent), and a list shows both downloads with size and sha256. The pages explain the macOS install and the DMG's Gatekeeper steps: Open Anyway in System Settings → Privacy & Security on macOS 15+, Control-click → Open on macOS 14, or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/<App>.app`. A `latest.json` from before MAC-7 still shows its Linux download, and the list says "Mac: not in this release yet". Checked in headless Chromium with a Mac and a Linux platform, against both kinds of release. The suite page and `llms.txt` say Linux and macOS: requirements, what the installer does on each, the DMG's Gatekeeper steps, and the `platforms` format. Specs (Brick, Slate, Frame, Etch) and ADR 0011 item 6 are amended.
  - Proof, macOS: actions/runs/38842348548 green. `bundle` built all four. `verify` ended `check_mac_install: OK` with 73 ok lines, on macOS 14.8.9 arm64 with no `/opt/homebrew`. Per app:
    - a tampered tarball is refused and nothing installed;
    - `curl …/install.sh | sh` installs; no quarantine attribute (curl sets none, so Gatekeeper never asks); the ad-hoc signature is valid; the app is in Launch Services' database, and `NSWorkspace.urlsForApplications(toOpen:)` lists it for its sample file;
    - started on the file it shows a window, with `DYLD_PRINT_LIBRARIES` 41 libraries from its bundle and none from `/opt/homebrew`; it logs "running from the bundle", Brick, Slate, and Frame add the bundled fonts, and the loader list and fontconfig's cache land in `~/Library/Caches/<app id>`;
    - `~/Library/Application Support/<app id>` is created (Brick's `recent`, Slate's folder, Etch's `recent`; Frame keeps no settings or state, so its `~/Library/Caches/dev.cronkite.Frame` instead);
    - `open -a` with a second file opens it; `--uninstall` leaves neither app nor registration;
    - the DMG mounts with `<App>.app` and an Applications link, and the copy in `/Applications` opens the file.
    - Gatekeeper is on there (`spctl --status`: assessments enabled) and `spctl --assess` rejects every ad-hoc app, as expected. Etch copied from its DMG with a Safari quarantine flag isn't started: macOS 14 shows "“Etch” cannot be opened because it is from an unidentified developer" with only OK. - **2026-10-16 MAC-5:** Every file reaches each app through GApplication's `open` (`HANDLES_OPEN`), from the command line or the system; `activate` means no file. `suite_ui::launch::run` hands the command line's files to GApplication as absolute paths (`run_with_args_os`, so non-UTF-8 and `-`-names survive); `suite_ui::launch::no_file` makes the no-file window (Brick's and Slate's start windows, Frame's new deck, Etch's blank image or crash reopen), held back on macOS until `suite_platform::launch::when_finished` (CoreFoundation observer of `NSApplicationDidFinishLaunchingNotification`; at once on Linux). Brick's command-line file is still mapped before GTK starts and consumed by the first `open`; benchmarks keep working (Brick, Slate, Frame through `open`, Etch's on `activate`). `suite-platform` gains a private `cf` module shared with `appearance`.
  - What happens on macOS (probe runs actions/runs/38825590170, /38826328946, logs with timestamps): Launch Services starts the app without arguments; GTK emits `activate` before its main loop, then handles the Apple Event (`gtkapplication-quartz.c` `application:openFiles:` → `g_application_open` in-process, only with `HANDLES_OPEN`), then Cocoa posts DidFinishLaunching (≈50 ms after `activate`). Without the hold-back, Frame and Etch showed an Untitled window beside the file (first probe run: 2 windows). A second `open -a` goes to the same process (GTK delivers it inside it) and opens a second window. `open -a App` with no file, and a terminal launch, post DidFinishLaunching a few ms after `activate`. Drop-on-Dock sends the same Apple Event [INFERENCE, not run].
  - Uniqueness (decision): all four stay `NON_UNIQUE`. macOS needs no D-Bus uniqueness: Launch Services keeps one process per bundle and GTK hands its opens to the local instance. Linux is unchanged: each `app FILE` (desktop `Exec=… %f`) is its own process, as each app's earlier choice said.
  - Renderer (decision): cairo on macOS unless `GSK_RENDERER` is set (`suite_ui::renderer::init`, before GTK and threads); Linux keeps GTK's choice.
