- **2026-10-15 MAC-4:** The menu bar, app menu, and native title bars on macOS. One module, `suite_ui::chrome`: `MENU_BAR` (true on macOS); `menu_bar(&[(label, menu)])`, the app's menus as submenus and on macOS a Window menu after them (Minimize ⌘M, Zoom, and the windows, which AppKit lists: the submenu carries `gtk-macos-special=window-submenu`); `install(app, kind, version)` at startup, adding `app.about` (the About dialog), `app.minimize` (⌘M on macOS) and `app.zoom`; `about()`, a GtkAboutDialog with the name, version, a line on what the app is, the `MIT OR Apache-2.0` licence, and `https://<app>.cronkite.cc`, with the icon theme's icon, or else the packaged SVG decoded (macOS); `Toolbar`, a HeaderBar on Linux and an ActionBar row on macOS (`pack_start`, `pack_end`, `put_on`: a header bar becomes the title bar, a row is returned to go over the content). Each app builds its menus once, and Linux's header menu (Slate) and in-window menu bar (Etch) are the same `gio::Menu`s.
  - GTK facts (4.22.4 source: `gtkapplication-quartz.c`, `gtkapplication-quartz.ui`, `gtkapplication-quartz-menu.c`): the macOS backend always makes the app menu itself: About %s runs `app.about`, Preferences runs `app.preferences` (macOS shows it as Settings…, greyed, as no app has settings yet), then Services, Hide ⌘H, Hide Others ⌥⌘H, Show All, and Quit %s, which runs `app.quit` with the `<Meta>q` GTK sets. `set_menubar`'s menus follow it. GTK 4 has no API to replace that menu. An item shows its `accel` attribute, else its action's accelerator. Without `set_menubar` GTK adds Edit and Window menus of its own. A window without `set_titlebar` gets the NSWindow title bar (traffic lights, the window's title). A key no GTK handler takes goes on to AppKit, so a menu item's key equivalent fires then. For example, Frame's ⌘D while text is edited probably duplicates the slide on macOS, where Linux does nothing [INFERENCE, not exercised].
  - Brick (`apps/brick/src/menus.rs`): File (New, Open, Open Recent, Close Window ⌘W, Save, Save As), Edit (Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste, Find, Replace, Go To), Sheet (New, Next, and Previous Sheet; insert and delete rows and columns), Format (bold, italic, underline, strikethrough; Number Format ▸ General, Number, Currency, Date, Percent, Custom; Clear Formatting; merging), Data (sorting). The window's key handler's commands moved into one `run(Command)`, which the key handler and the menu's `win.*` actions both call. Rows, columns, sorting, and merging go to the grid's own `grid.*` actions. Cut, Copy, and Paste go to the focused text's `clipboard.*` (a cell editor, the formula bar, the find bar), else to the grid's selection. Open Recent is `menus::fill_recent`, which is also Linux's header menu, refreshed when a file is remembered, when the list is cleared, and when a window becomes active. On macOS the toolbar row has the delimiter and Header row; Open, Recent, New, and Save As are File's. The start window has no header bar there. The menu's Undo and Redo are the grid's even while a cell is being edited (⌘Z in the editor stays the editor's).
  - Slate: `Menus` (a thread-local, built once): File, Edit, Format, and View are sections of Linux's header menu (`primary`, with Quit) and menus of their own in macOS's menu bar. On macOS there's no header bar and no toolbar row: Open is ⌘O, File → Open, and the start window's button. The title bar shows the name and the "• ".
  - Frame (`apps/frame/src/menus.rs`): File (New, Open, Close Window ⌘W, Save, Save As), Edit (Undo, Redo), Insert (Text, Shape ▸ every preset, Picture…, Table: new `win.insert-*` actions), Slide (New, Duplicate, Delete, Move Up, Move Down), View (Outline, Slideshow, Slideshow from This Slide). On macOS the toolbar row holds New Slide with its layouts, Insert, Outline, and the slideshow button, as Keynote's does. New, Open, and Save are in File.

- **2026-10-15 MAC-6:** Fonts and dark mode on macOS. `suite_ui::fonts::init()` runs first in every app's `main`; `suite_platform::appearance` (macOS only, CoreFoundation) reads and follows the Mac's appearance, and `suite_ui::appearance::set_dark` (moved out of Brick's `theme.rs`) tells GTK. Linux is unchanged: `init` does nothing there, and Brick's portal reader keeps calling the same GTK switch.
  - Decision: Pango on fontconfig, not CoreText, on macOS (`PANGOCAIRO_BACKEND=fc` unless the environment already names a backend). Evidence from the runner (macos-look job, `PANGOCAIRO_BACKEND=coretext` shots beside the default ones):
    - CoreText still clips the tops of Brick's grid text (`0` reads as `U`, `7` as `/`, the dots of `i` gone); fontconfig draws them whole.
    - CoreText's font map can't see fonts added to fontconfig, so Slate's Calibri and Cambria fell back to a Helvetica-like face. Under fontconfig they draw in Carlito and Caladea.
    - Fontconfig gives one layout engine on both systems, and Slate's embedded `.docx` fonts work unchanged.
    - Costs: glyphs rasterise through FreeType, not CoreGraphics. Fontconfig builds its cache on first start. Fonts that Office for Mac registers privately are invisible to fontconfig, but Carlito and Caladea stand in with the same metrics.
  - Fonts: Liberation 2.1.5, Carlito, and Caladea (7.2 MB with their licences: SIL OFL 1.1 `LICENSE-Liberation.txt`, `OFL-Carlito.txt`, `OFL-Caladea.txt`).
    - They aren't vendored in git. `scripts/fetch_fonts.py [DEST]` fetches them from pinned upstream sources (the Liberation release tarball, and google/fonts at the commits that last changed `ofl/carlito` and `ofl/caladea`), checks every file's SHA-256, and skips files already present (`scripts/test_fetch_fonts.py`).
    - Each app looks for the folder next to its executable: `Contents/Resources/fonts` in a bundle, else `../fonts` (`target/fonts` for `target/{debug,release}/<app>`). Every font in it becomes an application font of fontconfig's current configuration (`FcConfigAppFontAddDir`, 8 to 42 ms on the runner).
    - Fontconfig's own metric aliases then give Carlito for Calibri and Caladea for Cambria; the Mac's Arial, Times New Roman, and Courier New draw as themselves.
    - Linux: not bundled. The system's packages stay in use, as before.
  - GTK names the Mac's system font `.AppleSystemUIFont`, which fontconfig doesn't know. Unaliased, the UI drew in Verdana. SF itself (`System Font`) drew every `i` without its dot through FreeType (run 38816228836). An alias now gives Helvetica Neue.
  - Slate at 96 dpi: GTK on macOS gives widgets 72 dpi (`gtk-xft-dpi`), so Slate's text drew at three quarters of its size on a page measured in 96-dpi pixels, and lines broke elsewhere than in Word. On macOS the page lays out with a context of its own at 96 dpi (`Page::document_context`).
    - The same `office-fonts.docx` (Calibri, Cambria, Arial, Times New Roman, Courier New) now breaks every line where Slate breaks it on Linux (Arch container, `ttf-carlito`/`ttf-caladea`/`ttf-liberation`, same crop compared).
    - Brick's cells and the UI keep the Mac's points. Frame scales its slides itself, and its text matched without changes.
    - Linux keeps the widget's context. So a GNOME text-scaling factor still grows Slate's text but not its page, and line breaks then differ from Word's (existing; follow-up).
  - Dark mode: the appearance is the global `AppleInterfaceStyle` preference (`Dark` or absent; macOS keeps it current under Auto too), read at startup and again on each `AppleInterfaceThemeChangedNotification` (distributed centre, delivered immediately).
    - First runs: the notification arrived on the main thread with the right value, and the setting changed, but windows stayed light for good. Re-setting the theme name or the old prefer-dark switch didn't help, and an idle source never ran at all.
    - Cause: macOS delivers the notification from the run loop GDK waits in, which is inside GLib's poll. GLib wakes a polling context only for sources added from other threads, so GTK's restyle sat queued until the next input event. `set_dark` now wakes the main context after setting it. With that, Slate turned dark and light and Brick turned dark, each within 3 s.
  - CI: `ci.yml` gains a job `macos-look` (`scripts/check_mac_look.sh`: debug builds, fetched fonts, `GSK_RENDERER=cairo`, appearance switched through System Events, brightness of each window's header bar, dark < 100 and light > 150). The `macos` job fetches the fonts before testing.
    - `suite-ui`'s macOS test: Calibri→Carlito, Cambria→Caladea, the Liberation faces, and `.AppleSystemUIFont`→Helvetica Neue after registering `target/fonts`.
    - `suite-platform`'s macOS test: `is_dark` agrees with `defaults read -g AppleInterfaceStyle`.
  - Proof, macOS: actions/runs/38817067548 (all four jobs; LOOK OK). The same checks were green on run 38816228836, before the Helvetica Neue alias:

    - dark at start: Brick 42, Slate 44, Frame 43, Etch's middle 55;
    - live: Slate dark after 3 s and light again after 2 s, Brick dark after 3 s;
    - every app's log names `target/fonts` added.
  - Proof, Linux: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (171 targets, 783 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored), script tests (16 OK). On the release build: `check_slate` SLATE OK, `check_frame` FRAME OK, `check_etch` ETCH OK, `check_format` FORMAT OK.
  - For later:
    - MAC-7 copies `target/fonts` (from `fetch_fonts.py`) into `Contents/Resources/fonts` of Brick, Slate, and Frame. Etch needs none: it logs that no folder was found and uses the system's fonts.
    - A Homebrew-free bundle must also carry fontconfig's configuration with `conf.d` (`30-metric-aliases.conf`, the Mac font folders) and point `FONTCONFIG_FILE` at it, or the aliases are gone.
    - Homebrew on `macos-14` has GTK 4.22.4, and that runner's display is 1920×1080 at scale 1.
    - `Calibri Light` (Frame's blank deck title) has no metric alias, as on Linux.

- **2026-10-15 MAC-3:** One helper, `suite_ui::keys` (suite-ui now depends on gtk): shortcuts are written once with `<Primary>` (`<Primary><Shift>s`), and `Primary::{Control, Command}` turns them into GTK accelerators (`<Control>s`, `<Meta>s`), labels (`Ctrl+Shift+S`, `⇧⌘S`), and key-handler matches (`matches`, `is_primary`); `Primary::HERE` is the build's, `Primary::ALL` lets each app's tests check both on either OS; `clashes` finds a key bound twice, an unknown key, or one of `MAC_RESERVED` (⌘H, ⌥⌘H, ⌘M, ⌘,, ⌘`, ⌘Space, ⇧⌘3/4/5, ⌃⌘F, ⇧⌘Q, …). Each app has one table: `apps/{brick,slate,frame}/src/keys.rs` (new) and Etch's `keys.rs` (`Mods.ctrl` is now `primary`); every accelerator, menu `accel`, tooltip, and key handler goes through it.
  - GTK facts (4.22.4 source): the macOS backend reports ⌘ as `META_MASK` (`gdkmacosdisplay-translate.c`), and GTK 4's own `<Primary>` parses as Control on macOS too (`gtkaccelgroup.c`), so it isn't enough: the helper writes `<Meta>`, which GTK's macOS menu bar shows as ⌘. GtkText already binds ⌘A/C/V/X/Z and ⌥/⌘ arrows there, so fields keep their own keys. Option changes a letter's keyval on macOS (⌥F arrives as ƒ), so Option+letter shortcuts are accelerators (GTK matches the keycode too), not handler matches.
  - Mac conventions (decisions): Redo is ⇧⌘Z (Ctrl+Y stays Linux's alone, in every app); Replace is ⌥⌘F (Brick, Slate; ⌘H hides the app); Frame's New Slide is ⇧⌘N (PowerPoint for Mac's; ⌘M minimizes); Etch's Rotate 90° Right is ⌘] (Pixelmator's; ⌘R is Resize Image); ⌘-wheel zooms in Etch; ⌘-click opens a link in Slate. Kept on Control on macOS, as Excel for Mac has them: Brick's ⌃Space and ⇧⌃Space (⌘Space is Spotlight), ⌃⇧digit number formats (⇧⌘3/4/5 take screenshots), and ⌃Page Down/Up between sheets; each app's test asserts its exact `<Control>` list (only Brick's is non-empty).
  - Caret on macOS: Slate's page and Frame's text editing move ⌥←/→ by word, ⌘←/→ to the line's ends, ⌘↑/↓ (and ⌘Home/End) to the document's (Frame: the text's), Shift extending; ⌥⌫/⌥⌦ delete a word, ⌘⌫ back to the line's start. Frame had no word moves: `editing.rs` finds word edges itself (letters, digits, `_`; pango 0.22 doesn't expose Pango's word boundaries). Linux keeps its moves; Slate and Frame still have no Ctrl+Backspace word delete there.
  - Linux: the same accelerators, keys, and tooltip text (each app's test writes the pre-MAC-3 strings out). Window and page keys now match their modifiers exactly, so combinations the old handlers took only because they ignored extra modifiers no longer fire (Ctrl+Alt+Z, Ctrl+Super+Z, Ctrl+Shift+B as Bold, Ctrl+Shift+F in Slate's find bar, Ctrl+Shift+Page Down in Brick); Caps Lock with Ctrl+Z now undoes in Brick (it redid). Brick's Ctrl+H became the accelerator `win.replace`, same effect.
  - Fixed on the way (macOS only in effect): Slate, Frame, and Etch set their input method's client widget at construction, before the widget had a window. `GtkIMMulticontext` hands the widget to GTK's macOS input method at once, which reads the window then and never again, so every typed key was dropped (smoke run 38812919481: nothing typed, before or after a click; spike C13's "keys reached nothing until clicked" too). `suite_ui::im::attach` sets it while the widget is realized, as GtkText does; Slate's typing works on the runner now without a click. Frame's and Etch's use the same fix but aren't exercised on the runner.
  - Proof: unit tests over each table, run on Linux and macOS (`brick keys::tests` 6, `slate keys::tests` 6, `frame keys::tests` 7, `etch keys::tests` 11, `suite_ui::keys::tests` 5): clashes empty for both mappings, Linux strings unchanged, ⌘ where Linux has Ctrl but for the named exceptions, ⌘Z undoes under Command and ⌃Z doesn't. macOS smoke test `scripts/check_mac_keys.sh` in ci.yml's `macos` job (release builds, `GSK_RENDERER=cairo`, System Events keys one every 0.3 s): actions/runs/38815385754 green, `check_mac_keys: OK`: Brick ⌘S saves A2 = zz, ⌘Z takes yy out of A3; Slate ⌘S saves "Hello Original line.", X then ⌘Z then ⌘S leaves it; Frame ⇧⌘N then ⌘S adds a slide to the .pptx (5 → 6), ⌘Z takes the second back; Etch ⌘G then ⌘S saves the PNG 32x64, ⌘G ⌘Z ⌘S keeps it; ⌘O shows each app's native open panel. Screenshots (artifact `macos-keys`) looked at: the open panels, Slate's title losing its • after ⌘Z, Frame's slide list going 7 → 6, Etch's status 32×64. The same run's `cargo test --workspace` on macOS: 172 targets, 0 failed. Linux: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (810 passed, 0 failed), script tests (12) OK; SLATE OK, FRAME OK, ETCH OK (after the input-method fix too), FORMAT, FORMULA BAR, FORMULAS, MALFORMED, REACH, RECOVERY, SHEETS, STRUCTURE, XLSX, DROP, LOOK OK. `check_styles.sh` fails step 3 (its Styles!J30 screenshot scrolls the fills out of view) with main's Brick (2ebab08) too, 7 FAILs both: not this story's.
  - For later MAC stories: MAC-4's menu bar should build its menus from the same tables (`keys::accels`, Slate's `item`, Etch's `menu_item`), so the macOS menus show ⌘ keys; GTK's own app menu already has Hide ⌘H and Quit ⌘Q. Brick's grid has no Ctrl+arrow (jump to data edges) on either OS; when it does, macOS wants ⌘arrows. Tooltips show ⌘ labels on macOS; MAC-4 may move them into menus.
- **2026-10-15 MAC-2:** One folder helper, `suite_platform::dirs::{config_dir, state_dir, cache_dir, runtime_dir}(AppKind)`, toolkit-free (suite-platform now depends on suite-core; `csv-engine` and `pptx` depend on suite-platform for their cache folders). Linux: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `$XDG_STATE_HOME`, `$XDG_CACHE_HOME` + `/<command>`, else `~/.config`, `~/.local/state`, `~/.cache` + `/<command>`; `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/<command>` when that's a folder. macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/<app id>` for settings and state, `~/Library/Caches/<app id>` for caches, no runtime folder (Slate's embedded fonts go to Caches). `AppKind` gains `command()` and `app_id()`; the apps' `APP_ID` constants now come from it.
  - Decisions: the macOS folders are named by bundle identifier (`dev.cronkite.Brick`, …), as Apple's File System Programming Guide asks of an app's own folders in Application Support and Caches, and because "Slate" and "Frame" are common app names that could collide. MAC-7's bundles must use the same IDs as `CFBundleIdentifier`. Settings and state share one folder on macOS: their files (`settings`, `recent`, `recovery/`) don't clash, and Apple has no separate state folder. macOS ignores the XDG variables.
  - Routed (inventory F16-F20): Brick's `state.rs` (recent, recovery), `csv_engine::scratch_path` (Brick's cache: decoded copies, XLSX sheets and package copies), Frame's `pptx::Original`, Slate's settings, recovery, `saving::Original`, and embedded fonts, Etch's settings, recent, and recovery. No folder code is left outside `dirs` (grep for `XDG_`, `user_*_dir`, `home_dir`). Brick's PKG-5 move from `bricks`/`spreadsheet` now runs only on Linux: earlier releases were Linux-only, and on macOS sibling folders in Application Support belong to other apps.
  - Linux paths are unchanged byte for byte with the XDG variables set to absolute paths or unset (`dirs::tests`). Edge cases now agree everywhere: an empty or relative XDG variable is ignored, as the XDG spec says. Before, Brick's and Etch's state did that, but the caches and GLib's config folder took any non-empty value, so `XDG_CACHE_HOME=rel` put caches in the working directory. With `HOME` unset, the home folder comes from the password database, then the temp folder. Brick used to fall back to a relative `.local/state` there.
  - Liveness (decision: owner locks, not `kill(pid, 0)`, so a reused pid can't keep a gone process's files). Recovery's owner lock moved into `suite_file::owner` (`hold`, `alive`, `release`, `lock_path`; one lock per folder per process; a `parking_lot` Mutex, so suite-file gains `parking_lot`), and `recovery::Store` uses it. Slate's `Original` takes its lock in the cache folder before its first copy. The sweep removes copies whose owner's lock isn't held, then their lock files. Copies made by a Slate from before MAC-2 have no lock file, so the first sweep after the upgrade removes them. That includes the copies of an older Slate still running at that moment, which then can't save its `.docx` until it's reopened. Accepted as a one-time cost.
  - `F_FULLFSYNC`: Rust's `File::sync_all` is already `fcntl(F_FULLFSYNC)` on Apple targets (`library/std/src/sys/fs/unix.rs`, `os_fsync`), so `save_atomic` needed no code, only a doc note. The macOS CI job's new step "Saves use F_FULLFSYNC" traces `cargo test -p suite-file --test atomic` with `sudo fs_usage -w -f filesys` and fails without FULLFSYNC calls from the test binary. It saw 3 (`fcntl F=3 <FULLFSYNC>`, ...): the file and the folder of the successful save, and the file of the save whose verification failed. The tests pass, so APFS takes it on a folder too.
  - Proof, macOS: actions/runs/38803020443 is green. The macos job ran 172 test targets: 785 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored. Among them:
    - `dirs::tests::this_platforms_folders`: a child process with `HOME` in a temp folder, XDG variables unset and set, finds every app's Library folders;
    - `saving::tests::a_sweep_keeps_the_copies_of_running_slates`: a real second process holds a copy. The sweep keeps it and removes a gone one's copy and lock. After `SIGKILL`, the next sweep removes the killed process's copy;
    - the `owner` and `recovery` tests;
    - the fs_usage step.
    - Run 38802370139 failed only on Etch's `the_list_lives_in_etchs_state_folder`, which asserted `etch/recent`; the test now knows the macOS folder.
  - Proof, Linux: `cargo fmt`, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (0 failed), and the script unittests pass. GUI checks on the release builds: `check_recovery` RECOVERY OK, `check_drop` DROP OK, `check_xlsx` XLSX OK, `check_frame` FRAME OK, `check_etch` ETCH OK, `check_slate` SLATE OK.
  - For later: Frame's package copies have no sweep, so a killed Frame leaves its copy in its cache folder for good. Frame could take `suite_file::owner` locks as Slate does. Windows needs a `dirs` arm (`%APPDATA%`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%`); `File::lock` works there too. Recent-list labels still read `$HOME` for `~` (F12; that's display, not a folder).
- **2026-10-15 MAC-1:** `ci.yml` has a `macos` job: `macos-14` (arm64, macOS 14.8.9, Homebrew GTK 4.22.4, Rust 1.99.1), `brew install gtk4 pkgconf`, clippy `-D warnings` on all targets and features, `cargo test --workspace`. Green: actions/runs/38794704200 (cold cache, job 4m14s: brew 11 s, clippy 64 s, tests 148 s) and actions/runs/38797153523 (warm cache, job 2m26s: clippy 14 s, tests 91 s); both 171 test targets, 772 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored, the same counts as Linux. Homebrew isn't cached: with `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE` the install takes 11 s (the image already has pkgconf and most of GTK's dependencies), less than restoring a cache would.
  - `suite_platform::Platform` gains `MacOs`; `current()` accepts `"macos"`, everything else (Windows) is still `Unsupported`, whose message now reads "only Linux and macOS are supported". Slate, Frame, and Etch only test `current()` for an error, so they now start on macOS; nothing else matches on `Platform`. Tests: `linux_and_macos_are_supported`, `this_build_is_supported` (passes on both runners).
  - Linux-only test code: the four `evict` helpers (`csv-engine/tests/{fetch,index}_perf.rs`, `brick/tests/first_rows.rs`, `brick/benches/corpus.rs`) drop the page cache with `posix_fadvise` (and `madvise` in fetch_perf) only under `cfg(target_os = "linux")`; elsewhere they do nothing, so those tests and benches still run on macOS, against a warm cache. That's honest enough: their timing gates belong to the Linux reference box. macOS could use `fcntl(F_NOCACHE)` or `purge` if Mac cold-cache numbers are ever wanted. Nothing else needed gating: the `/proc/self/status` RSS reads (inventory F21-F23) are in `#[ignore]`d tests or return 0, and they compile.
  - `/private/var`: the three `suite_file::recent` tests compared the list's canonical paths with paths built under `std::env::temp_dir()`, which on macOS is a symlink (`/var/folders/…` → `/private/var/folders/…`). The tests' folder is now canonicalised once when made, so every expected path is canonical, as the list's are; the product code (canonical paths stored and compared) was already right.
  - No other macOS compile or test failure: the SIGBUS guard test (`source::tests::truncation_while_mapped_*`) and `suite_file::recovery`'s `File::lock` tests pass on macOS 14 too.
  - Linux: fmt, clippy, `cargo test --workspace` (772 passed, 0 failed, 73 ignored), script tests (12 OK), `check_slate.sh` (SLATE OK), `check_frame.sh`, `check_etch.sh` (FRAME OK, ETCH OK). Brick's GUI checks weren't run: its runtime code is unchanged (only a test and a bench helper).
  - For later MAC stories: `spikes/dec-11/macos/ci/allow-macos.patch` no longer applies (this does it); the job runs no GUI, so the screenshots and smoke tests MAC-3/MAC-5 ask for need their own steps (`GSK_RENDERER=cairo`, as `spike-macos.yml` did).

- **2026-10-15 DEC-11:** cross-platform spike, in three slices (`spikes/dec-11/{inventory,windows,macos}/REPORT.md`); ADR 0011 proposed. New suite milestone XP0 (`backlog.py next suite`).
  - Windows (GitHub `windows-latest`, MSYS2 UCRT64 GTK 4.24.1 and MSVC with gvsbuild GTK 4.22.4): nothing compiles as is (`suite-file`'s `OsStrExt`, `csv-engine`'s libc SIGBUS guard). With 8 small experiments (`spikes/dec-11/windows/experiments.patch`, 34 lines) all four apps build, start, and draw their files, also as a self-contained folder (77 MB unpacked, 32 MB zipped for all four). Every save reports failure after replacing the file (the folder fsync opens a directory: os error 5); a second Slate deletes the first's package copy (`/proc` liveness); recent lists stay empty; Brick's state folder is relative; a console window opens beside each app; fonts fall back; fixtures turn CRLF (no `.gitattributes`). Renaming over Brick's mapped source works on Windows (the inventory's worry), truncating a mapped file doesn't. A Fedora MinGW cross build works but its GTK is a 4.21 snapshot that drew black with GL; Wine only as a smoke test. Runs: actions/runs/38781634209 … 38789516108.
  - macOS (GitHub `macos-latest` arm64, Homebrew GTK 4.24.1; Intel checked): everything compiles; 768 tests pass, 3 fail (`/private/var` temp paths), 4 perf targets need Linux's `posix_fadvise`. Brick runs as is; Slate, Frame, Etch need `suite_platform` to accept macOS. With that and the cairo renderer (GL drew black on the runner VMs), all four draw their files. Cmd shortcuts do nothing; Finder opens are dropped (apps read argv, not `GApplication::open`); only Etch uses the menu bar; dark mode not followed; Slate's package-copy sweep has the same `/proc` problem. Brick.app via dylibbundler: 31 MB, DMG 12.5 MB; Homebrew's libraries need the runner's macOS, so release builds belong on `macos-14`. Runs: actions/runs/38781402667 … 38789717490.
  - Inventory: 43 findings with file:line and the owner (`suite-platform` for folders, liveness, the primary modifier, fonts, file-open events). Every toolkit-free crate compiles for macOS; on Windows, three root causes.
  - Estimates in ADR 0011: an unsigned preview that runs like the Linux apps in about 2 days per platform; signed installers/bundles and CI about a week more each; Mac conventions about a week. Open questions for the user are in the ADR (signing accounts, which platform first, native folders, title bars, minimum OS).
  - Not exercised: real GPUs (renderer choice), HiDPI on Windows, input methods, drag and drop, printing, screen readers.
- **2026-10-15 ETCH-26:** the canvas draws the image in device pixels; tiles meet without seams and the checkerboard is crisp at any scale and with every GSK renderer. Root cause (GTK 4.24.1 source): at any render scale but 1, both renderers resample a texture-scale node from an intermediate at *widget* resolution. The GPU renderers (GL, Vulkan; `gsk/gpu/gskgpunodeprocessor.c`, `add_texture_scale_node`: `need_offscreen = … self->scale != 1`) draw it into an offscreen at scale 1 over its clip rounded out to widget pixels, then sample that linearly; the cairo renderer (`gsktexturescalenode.c`) fills its bounds into an image surface sized in user units with no device scale, antialiased, then paints it back scaled. ETCH-2 rounded tile edges to device pixels, which at scale 2 are half widget pixels: the intermediate's edge texel came out half covered, so where two tiles meet each leaves a half-transparent pixel and the checkerboard shows through, a pale line 2 device px wide (only edges at a half widget pixel: the site shot's y≈337 and x≈533, 812). At scale 1 there is no offscreen and edges are whole pixels, so check_etch.sh never saw it; there the checkerboard's region was the unrounded image rectangle, so at a half-pixel offset its last column and row blended into the boundary line.
  - Fix (`apps/etch/src/canvas.rs`): `snapshot` draws the boundary line, the checkerboard, the tiles, the floating pixels, and the shape preview inside `snapshot.scale(1/s)`, so user units are device pixels and the render scale is 1: the GPU renderers sample each tile directly (nearest, or mipmapped below 100%), and cairo's intermediate is at device resolution with whole-pixel bounds. The text raster does the same around its own texture. `Device` (scale, and how far into a device pixel the canvas's corner falls: `compute_point` to the native plus `surface_transform`, non-zero only at fractional scales) rounds every edge; `shown_rect` is the one formula for image pixels' device rectangles (tiles, floating pixels, selection outline, preview, text). Checkerboard squares are `round(8 · scale)` device px from the image's rounded corner, its region the rounded image rectangle; the line is `round(scale)` px. Overlays (selection outline, text frame and caret, tip ring) stay in widget units with edges from `Device`. `to_shown` (hit-testing) uses the same rounding. Tile invalidation is unchanged (still per changed tile). No texture borders or overlaps were needed: with the render scale at 1 nothing samples outside a tile.
  - Proof: check_etch.sh section 31 (54 checks; ETCH OK, 446 ok lines; section 8 there 0.049 ms segment p99, frames 4.1 ms p99): opaque #3366CC 1024×768 (12 tiles) at 100%, Fit (79.6%), 50%, 200%, 600×450 at Fit (135.8%); transparent 400×300, 401×301 at 100% and 1501×1001 at Fit (61%); output scale 1, 2, and 1.5, GL (the container's default: `GskGLRenderer` on llvmpipe; Vulkan has no driver there) and cairo. Pixels off the colour inside its box, before (v0.2.0) → after: scale 2, both renderers alike, 100% 4088 → 0, Fit 8108 → 0, 200% 7460 → 0, 135.8% 5676 → 0, 50% 0 → 0; scale 1, 0 → 0 everywhere. Checkerboard at scale 1: 400×300 at 100% crisp over 399×299 with a blended edge → 400×300 framed by the line; 1501×1001 at Fit 915×611 → 917×611 framed. Scale 1.5 with the corner offset forced to 0: 2880 off at 100% and 22050 checkerboard pixels off, so the offset is needed; with it 0. Unit tests `canvas::tests` (2): tiles meet on whole device pixels at scales 1, 2, 1.25 (offset) and four views; a 401×301 image at 100% spans exactly 401·s × 301·s. Bench (`--bench-stroke 120`, 8K, in the container on llvmpipe, 9 runs each, before vs after): segment p99 0.04–0.07 vs 0.05–0.08 ms (target 8 ms); frame CPU p99 2.8–4.5 vs 3.2–4.6 ms at scale 1 and 9.3–9.8 vs 7.9–9.3 ms at scale 2 (noise, no change); snapshot p99 0.10–0.16 vs 0.11–0.21 ms.
  - For later: not verified on this machine's GPU (NVIDIA, Vulkan under Hyprland): the session was locked, so no window could be shown or captured, and the reference box's frame time wasn't re-measured; the Vulkan renderer runs the same node processor as GL. `gtk_snapshot_set_snap` (GTK 4.24) would round edges for us but needs 4.24 (Ubuntu 24.04 has 4.14).

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-25:** crash recovery through `suite_file::recovery::Store`, after Slate's SLATE-17. New `apps/etch/src/recovery.rs`: `store()` (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/etch/recovery`, `<pid>-<n>.journal`, owner lock per process), `Header` (path, the file's size and modification time when read or saved, time written, image size), `write`/`read_image`, `crashed` (newest first, headers only), `crashed_for(path)`, `Recovered` (read off the UI thread with the file; `offer` asks in its window), `changed` (Keep for Later, Discard), `reopen`. `saving::Saver` keeps the window's journal as Slate's does: `WRITE_AFTER` (5 s) after the first change it doesn't hold, the image copied on the UI thread (as a save copies it) and written in `gio::spawn_blocking`, the next write scheduled once that one ends; nothing unsaved (saved, or undone to the saved position) removes it, and so does closing (saved or Discard). `Saver` now records the file's identity when read (`saving::Opened`) and after each Save. Model: `Operation::Replace` and `Document::replace(image)`, one step keeping the image before it whole (as crop and resize do), any size, always a step; `Canvas::restore` applies it and shows the image anew.
  - Decisions: **the journal is a binary header and a PNG** (`ETCHJ1\n`, little-endian numbers, the path's raw bytes so any Unix path survives; then `image_io::encode` PNG, the `image` crate's fast setting that saves use): lossless, alpha included, no serde in Etch. **`etch` alone after a crash opens each crashed image in its own window instead of the blank one** (Etch has no start window to list them): the file as it is now, or a new white Untitled image of the journal's size, each asking "Restore unsaved edits?" (Discard, Restore; Escape keeps it for next time). Opening such a file any other way (command line, Open, Open Recent, a drop) asks the same. Restore replaces the pixels as one step, so the title shows "• ", the file is untouched, and one Ctrl+Z gives back the file's pixels (an Untitled image's white). A file whose size or time changed since, **or that can't be opened any more**, isn't restored onto: after the open alert, "“NAME” changed after the crash" with Keep for Later and Discard (so a journal of a deleted file can go). A damaged journal says why, with Keep for Later and Discard (Slate only alerts). Paths are written canonical, so an etch started in another folder finds them. `--bench-*` windows don't journal. The journal holds the image's pixels: text being typed and floating pixels not landed yet aren't in it; a write during a stroke holds the stroke so far (and the next one follows when it ends).
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/replace.rs` (3): one step undoing and redoing byte for byte, another size undoing to the old size (history keeps 130×100×4 bytes; steps after it undo onto it), same pixels still a step that drops the redo. etch `recovery::tests` (2): `journals_round_trip_and_list_newest_first` (pixels exact with transparent pixels' colours, a non-UTF-8 path with a newline, a Slate journal left out), `a_journal_is_offered_only_onto_its_file_as_it_was` (changed size or time, file gone, Untitled, cut-short journal, removed journal). `cargo test --release -p etch --bin etch recovery -- --ignored --nocapture` (8K photo-like, opaque, 7680×4320, Ryzen 9 5900, NVMe): copy on the UI thread 15.3 ms, write (encode, fsync, rename) 255 ms median of 3 (253–266; asserted under the 5 s `WRITE_AFTER`), 61.4 MB (46% of the raw pixels), read back 192–195 ms. `check_etch.sh` section 30 (29 checks; ETCH OK, 392 ok lines): k.png's journal appeared 5.7 s after the drag began (its press at 0.5 s: ~5.2 s after the first change, polled every 0.1 s), kill -9 kept it and the file's md5; `etch k.png` asked "Restore unsaved edits?" ("Etch closed unexpectedly at 09:… while “k.png” had unsaved edits."); Restore: "• k.png", the stroke black on screen, file unchanged, the crashed journal gone; one Ctrl+Z: k.png, the band red again; killed again, `etch` alone opened k.png and asked, Discard left red and no journal; undoing back to the file removed the journal, Ctrl+W and Discard left none and the file unchanged; a touched file: "“k.png” changed after the crash", Keep for Later kept the journal; an Untitled image: `etch` alone opened Untitled 1024×768, Restore gave as much ink (301 px) marked, Ctrl+Z none. The check's sections now end with `quit` (SIGTERM, then the journals cleared), since SIGTERM leaves journals as a crash does (as `check_slate.sh` does).
  - For later: the crashed journal goes on Restore and the window's own is written 5 s later, so a second crash within those 5 s loses the restored pixels (Slate's too); adopting the crashed journal (rename) would close it. At 8K each journal write copies 132 MB on the UI thread (15 ms here) every 5 s while drawing goes on.

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-24:** every tool on its key. New `apps/etch/src/keys.rs`: `BINDINGS`, the one table of every key Etch binds (`Combo`: `Mods` ctrl/shift/alt and the GDK key name, letters lower case; `Command`: `Tool`, `Swap`, `Pan`, `Nudge`, `DropShape`, `Action("win.…")`, `Label("Hex")`; `Via`: `Accel`, `Window`, `Both`, `Mnemonic`), with `Focus` (Image, Typing, Field) and the pure predicate `takes(binding, focus)`: nothing while a field has the keys, only Ctrl+Shift ones while text is typed. The app builds everything from it: `accels()` → `set_accels_for_action` (replacing window.rs's list and `image_menu::set_accels`), `menu_item` → every menu item's shown key (replacing the hand-set `accel` attributes), `mnemonic("File")` → the menu bar's and the colour row's `_` labels, `tool_key` → `Tool::key()` and the tool tooltips, and `window_binding` → the window's capture-phase key handler, now a lookup plus `run(command)` (`focus_of` decides Field/Typing/Image as before: a focused `gtk::Text`/`TextView`, then `Canvas::typing`). `image_menu::shifted_key` is gone (the table routes Ctrl+Shift+X and R).
  - Audit: **no combination was bound twice** (the old handler, accelerators, menu attributes, and mnemonics checked against each other; Alt+T is both the colour row's Tolerance and New Image's Transparent, but in different windows, and a menu's own item letters and dialog mnemonics only work while it's open, so they aren't in the table). **Gaps fixed:** Ctrl+Shift+X and Ctrl+Shift+R had no accelerator, so with a field holding the keys they did nothing while Ctrl+Shift+S and E worked: now `Both` like those. Kept as they were: Escape (Deselect) drops a dragged shape first; Shift+Escape dropping a shape (the old handler did so for Escape with any modifier) is now its own row (`DropShape`); keys with Super, Hyper, or Meta held bind nothing (`Mods::of`), as the exact modifier match did. Behaviour change none otherwise: Ctrl+X, C, V, A and Ctrl+Shift+Z now run their `win.` actions (disabled without a selection, as the canvas calls they replaced did nothing then).
  - Decisions: **the table lives in the app crate** (`keys.rs`, toolkit-light: tests use only `gdk::Key::from_name`, which needs no display). **The spec's table is the test's input**: `every_shortcut_in_the_spec_is_bound_to_its_command` reads docs/specs/etch.md's Shortcuts rows (pairs by `;` then `,`, `or` alternatives, `(Ellipse: O)` as a pair) and maps the spec's words to commands in the test, so a spec row without its binding, or bound to something else, fails. **Menus show the table's first binding** for each action (Redo shows Ctrl+Y, as before).
  - Proof: etch `keys::tests` (8): `no_key_combination_is_bound_twice`, `every_key_is_a_gdk_key_by_its_own_name_a_letter_in_lower_case`, `every_tool_has_its_own_key_unmodified`, `no_tool_key_nor_x_while_text_is_typed_or_a_field_has_the_keys` (every binding refused in a field; tool keys, X, Space, arrows, Delete, Escape, Ctrl+A/X/C/V refused while typing; Ctrl+Shift+Z still redo), `every_shortcut_in_the_spec_is_bound_to_its_command`, `accelerators_are_the_actions_keys_in_order`, `mnemonics_underline_their_letter`, `modifiers_beyond_ctrl_shift_and_alt_bind_nothing`; mutating the table (Ellipse on K, Size's mnemonic on H) fails the spec, duplicate, and mnemonic tests. `check_etch.sh` section 29 (23 checks): P B E F I T R L S O each turn their tool on (AT-SPI, the tool column), X swaps and swaps back; "pebfilrostx" typed into text, the size (Alt+S), the tolerance (Alt+T), the hex field (Alt+H), and the font list's search leaves the tool and colours as they were; back on the canvas B picks the brush; in the open View menu F fits (79.6%) and the eraser stays. Whole check: ETCH OK, 363 ok lines.
  - For later: the font list's search takes Escape twice (the first stops the search, the second closes the list: GTK's). A Tools menu (spec layout) could list the tools with their keys from `keys::tool_key`.

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-23:** colour chooser and hex entry. `colours.rs`: `parse_hex` (`#RGB`, `#RRGGBB`, `#RRGGBBAA`, any case, `#` optional, surrounding spaces ignored, ASCII hex digits only) and `from_rgba` (a GDK colour to the nearest of 256 levels a channel, alpha too). `window.rs`: the colour row gains the _Hex label and field and a Custom… button after the indicator. `Controls::hex_entered` (Enter): a colour sets the foreground and gives the keys to the canvas; anything else adds the `error` class, sets `AccessibleState::Invalid`, and rings the bell, the colour unchanged. `Controls::show` refreshes the field only while it hasn't got the keys (`hex_has_keys`: the window's focus widget is in it), and the window's `focus-widget` notify puts the colour back once the keys go elsewhere in the window. `Controls::choose`: `gtk::ColorDialog` with alpha, offering the current colour, titled Foreground Colour or Background Colour.
  - Decisions: **one Custom… button, a right-click for the background**, matching the palette's left and right clicks, rather than a second button (the row stays small). **Valid Enter gives the keys back to the image**, so a typed colour is followed at once by tool keys; a refused one keeps them in the field to correct. **The `#` is optional** (pasting `3366CC` works). **Reverting goes by the window's focus widget, not a focus controller**: a focus controller's leave also fires when another window (or a new virtual keyboard, in the check) takes the keys, which threw away what was typed; the field now keeps it until the focus moves elsewhere in the window.
  - Proof: etch `colours::tests` (3): `hex_reads_three_six_or_eight_digits_and_nothing_else` (13 bad inputs, among them 4, 5, 7, and 9 digits, non-hex, full-width digits), `a_chosen_colour_keeps_its_alpha`, and `hex` round trips. `check_etch.sh` section 28 (16 checks): typing #3366CC, #f80, and #3366CC80 set the foreground; "pebfilrostx" in the field switches no tool and Enter refuses it (invalid state, colour kept), as #12345; a canvas click shows the colour again; Custom… opens Foreground Colour, Cancel keeps it, the chooser's editor with #20A040 and alpha 0.25 gives #20A04040; a right-click opens Background Colour and #FFCC00 becomes the background. New `px` commands: `at NAME TITLE` (screen point of a widget in a floating dialog via sway's tree), `set NAME VALUE` (a slider), `invalid NAME`, `pressed` (toggle buttons on). Full check: 340 checks, ETCH OK.
  - For later: ETCH-24 can build its tool-key check on `px pressed`; the hex field is a `gtk::Text`-focused field, so the window handler already passes its keys on.

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-22:** recent files. Brick's list (APP-4) moved to `suite_file::recent` now that a second app needs it (CLAUDE.md's rule; suite-file's docs named it): `Recent::load_from(store)` (no default store: each app names its own), `add` (canonical, to the top, once, ten at most), `remove`, `clear`, `label`; Brick's `state::recent()` gives its list in its state folder, its call sites otherwise unchanged. Etch's `recent.rs`: the store `$XDG_STATE_HOME/etch/recent` (`~/.local/state/etch/recent`, as Brick's), one `gio::Menu` for File → Open _Recent (after Open…), each item `name  (folder)` (underscores doubled, so none becomes a mnemonic) bound to `app.open-recent` with the path; "No recent images" greyed when empty. Every read and write runs off the UI thread (`gio::spawn_blocking`, the app held until written), then the menu is rebuilt if the list changed. `window::open` adds a file when it opens (or when its window is brought forward) and removes it when it can't be opened; `Saver::saved` adds after Save and Save As; each window becoming active re-reads the list, so another `etch` process's opens show up.
  - Decisions: **Export doesn't count as saved** (the window keeps its own file; the copy is a one-off), and `--bench-open` doesn't touch the list. **A missing file stays listed until chosen**, then alerts and leaves (Brick's behaviour), rather than checking every file's existence each time the menu is built. **No Clear Recent item** (Brick has one; the story doesn't ask, it can come with any later polish). **The menu reads the list when a window becomes active** rather than when it opens: a `gio::Menu` in the menu bar has no "about to show" hook.
  - Proof: `suite-file` `recent::tests::opened_and_saved_images_list_once_newest_first_ten_at_most` (14 images opened and saved, interleaved: 10 listed, newest first, each once; a deleted one stays until removed) beside Brick's four moved tests; etch `recent::tests` (2: labels with the folder from home, underscores kept; the store under `etch/recent`). `check_etch.sh` section 27 (12 checks), its own `XDG_STATE_HOME`: four runs opening r1, r2, r3, r1 store r1 r3 r2; an Untitled image saved as r4.png goes on top; a new run's Alt+F, R submenu reads (AT-SPI, `px menu`: the text of the label each popover item is labelled by) r4.png r1.png r3.png r2.png, "r4.png  (/tmp)"; Enter opens r4.png; with r2.png deleted, choosing it alerts "Cannot open “r2.png”" / "The file doesn't exist.", opens no window, and drops it from the file and the menu. Full check: 324 checks, ETCH OK.
  - For later: `px menu` reads popover menu items' text, so later checks can read menus rather than only drive them by mnemonics (ETCH-17's note).

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-21:** drag and drop to open. `window.rs`: every window (`new_window`, so the "Opening…" ones too) gets `accept_drops`, a `gtk::DropTarget` for `gdk::FileList` (COPY), as Slate's; the files open in an idle after the drop through `open_file`, which File → Open now shares (a file without a local path alerts "Only local files can be opened."), then `open`: an open file's window is presented, a new one says "Opening…" while the file is read off the UI thread, and a file that can't be opened alerts with ETCH-6's reasons and its window closes. `scripts/dnd_helpers.py source` takes several files now (one `text/uri-list`).
  - Decisions: **a drop opens; it never replaces the image in the window it lands on** (as Open, Slate, and Frame: an Untitled window stays as it is). **The check asks for "forward", not focus**: `present()` makes an xdg-activation request, which sway (the check's compositor) turns into the urgent mark by default, and only when the app had input lately (a click in the window first); with no recent input the compositor ignores it. Desktops decide; Etch asks.
  - Proof: `check_etch.sh` section 26 (9 checks): `etch t.png`, then a drag source floating in the corner drops one.png and two.jpg on its window: sway's tree shows one.png, t.png, and two.jpg, none urgent; after a click in the rightmost window, dropping t.png on it opens no window and marks t.png's urgent; dropping notes.png (text) alerts "Cannot open “notes.png”" / "It isn't an image Etch can open: PNG, JPEG, or BMP.", logged, and closing the alert leaves the three windows. Full check: 312 checks, ETCH OK.
  - For later: ETCH-22's recent files should take dropped files too (they go through `open`).

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-20:** rotate and flip. `image-model`: `orient.rs` with `Turn::{Right, Left, Half}` and `Flip::{Horizontal, Vertical}`; `Transform::Rotate(Turn)` and `Transform::Flip(Flip)` move every pixel whole (quarter turns write 16 new rows at once so each cache line of an old row serves them all; rows split across cores by `par::bands`). `Transform::inverse()`: the other quarter turn, the same half turn or flip, `None` for crop and the resizes. `Document::install` keeps the image before only for transforms without an inverse; `Document::undo` applies the inverse for those with one, and redo replays the transform (`Operation::apply`). The app: Image menu in four sections (crop; resize image and canvas; Rotate 90° Righ_t, Rotate 90° _Left, Rotate 1_80°; Flip _Horizontal, Flip _Vertical), `win.rotate-right` (Ctrl+H), `win.rotate-left` (Ctrl+G), `win.rotate-half` (Ctrl+J), `win.flip-horizontal`, `win.flip-vertical` (no keys), all through `Canvas::transform`. `Canvas::reshaped(before)` keeps the view when the size didn't change (flips, a half turn), still dropping every tile and the selection.
  - Decisions: **undo by the inverse, keeping no image**: exact, and an 8K rotation's step costs nothing instead of 133 MB, at 19–27 ms of UI-thread time per undo or redo (under the 200 ms budget; the stored-image transforms stay O(1)). ADR 0010's history bullet updated to say so. **Keys are Paint.NET's** (Ctrl+H right, Ctrl+G left, Ctrl+J 180°; none clash with the spec's keys or the unmodified tool keys); flips have none, as in Paint.NET. **The selection goes after a rotate or flip** (not transformed), as after the other transforms; undoing a same-size step (a flip) leaves the selection where it was.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/rotate.rs` (3): on six sizes (odd, even, a row, a column, 1×1, and 700×410, split across threads) four right turns, four left, two half turns, and two of each flip give back the original bytes; each transform puts every pixel where its formula says, and a quarter turn of 13×9 is 9×13; each undoes to the original bytes and size and redoes, with `history_bytes` 0; a run of rotations, flips, and a stroke undoes to the start and redoes to the end. `tests/transform_8k.rs` (release, ignored, `--test-threads=1`) on 7680×4320: right turn 24.2 ms, left 23.5, half 19.2, flips 19.5 and 18.3 off the UI thread; undo and redo on it 19.0–27.0 ms; copy 15–18 ms (Ryzen 9 5900). `check_etch.sh` section 25 (20 checks): Ctrl+H makes 300×400 with the red half on top and the checkerboard bottom right, saved with the corners turned; one Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300 with every region; Alt+I, V flips vertically, Ctrl+G and Ctrl+J turn, Alt+I, H flips horizontally, each saved with its corners where expected and each undone by one Ctrl+Z; four Ctrl+H saved equal t.png pixel for pixel, and one Ctrl+Z after them leaves 300×400.
  - For later: the 8K timing tests share the machine when run together; `--test-threads=1` gives the numbers above. Arbitrary angles (spec: later) would be a resampling transform keeping the image, like resize.

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-19:** Resize Canvas. `image-model`: `Transform::ResizeCanvas { width, height, anchor, fill }` and `Anchor` (nine, `ALL` row by row from the top left, `Centre` the default; `offset(old, new)`: where the old top-left pixel goes, 0, half the room, or all of it each way, the half truncated toward zero so a pixel left over goes right and down whether the canvas grows or shrinks). The new image is `fill` everywhere, then the old pixels copied where they land (`Rect::clipped`), so shrinking crops per the anchor. The app: Image → Resize Canvas… (`win.resize-canvas`, Ctrl+Shift+R through `shifted_key`, its menu item's `accel` attribute): `image_menu::dialog` with Width and Height (`side_field`), the anchor as a 3×3 `gtk::Grid` of grouped `ToggleButton`s (arrows ↖ ↑ ↗ ← ● → ↙ ↓ ↘ as a child label, accessible names Top left … Bottom right, tooltips; Centre on), and the new area Transparent (on) or Background colour (`window::background_of`, now `pub(crate)`, its hex in the tooltip and description). Resize runs `Canvas::transform`.
  - Decisions: **the new area is transparent at first** (the model is RGBA; Paint.NET fills with the secondary colour, Paint with white because BMP had no alpha); Background colour is the other choice. **A centred odd pixel goes to the right and bottom**, growing or shrinking (truncating `room / 2`): 5 more pixels add 2 left and 3 right, 3 fewer take 1 off the left and 2 off the right, so growing then shrinking back by the same amount about the centre gives the original. **No keep-aspect link** in this dialog: a canvas's sides are usually changed one at a time. **The anchor buttons hold their arrow as a child label** rather than `ToggleButton::with_label`: a button's own label becomes its accessible name (labelled-by), hiding "Top left" behind "↖".
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/canvas_size.rs` (3): each of the nine anchors on a 10×7 pattern grown to 15×12 gives its offset ((0, 0), (2, 0), (5, 0), …, (5, 5)) and every pixel where it says, the new area transparent or the given colour, each undoing to the original bytes and redoing; shrinking to 7×4 crops per each anchor ((0, 0) to (−3, −3)), every pixel the original's; wider and shorter at once (14×5, bottom right) places them at (4, −2); a same-size canvas makes no step, a 0-wide one is refused. `tests/transform_8k.rs` (release, ignored): 7680×4320 to 8000×5000 32.3 ms, to 4000×3000 11.6 ms off the UI thread; the copy 15.9–19.4 ms and undo/redo 0.0 ms on it. `check_etch.sh` section 24 (21 checks): Ctrl+Shift+R's dialog names its anchor buttons and offers 400 by 300, Centre on, Transparent on; Top left pressed through AT-SPI turns only it on; 600×400 shows the image top left and the checkerboard right and below, and saves `ff0000ff`/`0000ff80` at 0,0 and 399,299 with `00000000` beyond; one Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300 with every region; indigo the background (right-click), 500×400 at Bottom right with Background colour (Alt+G) saves `3f48ccff` at 0,0 and 99,99 and the old pixels from 100,100; 300×200 about the centre saves the red half's edge at x 150 (`00ff0000` past it) and the blue corner at 299,199; Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300.
  - For later: **ETCH-20** adds rotate and flip to `Transform` and the menu. ETCH-16's note: pasting an image larger than the canvas could offer to enlarge it now (`Transform::ResizeCanvas` with `Anchor::TopLeft`), as Paint does.

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-18:** Resize Image. `image-model`: `Transform::Resize { width, height, resampling }` with `Resampling::{Nearest, Smooth}` (`resample.rs`). Nearest: each new pixel the old pixel its centre falls in, `(2x + 1) · old / (2 · new)` in integers, so doubling copies each pixel into a 2×2 square and nothing new appears. Smooth: ImageMagick's Triangle filter, separable (taps per axis: a tent of radius 1 source pixel, widened to `old / new` when shrinking, centred on the new pixel's centre, `start`/`stop` and weights as IM's `HorizontalFilter`, normalized), premultiplied (colour times alpha summed with the alpha, divided back out at the end; no alpha at all is transparent black), f32 sums; each new row filters its few source rows across then sums them down, so memory is two rows a thread whatever the size. `par.rs`: `bands(out, row, f)` splits the new image's rows across `available_parallelism` threads (`std::thread::scope`; one thread below 1 MiB). `Image::check_size` is the size check `new` and `from_rgba` share, run before any pixel is computed. The app: Image → Resize Image… (`win.resize-image`, Ctrl+R): a dialog as New Image's (`image_menu::dialog`, `side_field`): Width and Height (1 to 16384, the image's at first), Keep aspect ratio (on: a side's change sets the other, `scaled`, rounded and clamped; a `linking` flag stops the echo), and Resampling, Smooth (on) or Nearest neighbour; Resize runs `Canvas::transform`.
  - Decisions: **the smooth filter is the triangle (tent) filter**, area-weighted when shrinking and bilinear when enlarging, rather than bicubic or Lanczos: no negative lobes, so no ringing or clamping in premultiplied alpha, and its reference is exact to reproduce (ImageMagick's `-filter Triangle -resize WxH!`, Q16-HDRI, which weights colour by alpha the same way). **Smooth is the default** (photos and screenshots are what's resized most); Nearest neighbour is the other choice, for pixel art. **No percentage field** (the story calls it optional): width and height with the aspect kept cover it, and the dialog stays small. **The choices aren't remembered** between dialogs. Smooth resizing to the same size would drop transparent pixels' hidden colour, so a resize to the image's own size makes no step at all (`Transform::unchanged`).
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/resize.rs` (6): nearest doubles a 13×9 pattern (opaque, see-through, transparent hiding colours) to 26×18 with every pixel its source's exactly, triples it too, and halving the doubled gives the original; smooth matches ImageMagick's references (`tests/data/resize/`: `source.rgba` 48×36 with transparent columns hiding bright green, partial alpha, stripes and a hard block; `down.rgba` 19×13, `up.rgba` 125×97, `wide.rgba` 100×20, made by `make_references.sh`, documented there) at most 0, 1, and 1 levels apart per channel; red beside transparent green shrinks to red at alpha 128 and enlarges to red fading out, never green; one colour stays exactly that colour at four sizes; same-size resizes make no step; a resize is one step, undo gives back the original bytes and size, redo the same result, both resamplings; sizes 0 and 16385 are refused. `apps/etch` `image_menu::tests` (1): the linked side rounds and clamps. `tests/transform_8k.rs` (release, ignored) on 7680×4320 off the UI thread: smooth to 3840×2160 44.9 ms, to 15360×8640 294.8 ms; nearest 6.8 and 61.5 ms; the UI thread's copy 14.7–16.9 ms and undo/redo 0.0 ms (Ryzen 9 5900, 24 threads): enlarging smooth is why transforms run off the UI thread. `check_etch.sh` section 23 (20 checks): Ctrl+R's dialog offers 400 by 300, Keep aspect ratio and Smooth on; Escape changes nothing; Width 600 sets Height 450; Nearest neighbour and Enter make 600×450, marked, each region on screen; saved, the halves meet exactly at x 300 and y 225 (`ff0000ff`, `00ff0000` hidden colour kept, `0000ff80`); one Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300; smooth to 200×150 saves `ff0000df` and `ff000020` either side of the red edge (alpha-weighted, no green), and Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300.
  - For later: **ETCH-19** reuses `image_menu::dialog` and `side_field` for Resize Canvas; its key can be Ctrl+Shift+R (Paint.NET's) through `shifted_key`. A percentage field, or remembering the resampling, can come with ET3's polish.

- **2026-10-15 ETCH-17:** crop to selection, and the Image menu. `image-model`: `transform.rs` with `Transform` (`Crop(Rect)` so far): `apply(&Image) -> Result<Image, SizeError>` builds the new image without touching the old (the rectangle clipped; off the image is `Empty`), `unchanged(&Image)` (a crop to the whole image). `Document::transform(t)` applies and records; `Document::install(t, result)` records a result computed elsewhere (off the UI thread). A transform's step is `Operation::Transform` with `Step::other`, the whole image on the step's other side: before it while done, after it while undone; undo and redo `mem::swap` it with the document's, so neither recomputes nor copies (ADR 0010's "save the whole image once"), and `history_bytes` counts it. The app: an Image menu after View (`image_menu.rs`: `menu()`, `add_actions`, `update`, `shifted_key`), `win.crop` (Crop to Selection, Ctrl+Shift+X through the window's key handler as Ctrl+Shift+S is, shown by the item's `accel` attribute; enabled with a selection and no command running). `Canvas::crop` commits (text, floating pixels land: the selection is where they landed), then `Canvas::transform(Crop(selection))`: the image is cloned on the UI thread, the transform runs in `gio::spawn_blocking`, the canvas is insensitive and `busy` meanwhile (undo, redo, delete, paste, the key handler, and the Image menu wait; saving still copies the image as it is), and the result goes in with `Document::install` and `Canvas::reshaped`: every tile texture dropped, the selection gone, the view anew (`View::new`: 100% centred or fitted, as opening), the accessible description and status updated. Undo and redo compare the image's size before and after and reshape when it changed (`Canvas::history`).
  - Decisions: **Ctrl+Shift+X** for Crop to Selection (Paint's and Paint.NET's; the spec gave none; Ctrl+W is Close). **The selection goes after a crop** (and after undoing or redoing a step that changes the size) rather than becoming the whole image: Paint deselects too. **The view resets** to how an opened image shows (100% centred, or fitted when larger) rather than keeping the zoom: the image's size changed under it. **Undo and redo swap the stored image** rather than replaying the crop: both are O(1) at any size, and the step holds one whole image either way. **Every Image menu command runs off the UI thread** through the same path, even crop, which would take 22 ms on the UI thread for 8K: one path, and the UI thread's share (the copy, 16 ms) stays the same for slower commands (resize). Floating pixels landing before a crop are their own undo step (as before saving), so one Ctrl+Z after a crop of moved pixels gives back the full image with them landed.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/crop.rs` (4): a 70×65 crop of a 150×90 image is the selection's bytes exactly (transparent pixels' hidden colour too), one step; undo gives back 150×90 byte for byte, redo the crop; a fill before and a stroke after a crop undo and redo through it exactly, the step keeping at least the whole image; a crop partly off crops to the part on (and `install` with a result computed elsewhere is the same, undoing exactly); a whole-image or wholly-off crop makes no step; a 1×1 crop keeps its pixel. `tests/transform_8k.rs` (release, ignored): on 7680×4320, copying the image for the thread 16.9 ms worst, cropping to 7000×4000 22.1 ms worst (off the UI thread), undo and redo 0.0 ms (Ryzen 9 5900). `check_etch.sh` section 22 (14 checks): Ctrl+Shift+X without a selection does nothing; Alt+I, C crops a 100×100 selection over red, transparent, and blue: 100×100 in the status, no selection, the title marked, the screen showing each region; Ctrl+S writes 100x100 with the corners `ff0000ff`, `00ff0000`, `0000ff80`; one Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300 with every region, Ctrl+Y crops again; moved floating pixels land then crop to 100×80 all red, and one Ctrl+Z gives back 400×300 with them landed.
  - For later: **ETCH-18 to ETCH-20** add `Transform` variants (resize, canvas, rotate, flip) to `transform.rs`, menu items and actions to `image_menu.rs` (and keys to `shifted_key` or accelerators), and call `Canvas::transform`; nothing else in the canvas changes. GTK's popover menu items have no accessible names (AT-SPI shows `menu item ''`), so the check drives the menu by its mnemonics and reads the result rather than the menu.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-16:** cut, copy, and paste through the system clipboard. `image-model`: `Image::from_rows(width, height, data, stride)` repacks RGBA8 rows `stride` bytes apart (a texture download's, rows may be padded), keeping the top-left `MAX_SIDE` pixels a side past it. The app: Edit menu Cut, Copy, Paste in their own section above Select All (`win.cut`, `win.copy`, `win.paste`; Cut and Copy enabled with a selection, Paste always). `Canvas::copy` puts a `gdk::MemoryTexture` (`R8g8b8a8`, GDK's straight-alpha format) on `widget.clipboard()`: the floating pixels' own texture while they float (no copy), else the selection's pixels from the image (one copy, as `lift`); GTK serializes it to image/png and its other image types when another app asks. `Canvas::cut` is copy then `delete_selection` (one step). `window::paste` reads `read_texture_future` (async), then `canvas::pasted` off the UI thread (`gio::spawn_blocking`: `gdk::TextureDownloader` to `R8g8b8a8`, `Image::from_rows`, and the texture shown), then picks the selection tool and `Canvas::paste(pixels, texture)`: text being typed commits, the selection goes (floating pixels land), and a `Floating::new` floats at the top-left of the image's visible part.
  - Decisions: **the keys are the window key handler's**, not accelerators (the menu shows them through the `accel` attribute, as Select All's), so a field keeps its own Ctrl+X/C/V, and **while text is typed they do nothing to pixels**: the text box has no clipboard of its own (pasting text into it would be a text-tool story). **Paste goes at the top-left of the visible part of the image**, the image's own top-left when it shows (Paint's), so a paste is always in view. **Pasted pixels replace what's under them when they land** (ETCH-15's landing; see-through pixels come in see-through, as copied), not blend. **Paste picks the selection tool** (Paint does), so the paste can be dragged at once. **An image larger than the canvas floats whole and lands clipped**: Paint asks to enlarge the canvas; until ETCH-19's resize exists there's nothing cheap to offer (moving the paste shows its other parts before it lands). Over 16384 px a side, the top-left 16384 is kept rather than refusing. **No image on the clipboard: nothing happens** (logged at info), no alert. **The clipboard isn't handed to a clipboard manager on exit**: what Etch copied goes when Etch quits (GTK 4 apps' default; `gdk::Clipboard::store_future` is the hook if users ask).
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/paste.rs` (3): padded rows become the image byte for byte (and unpadded ones; a 0-wide one is `Empty`); 16387×2 rows keep the top-left 16384 of each row exactly; a 14×11 paste on a 10×8 image moved by (−2, −1) lands clipped, every pixel the paste's (transparent ones too), one step, undo gives the original bytes and redo the pasted ones. `check_etch.sh` section 21 (14 checks, all ok; the whole check ends `ETCH OK`): another GTK 4 app (`/tmp/clip.py`, Python GObject, its own floating window) reads Etch's copy of a 100×80 selection as a 100×80 texture with pixels `ff0000ff`, `00ff0000` (transparent, its colour kept), and `0000ff80` (straight alpha exact), `wl-paste -l` lists image/png; Cut marks the title, shows the checkerboard there, gives the other app the same image, and one Ctrl+Z gives the red back unmarked; the other app's 60×40 PNG (opaque blue and blue at alpha 128) pastes as `Selection: 60×40`, blue on screen, title unmarked, the selection tool on; Ctrl+Z drops it; pasted again and dragged 100 px it moves (red back where it was, blue where it went, unmarked); Escape lands it (marked), and Select All + Copy give the other app `0000ffff`, `0000ff80`, and `ff0000ff` beside, exact; Ctrl+Z takes it back (red, unmarked), Ctrl+Y lands it again; while text is typed Ctrl+V pastes nothing.
  - For later: the check's other app offers PNG bytes (`Gdk.ContentProvider.new_for_bytes`): PyGObject has no `Clipboard.set_texture`, and `new_for_value` with a Python texture offers no types. A Wayland client takes the selection only with an input serial (the other app sets it on a key press) and reads the clipboard only while focused (`swaymsg [app_id=dev.cronkite.Etch] focus` after). **ETCH-19 (resize canvas)** can offer to enlarge the canvas when a paste is larger than it (`Canvas::paste` knows both sizes). **ETCH-17 (crop)** is unaffected: crop calls `Canvas::commit`, which lands a paste too. Copying a whole 8K image builds its texture on the UI thread (one copy of the pixels, ETCH-15's lift measured 26 ms); GTK encodes the PNG only when another app asks.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-15:** the rectangular selection. `image-model`: `select.rs` with `Floating` (pixels, the image pixel their top-left is over, and the `source` rectangle they were lifted from, `None` for pixels from elsewhere; `move_by`, `move_to`, `contains`, `bounds(image)` where they'd land, `unmoved`) and `spanning(a, b, bounds)` (the rectangle a drag spans: edges at the pixel edges nearest each point, clipped). `Document::lift(rect)` copies the rectangle (clipped) into a `Floating` without touching the image; `Document::land(floating)` is `Operation::Land(Floating)`: the source cleared to transparency, then the pixels replacing what's under them where they are, clipped, one step keeping the tiles both cover and the pixels (redo lands the same bytes); unmoved pixels, or pasted ones wholly off the image, make no step. `Document::clear(rect)` is `Operation::Clear(Rect)` (Delete), no step where the pixels are all bytes 0 already. `Image::fill_rect` and `copy_from` (row copies). The app: the tool column's Selection (S, a dashed-rectangle icon), `Action::Select`; the canvas holds `selection: Option<Rect>` (on the image) or `floating: Option<Floating>` with its texture, and the drags `Drag::Select { from }` and `Drag::Move { from, at }`; `draw_floating` paints the source's hole and the landing area with the checkerboard (clipped) and the floating texture over it; `draw_selection` strokes a white line with black 4-px dashes just outside the selection (the floating pixels' box while they float). `Canvas::select_all`, `deselect`, `delete_selection`, `nudge`, `commit` (text and floating pixels, before saving and closing), `selection_size`. Status bar: `Selection: W×H` between the position and the zoom. Edit menu: Select All, Deselect, Delete, their keys shown with the menu item's `accel` attribute (Slate's way) and handled by the window's key handler, so the size field and text being typed keep Ctrl+A, Escape, Delete, and the arrows; Deselect and Delete are disabled without a selection.
  - Decisions: **landed pixels replace what's under them** (see-through and transparent ones too, as they were lifted), not blend: Paint's and Paint.NET's moving, and moving a selection back and forth gives the original bytes. **The place they left is transparent**, not the background colour (the model is RGBA; Paint.NET does so; Paint fills with the background because BMP had no alpha). **Pixels float until the selection goes** (Escape, a click or drag outside, another tool, Select All) **or the image is saved or closed** (they land, the selection staying where they landed), so several drags and nudges are one step; **Ctrl+Z while floating drops the move** (the selection goes back to where they were lifted from), as Ctrl+Z drops text being typed; Ctrl+Y does nothing then. **Choosing another tool deselects** (Paint; nothing else clips to the selection yet). **Select All picks the selection tool** (Paint). **Delete keeps the selection** (Paint.NET, GIMP); with pixels floating it drops them, leaving their source transparent (Paint). **The arrows nudge** a pixel, Shift ten (Paint.NET), lifting the pixels. **The outline is static dashes**, not marching ants (no animation to redraw every frame); dark dashes on white show on any colour, the dashes offset half a pixel so each pixel is black or white. **Selection drags read the pointer against the origin rounded to device pixels**, where the tiles are drawn: unrounded, a centred image at a half-pixel origin made a 100-px drag select 101. For the same reason the checkerboard now starts on a device pixel: its squares' edges at half pixels let the pixels under the hole show through between squares. Everything runs on the UI thread: an 8K select-all lifts in 26 ms and lifts and lands in 125 ms at worst.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/selection.rs` (7): lifting leaves the image (and its position) as it was; landing after two moves leaves alpha 0 where the pixels were and places the bytes exactly (every pixel against the expected image), one step; a 30×20 move across tiles keeps exactly the 2 tiles it touched, undoes in one step to the original bytes and redoes exactly; moves off each edge and corner and wholly off clip (8 offsets, each against the expected image, each undoing), a rectangle partly off lifts the part on the image; landing back where they were lifted makes no step; pasted pixels (`Floating::new`) replace what's under them, transparent ones too, and wholly off make no step; Delete clears a rectangle partly off the image to transparency, no step for an already transparent one or one off the image, undo and redo exact; `spanning` rounds to the nearest pixel edges either way round, clips, and a click spans nothing. `tests/selection_8k.rs` (ignored, release): lift 7680×4320 24 ms median, 26 ms worst; lift and land moved 10 px 73 ms median, 125 ms worst; delete 32 ms median, 34 ms worst (target 200 ms; Ryzen 9 5900; a second run 21, 115, and 28 ms worst). `tools.rs`: `the_selection_selects_and_has_no_size`. `check_etch.sh` section 20 (18 checks) on t.png: S picks the selection, no size shown; a 100×80 drag says "Selection: 100×80", the title unmarked, and its top and left edges are dashed (30–70% black and white along each); a drag inside moves it 200 px right: still "Selection: 100×80", unmarked, the place left the checkerboard (all), the new place red (all), dashed; Escape lands it (no selection, "• t.png", the same pixels); one Ctrl+Z gives the red and the transparent quarter back, unmarked; Ctrl+Y moves it again; Shift+Right moves an 80×80 selection's pixels 10 px (the strip left checkerboard, the strip reached red); P lands it and deselects; Ctrl+A with the pencil picks the selection, "Selection: 400×300"; Escape deselects; Delete clears to the checkerboard, marked, Ctrl+Z gives the red back; while text is typed, Delete deletes a character and Ctrl+A selects nothing (text "a", no selection, Text on). ETCH OK, 213 checks (the sections before unchanged, Ctrl+A in the size field and New Image's fields still theirs); `--bench-stroke` in the container 0.03 ms segment p99.
  - For later: **ETCH-16 (paste)**: make the pasted image a `Floating::new(pixels, at)`, put it in `Canvas::floating` with its texture (the code in `Canvas::lift` that builds the texture), and it floats, moves, nudges, and lands through the same paths (landing with no source clears nothing); Delete or Ctrl+Z while it floats drops it. Pasted pixels replace what's under them; if paste should blend over instead (the ETCH-12 note), give `Floating` a mode then. Copy and cut read `Canvas::selection_box` (the floating pixels' own bytes while they float: `Floating::pixels`); cut is copy then `delete_selection`. **ETCH-17 (crop)**: call `Canvas::commit` (lands floating pixels; the selection stays where they landed) and crop to `selection` (a `Rect` already clipped to the image); after the crop the canvas must drop its tiles, view, and selection, as the image's size changes. Nothing clips painting to the selection; if it should, that's a later story.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-14:** the fill's tolerance. `image-model` `fill.rs`: `distance(p, q)`, the largest difference of any channel, alpha included, 0 to 255; with a fully transparent pixel on either side only alpha counts. A pixel fills when its distance from the clicked pixel is at most the tolerance (inclusive). The app: `tools::Settings::tolerance` (0 at first, kept across tools), `Action::Fill { tolerance, .. }`, `Canvas::fill(at, colour, tolerance)`; a Tolerance field (`_Tolerance`, 0 to 255, its meaning in the tooltip) in the colour row, shown only while the fill is the tool.
  - Decisions: **the metric is the largest channel difference in levels, 0 to 255** (GIMP's threshold), not Paint.NET's percentage: it's what ETCH-9's model already took, it's exact to reason about (a colour at the tolerance fills, one level beyond doesn't), and the field shows the number the model uses. **Default 0** (exact), so ETCH-9's behaviour stands until the user asks for more. **Transparent pixels' hidden colour never counts**: ETCH-9 matched fully transparent pixels only to each other; with a tolerance, against a fully transparent pixel only alpha counts, so a transparent seed takes nearly transparent pixels of any colour and the hidden RGB can't make the result differ. The tolerance isn't saved across runs.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/tolerance.rs` (5): at tolerances 1, 10, and 54, each channel (alpha too) at +t and −t fills and at ±(t+1) doesn't, and all channels at t at once still fill (the largest counts, not the sum); tolerance 0 is exact (±1 in any channel doesn't fill); 255 fills everything connected; a transparent seed takes transparent pixels hiding any colour and any colour up to alpha 5 at 5, not alpha 6, and at 0 only transparent ones, and a seed of alpha 5 takes transparent pixels; a tolerance fill undoes and redoes byte for byte. `tools.rs`: the fill's action carries the tolerance and keeps it across tools. `check_etch.sh` section 19 (6 checks): on n.png (left half white, right 249,249,249) Tolerance is hidden for the pencil and shows 0 for the fill; at 0 and at 5 a click on the left fills it red (all) and the right none (0/56064); at 6 both all; pencil and back keeps 6. ETCH OK, 195 checks. `tests/fill_8k.rs` (release, Ryzen 9 5900): whole blank 8K 87.2 ms median, 146 ms worst (target 200 ms; ETCH-9 measured 81.5, +7%, under the 10% note threshold), bounded by lines 45.8 ms.
  - For later: ETCH-24 (every tool key) may want a key for the tolerance; the magic wand, if it comes, can share `distance`.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-13:** New Image's background, and what it asked remembered across runs. The app: `settings.rs` (`Settings { new_size, new_background }`, `Background` White, Transparent, or Colour with `color(background)`; `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/etch/settings`, `key=value` lines `new-width`, `new-height`, `new-background`, parsed tolerantly as Slate's; written with `suite_file::save_atomic` after making the directory). `window.rs`: the dialog's Background row, three grouped check buttons (Wh_ite, _Transparent, Back_ground colour, the last described and tooltipped with the colour's hex); `NEW` replaces `LAST_SIZE`, loaded from the file the first time New Image asks; Create writes the file off the UI thread (`gio::spawn_blocking`, the app held until it's written) only when the choice changed; `CONTROLS` maps each window to its controls so Background colour is the active window's background (white with no window); `open_new` takes the colour.
  - Decisions: **the background choice is remembered too**, not just the size (it's part of what the dialog asked; Paint.NET keeps it). **`etch` without a file still opens white 1024×768** (spec ET0); only New Image follows what's remembered. **Background colour is read when the dialog opens**, from the window it was asked from. The settings file has no Saver thread like Slate's: it's written once per Create, and `save_atomic`'s temp names are unique a call, so two writes can't collide.
  - Proof: unit tests in `settings.rs` (4): `a_transparent_new_image_is_all_alpha_0` (and the other two backgrounds' exact bytes), `settings_round_trip`, `unusable_values_keep_the_defaults`, `written_settings_read_back` (no temp file left). `check_etch.sh` section 18 (14 checks): the dialog offers White (on), Transparent, Background colour (#FFFFFF); Alt+T and 300×200 make Untitled 2 showing the checkerboard over every pixel (`checker all light 1 grey 1`), saved as /tmp/clear.png it reads back `300x200 alpha True max 0`; the settings file holds `new-width=300 new-height=200 new-background=transparent`; after quitting and starting again Ctrl+N offers 300 by 200 with Transparent on. ETCH OK, 189 checks.
  - For later: ETCH-19's resize canvas can use `Background` for its new area (transparent or background). Other remembered things (tool sizes, colours) can add keys to the same file.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-12:** the text tool. `image-model`: `blend.rs` with `Blend` (pixels and the image pixel their top-left goes at) as `Operation::Blend`, committed by `Document::blend(at, pixels)`: each pixel composited source-over (straight alpha, `brush::over`) on the one under it, transparent ones skipped, clipped to the image, the tiles it covers saved; the step keeps the pixels, so redo lays the same bytes (ADR 0010's stored result for an operation that can't be replayed from parameters). Pixels wholly off the image make no step. The app: `text.rs` with `TextLook` (family, size, bold, italic, colour), `families` (Pango's families from the canvas's font map, plus `Sans`, sorted, listed once a process in a `OnceLock` so a family is a `&'static str` and `Settings` stays `Copy`), `TextBox` (the text, a byte caret, an input method's preedit, its `pango::Layout`, and its `Raster`), `render` (the layout drawn through a GSK text node onto a cairo ARGB32 surface clipped to the image, `straight` to RGBA, cropped to the ink, plus a texture), and `KeyOutcome`; Text (T, a T icon) in the tool column, `Action::Text(TextLook)`, `Settings::{family, bold, italic, sizes()}` with `TEXT_SIZES`; the canvas holds the `TextBox`, a `gtk::IMMulticontext` (set on a canvas key controller only while typing) and draws the raster as the tiles are drawn, a dashed frame, and a caret; `Canvas::{typing, text_click, commit_text, cancel_text}`; the window's capture-phase key handler lets every unmodified key (tool keys, X, Space) through while text is typed; a font list (`gtk::DropDown`, searchable like Slate's format bar), Bold, and Italic show beside the size for text; `Controls::showing` keeps the controls' own updates (the size's range changing per tool) from writing settings back.
  - Decisions: **size in image pixels** (the em, Pango's absolute size), 24 at first, 4 to 400 (its own range: text wants more than the pencil's 100); the field says px like the others. **Enter commits, Shift+Enter is a new line** (multi-line text, as Frame and Slate break lines). **The click point is the text's top-left corner**; a click off the image starts no text. **A click elsewhere commits and starts no new text** (MS Paint), choosing another tool, saving, or closing the window commit too (closing then asks to save; nothing is dropped unasked). **Ctrl+Z while typing drops the text** (only it; the image is untouched, as undo drops a shape drag), **Ctrl+Y does nothing while typing** (committing first would clear the redo it asks for).
