Backspace and Delete remove a character (as Frame's do), the arrows move by cursor position through `move_cursor_visually`, Up/Down by line, Home/End the line's ends (Ctrl: the text's). **Straight alpha**: the text is drawn opaque and the colour's alpha applied after; a pixel of the colour at some coverage gets the colour's exact RGB with alpha = coverage × colour alpha, so edges never drift in hue; other pixels (colour glyphs) are unpremultiplied. **Preview = what lands**: the canvas shows the rendered pixels (nearest from 100%, as tiles), not a vector layout, so zoomed in it shows the pixels it'll be. The canvas's accessible description says what's being typed ("1024×768 image, typing “Hello”"), for screen readers and the check. Rendering goes through `gtk::Snapshot::append_layout` and `RenderNode::draw` on a cairo surface rather than `pangocairo` (not a dependency of the workspace); it uses the canvas's Pango context, so hinting follows GTK's settings.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/blend.rs` (3): source-over byte for byte (opaque replaces; (0,0,255,128) over white (127,127,255); over transparent green the colour as is; over half red (85,0,170,192); a transparent pixel leaves (12,34,56,78)); clipping at 5 placements equal to the same blend on a larger image, the rect returned exact, 4 wholly-off placements no step; a 70×50 blend over 4 tiles keeps exactly those tiles' bytes, one undo restores the image and returns their rect, redo gives the blended bytes again. Unit tests: `text_is_typed_in_the_foreground_at_its_own_size_in_the_font_chosen` (tools.rs), `text_pixels_become_straight_in_exactly_its_colour`, `other_colours_are_unpremultiplied`, `the_ink_bounds_hold_every_pixel_with_alpha` (text.rs). `check_etch.sh` section 17 (24 checks): font controls hidden for the pencil; T on, Bold and Italic off, size 24; a click and "Hello" typed show 258 black pixels in the box, title "Untitled", described typing “Hello”; " pebfilrostx" typed goes into the text, Text still on, Pencil and Eraser off, colours unswapped; Enter marks the title and leaves ink, the box gone; one Ctrl+Z: no ink, unmarked; "Gone" then Escape: no ink, unmarked; "Click" and a click elsewhere: marked, ink, no new text; Ctrl+Z back; "illicit fillip": Bold more black pixels, DejaVu Sans Mono picked from the searched list draws it wider (Sans 112 px), "!" typed after the pick goes into it; through an input method on the seat (`scripts/ime_helpers.py`, now in this check too, with wqy-zenhei installed) the composition "かな" shows underlined (192 black pixels, nothing typed yet), the commit "仮名" is typed, Enter puts it on. All 175 checks passed (ETCH OK); `--bench-stroke` segment 0.053 ms p99 here.
  - For later: ETCH-16's paste can commit through `Document::blend` (a floating paste over transparent pixels blends; if paste should replace instead, add a mode then). ETCH-24's key table now has P, B, E, F, I, T, L, R, O; while text is typed the window handler passes every unmodified key on (`Canvas::typing`), which ETCH-24's test can assert. Text isn't editable once committed (spec). Rendering runs on the UI thread a keystroke at a time (a line at 24 px is a few hundred pixels; a 400 px multi-line text is a few megapixels); if huge text ever lags, render off the thread. The input method's caret follows zoom and pan; Ctrl+Left/Right don't move by words yet.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-11:** line, rectangle, and ellipse with previews. `image-model`: `shape.rs` with `Style` (Outline, Filled, Both), `Kind` (`Line`, `Rectangle(Style)`, `Ellipse(Style)`), `ShapeTool` (kind, width, outline and fill colours; `at(from, to, constrain)` floors the points and applies Shift), and `Shape` (tool, from and to pixels) as `Operation::Shape`, committed by `Document::shape`. A shape is computed as a few spans a row and handed out by `Shape::rects(clip)` as non-overlapping one-colour rectangles, rows alike merged (a rectangle outline is 4); committing paints exactly those, and the canvas previews by drawing them as colour nodes, so the preview is what lands. The line is the pencil's (Bresenham stamped with the pencil's disc; `pencil::bresenham` factored out, `Pencil::rows`/`reach` crate-visible); each row of a stamped line is one run, kept as a per-row min/max. The rectangle's outline is a band inside the box, square corners. The ellipse is Zingl's `plotEllipseRect` midpoint ellipse (even boxes included, error terms in `i128`), stamped with the pencil's disc along the box inset by the disc's reach, so wide outlines stay in the box; the left half's discs and the right half's each cover one run a row, and between them is the inside, which Both fills. Filled (no outline) uses the one-pixel geometry and everything inside it. `Document` gained `Covering` (a flag a tile before `Saved::cover`, now shared with the fill) and `record`; `geom::pixel` moved out of `document.rs`. The app: Line (L), Rectangle (R), Ellipse (O) with path icons; `Action::Shape`; each its own size (1 at first); Outline/Filled/Both linked buttons beside the size while the rectangle or ellipse is the tool (`choice` builds them and the brush's Hard/Soft); `Canvas::shape_begin/shape_to/shape_end/cancel_shape` with `Drag::Shape`, the preview's rectangles kept in `preview` and recomputed only when the shape changes; Escape (window key handler) cancels a shape drag; undo and redo drop a drag under way.
  - Decisions: **colours**: the outline is the foreground; Both fills with the background; Filled alone is the foreground (XP Paint with the left button and Paint.NET do so; a background-filled shape would vanish on the default white). **Shapes composite source-over, one coat** (as the brush, not replacing as the pencil): for opaque colours the same, and it lets the preview be plain colour nodes over the image; a see-through colour never doubles where spans would meet since they don't overlap. **One style shared** by rectangle and ellipse (Paint's single fill-style box). **Shift constrains** (cheap): a line to the nearest 45° (tan 22.5° thresholds), a box to a square on the drag's longer side; it's read from the motion events, so pressing or releasing Shift shows on the next pointer move. A click without moving draws the one-pixel shape (Paint does too). The right button doesn't draw shapes with swapped colours (Paint's); left for later if wanted. Commits run on the UI thread like the fill.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/shapes.rs` (13): exact masks for a line at widths 1 and 5 (the 5 identical to the pencil's stroke), a rectangle outline at 1 and 5 (and dragged any way), Both and Filled, an ellipse outline at 1 (odd and even boxes, a 20-px circle, boxes 2 wide and 1 high) and at 5 with Both, a box narrower than the outline, Filled at 1 and 5 and Both at 1; clipping at the edges (6 kinds × widths 1, 5 × 3 placements equal to the same shape cropped from a larger image; wholly off: no step); `rects` merges rows (a rectangle outline is 4 rects, Both 5), clips, and paints exactly the committed pixels with no overlap; a half-transparent outline and fill lay one coat ((127,127,127) and (127,127,255) over white); Shift's constraints; four shapes undo and redo byte for byte; an outline round a 1024-px square keeps exactly its 60 edge tiles (filled: 256), an ellipse ring under 100, one undo each. Unit test `shapes_outline_in_the_foreground_and_fill_with_the_background` (tools.rs). `check_etch.sh` section 16 (18 checks): R with Outline on at size 1; at size 5 a drag held 6 s shows the rectangle (5-px band black, inside white) while the title stays "Untitled", the release lands the same black pixel count and marks the title, one Ctrl+Z leaves no ink and no mark; O and Both with Red the background preview a black-outlined red ellipse (box corner white), unmarked, landing the same, one Ctrl+Z back to white; L hides the style, a held line previews 41/41 along its path, Escape drops it (no ink) and the release then draws nothing; a line lands and Ctrl+Z takes it back. All 151 checks passed (ETCH OK). Measured (release, Ryzen 9 5900, scratch run): computing a preview on 7680×4320 takes 2.2 ms for a whole-image Both ellipse at width 100 (10 266 rects), 0.9 ms for a width-100 line, 0.1 ms for a 1-px ellipse; committing them 69, 2, and 1 ms (a whole-image filled rectangle 43 ms). Fill unchanged after the `Covering` refactor: 80.3 ms median on blank 8K (was 81.5).
  - For later: ETCH-24's key table now has P, B, E, F, I, L, R, O. ETCH-12's text preview can follow the same pattern (state in `Drag`, an overlay drawn after the tiles, commit as one `Document` operation). Below 100% the preview's rectangles aren't snapped to device pixels (snapped, a thin outline at Fit could vanish), so they sit up to half a screen pixel off the trilinear tiles there.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-10:** the colour picker. The app only (`Image::pixel` already reads a pixel): Colour Picker in the tool column (I, an eyedropper icon), `tools::Action::Pick`, `Settings::set_tool` (remembers the tool before the picker) and `Settings::pick(colour, background)` (sets the colour, returns the tool to go back to); `Canvas::pick` reads the pixel under the pointer, alpha included, and hands it to `Canvas::connect_picked`'s callback, which the window wires to `pick` and then activates `win.tool` with the tool before. The picker has no size, so the size hides. The colour row already showed alpha (ETCH-4's `hex`: `#RRGGBBAA` when not opaque, a checkerboard under a see-through square) in its tooltip and accessible description.
  - Decisions: **a pick goes back to the tool used before the picker** (Paint's behaviour; picking then painting is I, click, drag); choosing the picker twice keeps the earlier tool to return to. **Right-click picks the background**, as in Paint. **Alpha is picked as is**, fully transparent pixels included (their hidden RGB too: picking the transparent green gives `#00FF0000`). Picking isn't an edit: no history step, the title stays unmarked. The cursor stays the crosshair (GTK has no picker cursor name, and the crosshair shows the pixel).
  - Proof: unit test `a_pick_sets_a_colour_alpha_included_and_goes_back_to_the_tool_before` (tools.rs). `check_etch.sh` section 15 (10 checks): on t.png with the brush the tool, I turns the picker on and hides the size; a click on the red half gives "Foreground #FF0000, background #FFFFFF" (the indicator's accessible description) and the brush is the tool again; I and a click on the half-transparent blue give #0000FF80; I and a right-click on the transparent green give background #00FF0000; the brush comes back after each; the title stays "t.png". All 133 checks passed (ETCH OK).
  - For later: ETCH-23's hex entry and chooser can reuse `colours::hex` and should take alpha the same way; ETCH-24's key table now has P, B, E, F, I.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-9:** the fill bucket. `image-model`: `Fill` (`fill.rs`: where, colour, `tolerance`) as `Operation::Fill`, and `Document::fill(at, color, tolerance)`: a scanline flood fill, 4-connected, a row span at a time, each seed carrying the span it was found from so looking back at that row skips it; a pixel matches when each channel is within the tolerance of the clicked pixel's (0: exact), and fully transparent pixels match each other whatever colour they hide. Pixels written stop matching, so no visited map is needed unless the colour put down itself matches the seed (only possible with a tolerance; then a bitset). Before each span is written the fill saves the 64-px tiles it overlaps (a dense flag per tile, then `Saved::cover`), so history keeps only touched tiles; redo replays the fill. An exact fill of the colour already there, or a click off the image, makes no step. `Image::pixels` (pixels as `[u8; 4]`). The app: Fill in the tool column (F, a bucket icon), `tools::Action` (`Draw(Tip)` or `Fill { foreground, background }`, from `Settings::action()`, replacing `tip()`), `Settings::width()` now `Option` (the fill has none, so the Size label, field, and px hide), `Canvas::set_action` and `Canvas::fill`: a click fills with the foreground, a right-click with the background.
  - Decisions: **the fill runs on the UI thread**: 8K whole-image fills take 81 ms median, 141 ms worst with history (under the 200 ms target, a few frames' hitch at most), and running it off-thread would need the image handed over and drawing blocked meanwhile anyway. **Right-click fills with the background**, as in Paint. **Transparent pixels are one colour** for matching (their hidden RGB is invisible); exact otherwise, so a pixel one level off stops the fill. The fill replaces pixels (alpha included), it doesn't blend. `tolerance` is in place for ETCH-14 (channel-wise, inclusive), with no UI.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/fill.rs` (7): a wall one level off white stops the fill and the rect is exactly the region; a one-pixel diagonal isn't crossed (4-connected); a comb's winding region is all reached; transparent pixels hiding different colours fill as one region and alpha 1 stops it, undo restores the bytes; filling with the colour there or off the image makes no step; a box's inside fill keeps exactly its 9 tiles, undo restores the image and returns those tiles' rect, redo gives the filled bytes, the outside's fill keeps 255 tiles (all but the one inside the box); tolerance 0 is exact and 3 takes near colours while the colour put down is within it. `tests/fill_8k.rs` (ignored, release, Ryzen 9 5900): the whole blank 7680×4320 81.5 ms median, 140.9 ms worst; a region bounded by a zigzag and a frame 43.6 ms median, 47.5 worst (target 200); redo (the fill alone, no tile saving) 48 and 37 ms. `check_etch.sh` section 14 (7 checks): F picks the fill and hides the size; a click fills a pencil box red to its edges, outside untouched; a right-click fills the outside indigo; Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y take back and restore each. All 123 checks passed (ETCH OK).
  - For later: ETCH-14 adds the tolerance's control and passes it to `Canvas::fill` (now 0). At Fit on 8K a whole-image fill drops every tile texture, so the next frame rebuilds all of them (the same cost as the first frame after opening).

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-8:** the brush, hard or soft. `image-model`: `Brush` (`brush.rs`: colour, width, `soft`) and `Tip` (`Pencil` or `Brush`; `Stroke` now holds `tip`, and `Document::begin_stroke` takes `impl Into<Tip>`, so pencil callers are unchanged). A brush segment covers each pixel whose centre lies within the width's radius of the path between the two points' disc centres (a capsule, so no stamp spacing or gaps): 255 inside a hard disc, a smoothstep from 255 at the centre to 0 at the rim for a soft one; the pixel keeps the most coverage its stroke gave it (a per-stroke `Coverage` map of 64-px tiles, dropped when the stroke ends) and becomes the brush's colour at that coverage composited source-over (straight alpha) on the pixel from before the stroke, read from the step's saved tiles. So a slow stroke never compounds within itself (Paint.NET's and Paint's behaviour); a second stroke blends over the first. Redo replays the stroke with fresh saved tiles and coverage, byte for byte. Disc centres follow the pencil's: a pixel's centre for odd widths, its corner for even ones. The app: Brush in the tool column (B, a brush icon drawn as a path), its own size (8 at first), and Hard and Soft linked toggle buttons beside the size, shown only while the brush is the tool; `Settings::soft`, `Settings::tip()` now returns a `Tip`; the canvas holds a `Tip`.
  - Decisions: **Soft is the brush's default edge** (it's what sets the brush apart from the pencil; Paint's default brush is antialiased too). A hard brush is a solid disc of the same capsule geometry, not antialiased, still composited (a translucent foreground, from ETCH-10's picker or ETCH-23, lays one coat a stroke). The soft falloff is smoothstep over the whole radius, so a soft line's opaque core is about a third of its width.
  - Proof: `image-model` `tests/brush.rs` (8): a hard brush fills exactly its disc at widths 1, 2, 8, 9, 20 and returns a rect holding it; a hard stroke is solid along its path, 5 px wide; a soft dab's alpha equals the smoothstep coverage pixel by pixel to 0 at the rim (128 at half the radius); a soft dab over half-transparent blue is source-over byte for byte; back and forth slowly gives the same bytes as one pass, and a translucent hard stroke crossing itself stays one coat; a second stroke blends over the first; soft and hard strokes over a patterned image undo and redo byte for byte; a dab keeps one 64-px tile. `check_etch.sh` section 13 (13 checks): the edge hidden for the pencil, B picks the brush with Soft on at size 8; at 15 the soft line's column has 5 black and 8 grey pixels, the hard line's 15 black and none grey; Ctrl+Z takes back each, Ctrl+Y gives the soft one back the same; the edge hides again on P. All 116 checks passed (ETCH OK); `--bench-stroke` segment 0.056 ms p99 here.
  - For later: shapes (ETCH-11) can composite with `brush.rs`'s `over` if they antialias; the brush has no spacing or pressure, by design (not a brush engine).

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-7:** Save, Save As, and Export as PNG, JPEG, and BMP; unsaved changes. `image-io` now writes: `encode` (PNG RGBA8 through `PngEncoder`; JPEG at `JPEG_QUALITY` 90 and BMP 24-bit, both RGB8 from `on_white`), `save` (encode, then `suite_file::save_atomic` with a verify that reads the temp file back and compares it to the encoded bytes, Slate's pattern), `Format::for_path` (extension, any case: png; jpg, jpeg; bmp; else `UnknownExtension` whose Display is the alert's reason), `Format::keeps_alpha`, `has_transparency`; `read` now returns the format too. `image-model`: `Document::position` (each step numbered when made; `None` mid-stroke), what dirty state compares. The app: `saving.rs` (`Saver`, modelled on Slate's: `win.save`, `win.save-as`, `win.export`, the save queue, `ask_before_closing`), `Canvas::copy_image`/`position`, `window::moved` (the open-files registry follows Save As), Quit closes each window so each asks (was `app.quit()`), `untitled()` ignores the "• ".
  - Decisions: **Export is its own command** (File → Export…, Ctrl+Shift+E, the shortcut GIMP uses): it writes a copy in the format its extension picks while the window keeps its file, format, and unsaved state; Save As makes the written file the window's. **Save writes the format the file was opened in** (told by content), not by its extension: a JPEG named .png stays a JPEG. **Transparency into JPEG or BMP asks first** ("Save “x.jpg” without transparency?", Cancel or Save; the verb is Export for exports), every time such a save happens, after a background scan for alpha below 255; the window keeps its transparency and counts as saved afterwards (as with JPEG's loss). JPEG quality 90 (the `image` crate's default is 75). A name without png/jpg/jpeg/bmp is refused with the reason, not given an extension. A new image is clean until edited (closing an untouched Untitled window doesn't ask), as in Slate; Save on it asks where (Untitled.png, or "Untitled 2.png", in the Pictures folder). Save failures alert "Cannot save “NAME”" with a worded reason (permission, missing folder, full disk, else the error) and keep the window open in the close flow. Saving mid-stroke records a position that never matches, so the title stays marked.
  - Proof: `image-io` `tests/save.rs` (6): PNG reads back byte-identical, alpha and a transparent pixel's colour included; `on_white` values; BMP 24-bit reads back exactly `on_white`; JPEG reads back within 3 levels a channel, 0.42 on average, of `on_white` (asserted 16 and 2.0), the transparent band white; extensions and what each encoding is by content; an unwritable directory leaves the old file byte for byte and no temp file. Unit tests (2) through `write_atomic`: a writer failing halfway, and a short write caught by verify ("1000 bytes written of the image's 4096"), each leave the old file and no temp. `image-model`: `positions_tell_whether_the_image_is_back_where_it_was`; `tests/copy.rs` (ignored, release): copying 7680×4320 takes 18.0 ms median, 19.5 worst (budget 60) on the Ryzen 9 5900. `check_etch.sh` section 12 (32 checks; 103 in all, ETCH OK): title "• s.png" after a stroke, cleared by Ctrl+S with the stroke's black in the file (GdkPixbuf), marked again, cleared by undoing to the saved image, marked by redo; Ctrl+W's dialog with Cancel (window stays, marked), Discard (closes, file md5 unchanged), Save (more black in the file, closes); Ctrl+Shift+S's Save As with the PNG filter first, /tmp/s2.jpg written as JPEG and the window renamed; erasing then Ctrl+S asks about transparency, Save writes it white (less black); Save As from the JPEG offers the JPEG filter; Ctrl+S on Untitled shows Save As with Untitled.png. Saves in the container: 400×300 PNG 8 ms, JPEG 5 to 11 ms.
  - For later: the GTK filter dropdown shows only the chosen filter to AT-SPI, so the check sees PNG and JPEG filters, not BMP. Save As onto a file open in another Etch window doesn't merge the two windows (the other keeps showing its old pixels). Recent files (ETCH-22) should record saves too; crash recovery (ETCH-25) can journal from `Saver`'s position, as Slate's `saving.rs` does. Formats written aren't remembered between runs: Save As offers the image's own format.

- **2026-10-14 ETCH-6:** opening PNG, JPEG, and BMP. New toolkit-free crate `image-io` (`read`, `decode`, `format_of`, `OpenError`, `Format`) over the pure-Rust `image` crate, `default-features = false`, features png, jpeg, bmp (orchestrator's codec decision; `[workspace.dependencies]`); `Image::from_rgba` in `image-model`. The app: `etch FILE...` (one window each, titled with the file name), File → Open… (Ctrl+O, `gtk::FileDialog` filtered to "Images (PNG, JPEG, BMP)"), read and decoded in `gio::spawn_blocking` while the window says "Opening…" (Frame's pattern), then `show` fills that window; a failure alerts "Cannot open “NAME”" with `OpenError`'s reason and closes the window; a file already open presents its window. `--bench-open FILE` prints JSON from process start to the end of the first frame that drew the image (`Canvas::frames_drawn`). Desktop entry `Exec=etch %F`, `MimeType=image/png;image/jpeg;image/bmp;`; `sites/etch/install.sh` got Frame's `set_defaults` for those three types (uninstall drops only Etch's); the download page says so.
  - Decisions: Ctrl+O always opens a new window, even from an untouched Untitled one (as Slate and Frame do, and as New Image does: nothing is ever replaced), not Paint's replace-in-place. Format by content (`image::guess_format`), never the extension: a GIF named .png says "It's a GIF image. Etch opens PNG, JPEG, and BMP images.". BMP alpha: every BMP opens opaque, including 32-bit ones with an alpha mask (the acceptance's "BMP opaque"; Paint ignores BMP alpha, and the fourth byte of most 32-bit BMPs is junk). The size is checked against `MAX_SIDE` from the header before decoding, and decoding runs with no `image` allocation limit (its default 512 MiB is short of 16384²). zune-jpeg silently fills a truncated JPEG with grey, so a JPEG with no end-of-image marker after its last scan is refused as damaged ("the file ends too soon").
  - Fixtures (`apps/etch/crates/image-io/tests/data/`, made by `make_fixtures.sh` with ImageMagick 7, each with a `.rgba` reference ImageMagick decoded): `alpha.png` (16×12 RGBA, fully transparent, partial, and opaque columns), `photo.jpg` (4:4:4) and `photo420.jpg` (2×2 chroma), `rgb24.bmp` (13×7, padded rows), `alpha32.bmp` (BITMAPV5 with an alpha mask; reference with alpha off), `tiny.gif`. PNG and BMP match byte for byte. JPEG can't: decoders may round IDCT, colour conversion, and fancy upsampling differently, and zune-jpeg and libjpeg-turbo do, so the test allows 2 levels a channel at 4:4:4 and 5 at 4:2:0 (measured worst; means 0.033 and 0.335) and a mean under 0.5; another upsampling or a colour shift is tens off.
  - Proof: `image-io` tests (11): every fixture's pixels against its reference, PNG alpha kept (0, partial, 255, and a transparent pixel's colour), JPEG and BMP all 255; PNG, JPEG, and BMP cut in half → damaged; a spoilt PNG → damaged; GIF and GIF-named-.png → unsupported GIF; text named .png → not an image; empty, missing; 16385×1 header → too large, before decoding. `image-model`: `an_image_from_rgba_holds_those_pixels`. `check_etch.sh` section 11 (18 checks; 71 in all, ETCH OK): `etch t.png` titled t.png, 400×300, 100%; on screen the opaque half red, the transparent quarter the checkerboard (white and grey, no green), the half-alpha quarter blended blue; Ctrl+O's dialog (Open, the images filter), Ctrl+L and a path open c.jpg (320×200) beside Untitled; `etch bad.png` alerts "Cannot open “bad.png”" / "It's a damaged or incomplete PNG image (the file ends too soon)." and Enter closes it and etch exits; a text notes.png alerts "It isn't an image Etch can open: PNG, JPEG, or BMP.". `cargo test --release -p etch --test open_8k -- --ignored`: a 7680×4320 photo-like RGB PNG (26.4 MB) shows in 659 ms median of 3 (697 ms in an earlier run; target 1000; read and decode 210 ms off the UI thread, the image handed to the window at ~390 ms, snapshot 65 ms, fitted at 23.6%; Ryzen 9 5900, Hyprland, file in the page cache). `scripts/ship.sh etch build` made `dist/etch-0.1.0-x86_64-linux.tar.gz` (551 KB, up from 329 KB with the codecs; sha256 4bdf1552…); `verify` printed RELEASE OK and INSTALL OK on Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, Fedora: over eog's image/png and image/jpeg defaults, image/png, image/jpeg, and image/bmp are `dev.cronkite.Etch.desktop` (gio names it), image/gif and text/plain stay; `gio open /tmp/t.png` starts `~/.local/bin/etch /tmp/t.png`, which logs `opened /tmp/t.png: 16×12`; uninstall leaves no Etch default and the others as they were. Nothing uploaded or deployed.
  - For later: ETCH-7 writes through `image-io` too (save there, atomic through `suite_file`). cronkite.cc (`sites/cronkite/index.html`, `llms.txt`) still says Etch opens no files and takes no file types: true of the released 0.1.0; update it with the release that ships ETCH-6. The checkerboard's squares sit at the view's unrounded origin, so with a half-pixel origin their edges blend (229/230 greys in screenshots); the check accepts neutral greys.
- **2026-10-14 ETCH-5:** Etch's desktop entry and icon (`packaging/dev.cronkite.Etch.*`), installer and download page (`sites/etch/`), and install check (`scripts/check_install_etch.sh`). Proof: `scripts/ship.sh etch build` made `dist/etch-0.1.0-x86_64-linux.tar.gz` (329 KB, sha256 5191c432ecc6…, `etch --version` prints 0.1.0); `scripts/ship.sh etch verify` printed RELEASE OK (glibc floor, `--bench-stroke 5` on Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, Fedora) and INSTALL OK (each distro: install, checksum refusal, GTK hint, `gio launch` of the menu entry starting `~/.local/bin/etch`, `mimeapps.list` byte for byte unchanged, update, uninstall). `upload --dry-run` names `etch/releases/v0.1.0/` in the bucket. Nothing was uploaded or deployed.
  - Decision: Etch's installer takes no file types, since Etch opens no files yet; its desktop entry has no `MimeType`. **User decision (2026-10-14), for ETCH-6:** once Etch opens images, installing it makes it the default for PNG, JPEG, and BMP, replacing whatever was the default, as Frame does for .pptx; not merely listed under Open With. The desktop entry gets those `MimeType`s and the installer sets them in `mimeapps.list` (Frame's `set_defaults`); uninstalling drops Etch's defaults. ETCH-6's acceptance says so.
  - cronkite.cc lists Etch as an "Early preview" (2×2 app grid, its install and uninstall commands, llms.txt). Deploy the suite page only after Etch's first release and site deploy, or its command 404s; the ship-it skill now says so.
- **2026-10-14 ETCH-4:** colours and sizes. `tools::Settings` (tool, foreground, background, each tool's own size, `tip()`, `swap()`) is toolkit-free and unit-tested; the window's `Controls` holds it and pushes the tip to the canvas (`Canvas::set_tip`, which replaced `set_tool`), the colours to the indicator, and the size to the spin. `colours.rs`: Paint's 20 colours (Windows 7 Paint's first two rows), palette buttons (left click foreground, right-click background, a11y names the colour), and the indicator whose accessible description reads `Foreground #RRGGBB, background #RRGGBB`. Sizes 1–100, pencil 1 and eraser 9 by default; the tip's ring shows around the pointer once it's 4 screen px across (also a 1 px pencil from 400%). X swaps; tool keys, X, and Space are ignored while a text field (the size) has focus. Proof: 2 `Settings` tests and a `hex` test; `check_etch.sh` step 10 (53 checks in all, all passing). Step 5's sharpness box moved off the pointer, where the ring now draws; `dnd_helpers.py click` takes a button (right-click).
- **2026-10-14 ETCH-3:** the eraser and the tool column. The eraser is `Pencil::eraser(width)`, the pencil's tip writing `Color::TRANSPARENT` (writes replace, so it erases to alpha 0 with no new operation kind); 9 px wide until ETCH-4's sizes. `tools.rs` holds `Tool` (id, label, key, tip, icon) and the tool column: toggle buttons bound to the stateful `win.tool` action, icons drawn as stroked GSK paths in the theme's colour (Adwaita has no eraser icon, and this needs no icon theme). Unmodified tool keys go through the window's capture-phase key controller; ETCH-12's text tool must bypass them while typing (ETCH-24). Proof: 2 `image-model` eraser tests; `check_etch.sh` step 9 (43 checks in all, all passing).
- **2026-10-14 ETCH-2:** Etch's window and canvas (`apps/etch`: `main.rs`, `window.rs`, `canvas.rs`, `view.rs`), section 38 of the user's spec. `check_etch.sh` passes all 36 checks; `--bench-stroke 300` on this machine (Ryzen 9 5900, Hyprland, GPU renderer): frame CPU 1.2 ms p99, segment 0.05 ms p99 (target 8 ms); in the check's software-rendered container 3.2 ms. `check_release.sh` runs `etch --bench-stroke 5` (limit 120 s: Broadway without a browser ticks about once a second) and passed on Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora with the host build.
  - For the next stories: tools reach pixels only through `Canvas::draw_begin`/`draw_to`/`draw_end` → `Document`; each returns the changed `Rect`, and `Canvas::invalidate` drops the 256-px tile textures it overlaps. The eraser (ETCH-3) and colours (ETCH-4) replace the `PENCIL` const with the window's tool state; the checkerboard already draws under the tiles.
  - Tile edges are rounded to device pixels: unrounded, Cairo-style renderers (the container's) left faint seams between tiles. `View` keeps its origin unrounded, so repeated zooms don't drift off the pointer.
  - Ctrl+Shift+Z is handled by the window's key controller, not an accelerator: wtype's keymap doesn't consume Shift, so `<Control><Shift>z` never matched there (Frame does the same). Edit → Redo shows Ctrl+Y.
  - `scripts/dnd_helpers.py` gained middle-button drags and `hold` (a second virtual pointer that stays, so the app keeps seeing the pointer between helper calls); wtype's `-P` needs a leading `-s` pause, or the press arrives before its keymap and is dropped.
- **2026-10-14 ETCH-1:** `image-model`: `Image` (RGBA8, `MAX_SIDE` 16384), `Color`, `Rect`, `Pencil` (Bresenham, disc stamps: offsets from `-(w-1)/2`, so even widths lean right and down), and `Document` with `begin_stroke`/`extend_stroke`/`end_stroke`, `undo`, `redo`, `history_bytes`. A stroke that touches no pixel leaves no step. 16 tests.
- **2026-10-14 SUITE-5:** Etch, the suite's raster image editor (user request, with a product spec). `docs/specs/etch.md` condenses it; ADR 0010 decides the image model (RGBA8 straight alpha, toolkit-free `image-model` crate), history (operation plus the 64-px tiles it replaced; redo replays), and canvas (256-px tile textures, nearest at 100% and up). The spec's five phases became milestones ET0–ET3 (its Phase 5 is the spec's later list, no milestone). Decisions taken here, not in the user's spec: the app is `AppKind::Etch` beside `Brick` (the spec says "Bricks"; the repo renamed it Brick in PKG-5); Ctrl+0 is Actual Size and Ctrl+9 Fit to Window; Ellipse's key is O; New Image opens a new window, so nothing unsaved is replaced; images are capped at 16384 px a side; the desktop entry and installer (ETCH-5) are in ET0 so ET0's gate can ship. `scripts/backlog.py` reads `ET` gates and has the `etch` chain; both skills now cover every app; `scripts/ship.sh etch` is wired (bucket `etch/`, `CF_ETCH_PAGES_PROJECT`, tags `etch-vX.Y.Z`), and `preflight` fails while an app lacks packaging, a download page, or an install check.
- **2026-10-14 SLATE-28:** list markers from the numbering (ADR 0009). A subagent built it; it ran out of budget before `flow_perf` and the GUI check, and both were run here.
  - Model `numbering.rs`: `ListLevel` (format, text with symbol characters mapped, lvlJc, suff, the level's indents and tabs, its run look), `ListLevel::slate` (Slate's own levels, also what save's `add_numbering` writes), and `Numbering`. `ListItem.numbering: Option<ListNumbering { num, mark }>`, where `mark` is the paragraph mark's run look. `DocxInfo.numbering`. `Format::list_level(item)`.
  - `StyleSheet::marker_look`, `list_spacing` (style < level < own), and `list_tabs`.
  - `symbol_text` maps Symbol's full table and Wingdings by Unicode's mapping, with ➢ for 0xD8 and ❏ for 0x71. The mapped level then loses its symbol font.
  - Reader: levels' `lvlJc`, `suff`, `pPr`, and `rPr`. The marker font follows the hint, eastAsia, cs, then ascii and hAnsi. `ParaProps.mark` reads `w:pPr/w:rPr`.
  - Flow: `Content::place(MarkerWidth)` replaces `text_left` and `first_indent`. The marker sits at the first line's start, aligned by lvlJc. A tab suffix goes to the next stop past the marker's end, the implicit stop at the hanging indent counting; that implicit stop now counts for text tabs too, SLATE-24's leftover. `Shaper::marker_width` measures the marker.
  - Word list items now take their style's spacing and their level's indents: SLATE-26's 28 px clamp and ignored first-line indent are gone for Word. Markdown and text lists are unchanged.
  - Hit-testing, the caret, and accessible text leave markers out, as before.
  - `scripts/lo_docx.py lists OUT` makes `tests/data/lists.docx`, 26 paragraphs: Google's ●○■ levels, Symbol 0xB7, Courier New o, Wingdings 0xA7 and 0xD8, `%1.`, `%1.%2.`, `(%3)`, right-justified upperRoman, space and nothing suffixes, empty lvlText, own indents over a level, and a red 14 pt paragraph mark.
  - The dump's LABEL is now the bullet's character, and it gains NUMLEFT, NUMFIRST, MARKERJC, SUFFIX, and MARKERFONT. All 9 dumps were regenerated.
  - Old expectations changed: a Word list item's flow indent moves from 28 to 48 px (the level's 720/360), its tab stops shift to match, and the Markdown own-indent test was split from a new Word one.
  - Deviations:

    - A Word list item's borders start at its text, not its marker.

    - Markdown: whole 300 ms, typing p99 17.6 ms.

- **2026-10-14 SLATE-27:** paragraph borders and shading (ADR 0009). A subagent built it; it was integrated and verified here.
  - Model: `BorderStyle` (None, Single, Thick, Double, Dotted, Dashed) and `Border` (style, `w:sz` eighths, `w:space` points, colour or automatic); `Borders` holds top, bottom, left, right, and between. `BorderOverrides` sets sides one by one, so a nil side removes the style's. `Shading { fill }`. `ParaLook` gains borders and shading, and `ParaOverrides` gains `borders` and `shading`. Resolvers: `StyleSheet::borders`, `shading`, and `bordered_together` (Word's group rule).
  - Reader: `w:pBdr` (start and end as left and right; no bar border) and `w:shd`, merged side by side along pPrDefault, basedOn, and the paragraph, in table cells too. Styles map as LibreOffice reads them: dash variants draw dashed, triple, thinThick, and the like double, wave, 3-D, and art single. `clear` uses the fill, `solid` the colour, and pctNN blends.
  - Flow: `TextBox.frame` holds the paragraph's frame. The top and bottom zones (space plus lines, whole px) add to the height in measuring and estimating, inside the space before and after. Left and right lines lie their space outside the indents. In a group, the top line is drawn on the first paragraph, between lines on the later ones, and the bottom line on the last.
  - Paints `Fill` and `Stroke { dash }` are drawn in `view.rs`. A Markdown rule is now a paragraph with its own bottom border, written byte-identical to before.

- **2026-10-14 SLATE-26:** a paragraph's own spacing and indents (ADR 0009). A subagent built it; it was integrated and verified here.
  - Model: `Paragraph.own: ParaOverrides` holds space before and after, line spacing, left, right, and first-line indents, and contextual spacing, each `Option` (serde default; old journals load). `ParaLook` holds a `ParaSpacing`, which gained a right indent and contextual spacing. One resolver, `StyleSheet::spacing(&Paragraph)`, serves the flow, the drawing, and the LibreOffice test.
  - The reader reads `w:right` and `w:end` and `w:contextualSpacing`. Automatic spacing counts as 280 twips.
  - Saving keeps the template's `w:spacing`, `w:ind`, and `w:contextualSpacing` byte for byte when they give the model's values; otherwise it writes them from the model. This replaces the old rule that dropped `w:ind` when a paragraph's list status changed.
  - Enter keeps a paragraph's own looks on both halves. Joining or leaving a list, or changing level, drops its own indents.
  - Flow: own looks apply to paragraphs, table-cell paragraphs, and list items. A right indent narrows the wrap, and contextual spacing removes the space between neighbours of the same style.
  - Deviations:
    - List items keep the viewer's list spacing and level indent unless they have their own. Their own left indent is clamped to at least 28 px, and their own first-line indent is ignored. SLATE-28 puts the numbering level's indent there: style < level < own.
    - Negative indents are clamped to the column when drawing.
    - Automatic spacing ignores LibreOffice's special cases.
    - `beforeLines`, `afterLines`, `*Chars` indents, and `mirrorIndents` aren't modelled.
    - Indents written from the model use `w:left` and `w:right`.
  - Corpus: `tests/data/spacing.docx`, from `python3 scripts/lo_docx.py spacing OUT`, byte-identical each run. The dump gains RIGHT and CONTEXT, and all 7 recorded dumps were regenerated.
  - Acceptance:
    - `cargo test -p docx --test libreoffice`: 498 paragraphs' effective space before and after, line spacing, indents, and contextual spacing match LibreOffice.
    - `--test spacing --test save -- --include-ignored`: 12 and 5 pass.
    - Flow: 4 pixel-exact tests.
    - `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK, 256 checks, 9 of them new in section 31, measured over a decoy style: before 30, after 20, left 60, right 100, hanging 30/60, exact pitch 40. Typing and saving kept `w:spacing` and `w:ind` byte for byte.

- **2026-10-14 SLATE-25:** embedded fonts and family fallbacks (ADR 0009). A subagent built it; it was integrated and verified here.
  - Model: `document_model::fonts` (`FontTable`, `Font` with family class, pitch, altName, and embedded faces as `Arc<[u8]>`). `DocxInfo.fonts` holds the table, `Format::fonts()` returns it, and `DocxInfo` and `Format` are no longer `Copy`.
  - docx `fonts.rs` reads `fontTable.xml` through its relationship, its `.rels`, and the content types. A face is de-obfuscated when it's `.odttf`, has the obfuscatedFont content type, or isn't already a font: the GUID's bytes reversed, XORed over the first 32 bytes. A face is kept only if it then starts with an sfnt header.
  - Saving copies the font parts and `fontTable.xml` raw, and tests check they stay byte for byte.
  - App `src/fonts.rs`: the open worker writes each face once to `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/slate/fonts` (or the cache dir), mode 0700, named `<fnv1a64>-<len>.ttf`, atomically. `Page::new` adds new faces with `FcConfigAppFontAddFile` on the page font map's config, reads their families from the application set, and calls `pango_fc_font_map_config_changed` once.
  - The FFI is raw `extern "C"` against libfontconfig and libpangoft2, which GTK already ships; Pango 1.50+ is enough, and the Ubuntu 24.04 release image has both. No new crates.
  - Family mapping per font-table entry:
    - An embedded font keeps its name, or maps to the face's real family.
    - An installed font is left unchanged.
    - Anything else becomes `Name,generic`, by `w:family`: roman serif, swiss sans-serif, modern monospace, the rest serif.
    - Calibri still gets Carlito, because fontconfig's alias comes before the generic.
  - Deviations:
    - Embedded fonts stay loaded for the process, since fontconfig can't remove one.
    - Fonts missing from the table keep fontconfig's default.
    - altName and pitch are read but not used.
    - The format bar's style-list previews use the raw font name.
  - Test data: `apps/slate/crates/docx/tests/data/fonts/Cardo-Regular.ttf`, a 24 KB Latin subset of google/fonts ofl/cardo, with `OFL.txt`. The OFL reserves no font name.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK, 247 checks, 18 of them new in section 30. Plain (zero key) and obfuscated (.odttf) Cardo, a roman missing font, and a swiss one are measured against Pango at 96 dpi within 2 px:
    - Cardo: 310 against 311.
    - Roman: 367 against serif 368.
    - Swiss: 364 against sans 364.
    - After an edit and save, the font parts are byte for byte the same.
    - There's one private font file, in a 0700 directory, and no font is installed for the user.
  - Tests: docx de-obfuscation (GUID order, a reference vector, a round trip against an independent implementation, zero key, junk), `tests/fonts.rs`, family mapping, file writing.
  - `flow_perf`, within noise of SLATE-24:
    - Word document: whole 316 ms, first frame 8.6 ms, scroll p99 5.9 ms, typing p99 18.4 ms.
    - Markdown: whole 283 ms, typing p99 19.0 ms.
    - Find: first match 14.0 ms.
  - The user's résumé now draws its Cardo line in Cardo instead of sans. Its title's Georgia, not installed here, falls back to the system serif.
- **2026-10-14 SLATE-24:** tab stops (ADR 0009). A subagent built it; it was integrated and verified here.
  - Model: `document_model::tabs` (`TabStop` with position in twips from the margin, `TabAlign` including Clear, `TabLeader`; `merge_tabs`, `effective_tabs`). `Paragraph.tabs` holds the paragraph's own stops, clears included; serde default keeps old journals loading. `ParaLook.tabs` holds a style's merged stops, so `ParaLook` is no longer `Copy`. `StyleSheet::tab_stops(&Paragraph)`. `DocxInfo.default_tab` is `w:defaultTabStop` from the settings part, 720 when missing or 0.
  - docx: `w:tabs` is read from paragraphs, styles, and pPrDefault, merged along `basedOn`. start and end read as left and right, `num` as left, `bar` is skipped.
  - Saving keeps the template's `w:tabs` byte for byte unless the model's stops differ (only pasted paragraphs today); then they're written from the model, and a bar stop is lost. `document-edit`'s `bare()` keeps stops through Enter, restyling, and lists.
  - flow: `Content::tabs()` gives the stops right of the layout's edge, in layout px. Then come two default stops past the last explicit one, measured from the margin; Pango repeats that interval. Markdown and text use four spaces; code keeps Pango's default. `Content::text_left()` is now the one source for `TextBox.indent`.
  - The `PangoShaper` sets a `pango::TabArray` only on text with a tab. Measuring, drawing, hit-testing, and the caret share the one cached layout. Leaders are drawn in `view.rs` from `PangoShaper::leaders()`.
  - Word drops default stops left of a custom stop. So the check's right-stop line has one tab, as such lines do in Word. The résumé's "Remote" lines use only default tabs, and section 29's (b) covers that grid.
  - Word's implicit stop at a hanging indent isn't modelled (SLATE-28).
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK, 229 checks, 9 of them new in section 29 on a 720 px Letter page:

    - A centre stop at 5400 centres "Middle" at 360.0 against 360.
    - A right stop with a dot leader at 7200 ends "42" at 479 against 480; the dots fill the gap on the baseline (widest hole 3 px), with nothing above it.
    - A Markdown file with a tab opens and reads as written.

- **2026-10-13 SLATE-17:** crash recovery. Brick's journal directory (owner lock per process, atomic journal writes, crashed sessions newest first) moved to `suite_file::recovery` now that two apps need it; Brick keeps its header reading (`recovery::crashed`) and journal format. Slate's journal (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/slate/recovery/<pid>-<n>.journal`) is a `SLATEJ1 ` line with a JSON header (path, the file's size and modification time when read or saved, time written) and the document's blocks as JSON (pictures' bytes as number arrays, so a document with pictures makes a big journal).
  - `saving::Saver` keeps the window's journal: 5 s after the first edit it doesn't hold (`recovery::WRITE_AFTER`), it clones the document on the UI thread and writes it with `gio::spawn_blocking`; the next write is scheduled once that one ends, so writes come at least 5 s apart. Nothing unsaved (saved, or every edit undone) removes it; so does closing the window, which happens only once its edits are saved or discarded. SIGTERM ends Slate without closing windows, so it leaves journals like a crash; `check_slate.sh`'s `quit` clears them between sections.
  - Restoring opens the file as it is and applies `document_edit::replace_document` (every block replaced, one undo step), so the title shows "• " and one undo gives the file's text back; the journal's pictures are decoded into the page's textures. A journal whose file changed size or time since it was read or saved isn't put onto it ("changed after the crash": Keep for Later, Discard). The start window stays open while it still lists sessions, so each can be restored in turn.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` section 27 (24 checks; SLATE OK, 209 checks, after merging SL3's main): .txt, .md, and .docx edits, kill -9, the start window offering the three, Restore from there and from the "Restore unsaved edits?" prompt, titles "• ", files unchanged until saved, no journal after a clean close or a discard; typing every half second for 20 s, the journal first appeared after 5.2 s and was rewritten 3 times, at least 5.17 s apart. The check's AT-SPI helper gains `choose` (a button in a row or a dialog) and `count`. `scripts/check_recovery.sh` RECOVERY OK (Brick, on the moved store). Unit tests: `suite-file` (2, moved from Brick), `brick` recovery (1), `slate` journal round trip (1), `document-edit` `replacing_the_document_is_one_step`.

- **2026-10-13 SLATE-22:** Slate saves the format bar's looks in .docx (ADR 0008). A subagent built the tests; it was integrated and verified here.
  - One writer bug, found by a new test and fixed:
    - A rewritten run copies another run's `w:rPr` when their direct formatting matches.
    - Slate reads an explicit `<w:color w:val="auto"/>` as "no colour", meaning the style's. So a colour cleared in a coloured style could copy a neighbour's `w:color auto`, and LibreOffice showed black.
    - `run()` now knows the paragraph style's colour. Such a run is written without `w:color`, so the style's colour shows.
  - Already right, now proven: cleared properties are removed, not copied from the template; `jc` is written when it differs from the style's, left included; custom style ids go in verbatim; bold off is `w:b w:val="0"`.
  - Follow-up (the reader, from SLATE-19): `RunStyle::color` can't tell an explicit automatic colour from "the style's".
    - An automatic run in a coloured style shows the style's colour, where Word shows black.
    - Once such a paragraph is rewritten, it saves that way too.
    - A `Color::Auto` override would fix both.
  - Acceptance:
    - `cargo test -p docx --test save -- --include-ignored`, 12 tests passing against LibreOffice 26.8.1.1. The edits are made through document-edit on a fixture copy with a custom style Fancy (Liberation Sans 13, blue):

      - Liberation Mono, strikethrough, underline, and 20 pt on words.

      - Bold off in Heading 2.
      - A direct 16 pt cleared back to the style.
      - Alignments: left on the centred Title, centre, and right.
    - Only `word/document.xml` changes, and Slate reads every look back through `StyleSheet::look`.
    - LibreOffice's dump equals the original's line by line except exactly those looks.
    - A colour cleared in a coloured style reads as the style's in Slate and in LibreOffice.
    - `document-edit` `markdown_keeps_headings_quotes_and_emphasis_and_drops_the_rest`: headings, quotes, bold, italic, and strikethrough are written as `##`, `>`, `**`, `*`, and `~~`; font, size, colour, underline, and alignment are dropped; it reads back with the same text and kept looks.
    - `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK, 171 checks, 9 of them new in section 25. After the bar's edits on bar.docx and Ctrl+S, `document.xml` has Fancy's `pStyle`, `jc` center, `rFonts` Liberation Mono, `sz` 40, `u`, and the red colour, and the other paragraphs are as they were. - **2026-10-13 SLATE-21:** Slate has a format bar (ADR 0008). A subagent built it; it was integrated and verified here.
  - document-edit (`tests/format.rs`, 7 tests):

- **2026-10-13 SLATE-20:** Slate draws each document's own fonts and paragraph looks (ADR 0008). A subagent built it; it was integrated and verified here.
  - One look path for every format. The shaper starts each layout from the default style's font and lays each paragraph's style look over the whole paragraph, so an empty paragraph has its style's line height. Each run then adds only the overrides that differ from its style.
    - The Markdown scale tables (`HEADING_SCALE`, `TITLE_SCALE`, `style_looks`) are gone: the built-in sheet's sizes draw Markdown.
    - Code and raw text stay monospace; links, table header rows, and quotes keep their looks.
    - Fontconfig substitutes faces, for example Calibri as Carlito. Slate has no substitution table.
  - Paragraphs: space before and after come from the style's `ParaLook` at twips / 15 px, added and not collapsed, as Word does. So do the left indent and the first-line indent (Pango `set_indent`; a hanging line moves the box left).
    - Line spacing:
      - `Auto(1.0)` leaves Pango alone; a factor of 1 would be 1 px tighter.
      - Other multiples use `set_line_spacing`.
      - `Exact` and `AtLeast` use Pango's absolute line-height attribute, so `pango` now enables `v1_50` (Ubuntu 24.04 ships 1.52). Hit-testing and the caret come from Pango itself.
    - The flow's heading, title, and quote spacing constants are gone. Lists, code, tables, and rules keep theirs.
  - `flow::Look` is now a font, a size, and a weight. The flow asks the shaper for metrics once per look and estimates each box with its look's metrics and its line spacing.
  - Visual change from SL1, from screenshots of every check section against the SLATE-19 binary:
    - Markdown headings moved by a fraction of a point: the built-in sheet holds whole half-points, e.g. H3 at 15 pt instead of 14.85.
    - Word documents now show their fonts: the fixture's body in Liberation Serif, its headings in Liberation Sans.
    - Text and everything else is identical.

The cairo renderer works. NVIDIA 615.78.08; the earlier notes record a 615.71/615.78 kernel and userspace mismatch. A reboot is the likely fix. The performance numbers below were taken with `GSK_RENDERER=cairo`.

- **2026-10-13 SLATE-19:** Slate's model has style sheets and fonts (ADR 0008). A subagent built it against a fixed contract; it was integrated and verified here.
  - document-model (`styles.rs`): `Document::styles`, a `StyleSheet` of `Style`s. Each style has an id, a name, based-on, next, outline level, title, a resolved `Look` (font, size, bold, italic, underline, strike, colour), and a `ParaLook` (alignment, space before and after, `LineSpacing`, left and first-line indent, in twips).
    - `Paragraph.style` is a style id. `ParagraphStyle` is gone.
    - `RunStyle` is the run's direct formatting: `Option` for every look, plus the new `font`. `StyleSheet::look(style, run)` gives what's drawn.
    - `StyleSheet::builtin()` (Normal, Title, Heading 1-6, Quote) reproduces SL1's Markdown look, for Markdown and plain text.
  - docx: the sheet is every paragraph style in styles.xml, custom ones included, resolved through docDefaults and based-on.
    - It reads theme fonts (`theme1.xml` major and minor), `rFonts` (ascii over hAnsi, and the theme slots), `w:spacing`, `w:ind` (hanging included), `outlineLvl`, and `w:next`.
    - A run's overrides are its character style plus its direct `rPr`.
    - Writing: `pStyle` is the paragraph's id. Runs in rewritten paragraphs carry exactly their overrides (`w:val="0"` for `Some(false)`, Latin `rFonts`, `sz` and `szCs`). Unedited paragraphs stay byte for byte.
  - Consumers moved to the sheet; drawing is unchanged on purpose, and SLATE-20 draws the fonts.
    - Consumers: the flow, the shaper (sizes and colours from the resolved look), accessibility, document-edit (`set_style` by id; Enter after a heading takes the style's `next`; Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I write `Some(!all_on)`), and the clipboard (fragments carry style names, mapped by name on paste, else built-in ids, else the default).
    - Changes from SL1:
      - Heading levels come from `outlineLvl` before the "heading N" name, so a custom style based on a heading lays out as one.
      - A .docx with no size anywhere draws at Word's default 10 pt.
  - `scripts/lo_docx.py dump` now also gives each paragraph's alignment, its style's spacing, line spacing, and indents, and each run's font, size, and colour. All 7 recorded dumps were regenerated with LibreOffice 26.8.1.1, which reports start and end alignment as 5 and 6. `check_slate.sh` reads the paragraph text from the dump's last field.
  - Deviations:
    - A paragraph's own spacing and indents (direct `w:spacing` and `w:ind` on the paragraph) aren't modelled yet, only its style's. The dump reads the style's too. (SLATE-26 models them, and the dump reads effective values.)
    - An edited run's `rStyle` is written as the direct formatting it resolves to.
  - LibreOffice exceptions in the test, each narrow:
    - Word's Normal and Body Text Indent show under LibreOffice's names.
    - LibreOffice adds " (user)" to a style whose name clashes with one of its own.
    - Lengths are compared within 1 twip, since LibreOffice stores 1/100 mm.

    - `cargo test -p docx --test libreoffice`: fixture.docx and the 6 POI samples match LibreOffice's dump, 478 paragraphs and 393 runs. Each paragraph's style name (custom ones included), alignment, spacing, line spacing, and indents, and each run's font, size, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, and colour, match.
    - Live re-dumps of fixture, IllustrativeCases, and SampleDoc are byte-identical to the recordings.
    - `cargo test -p docx --test save --test blank -- --include-ignored` passes against LibreOffice. `cargo test --workspace` passes, and `grep ParagraphStyle apps/slate` is empty.
    - `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK, 118 checks: drawing is as before, with heading height, bold ink, and text widths measured.
    - Performance, `cargo test --release -p slate --test flow_perf -- --ignored`: typing p99 18.2 ms (target < 25); whole-document re-wrap max 318 ms (target < 500); visible re-wrap in one frame, max 10.4 ms; scrolling frame CPU p99 9.8 ms (target < 16). The window was 1901x2100 on Hyprland, not the test's 1600x1000, so Full was 1853 px. This isn't the reference box.
- **2026-10-12 SLATE-16:** find and replace. Ctrl+F (Edit → Find…) reveals a bar above the page (search entry, Match Case, count, previous, next, close); Ctrl+H (Edit → Replace…) adds a replace row (entry, Replace, Replace All). `document_edit::find` searches the editable blocks (top level and top-level tables' cells) in document order, case-folding by lowercase forms with byte offsets kept on the text's character boundaries; `replace_one` is a text edit in the match's first character's look, `replace_all` one blocks edit over the top-level range from the first match's block to the last's, so one undo takes it back.
  - The page clones the document on the UI thread (about 2 ms for 150,000 words) and searches the copy with `gio::spawn_blocking`; each keystroke cancels the previous search (an `AtomicBool` checked block by block) and only the newest result shows. Matches stay a sorted `Vec`; drawing a block finds its own by binary search, so showing a search costs nothing per match. A search keeps at most 100,000 matches ("100,000+").
  - Enter selects the current match when it isn't selected yet, else the next one; Shift+Enter the last before the caret, else the previous one. Replace does the same: the first press selects, the next replaces and selects the next match. Escape anywhere in the bar closes it and leaves the last match selected on the page.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test --release -p slate --test flow_perf finding -- --ignored` on the 150,000-word document: the first match shows (call to the after-paint of the frame with the highlights) in at most 16.8 ms of five words (target < 100); typing "throughout" a letter at a time stalls a 4 ms UI timer at most 6.1 ms (target < 16), the call itself at most 2.4 ms. `scripts/check_slate.sh` section 26 (built as 23; renumbered when SL3's sections 23 to 25 merged): count, Enter and Shift+Enter through a table cell, Match Case, Replace, Replace All, and one undo for Replace All (15 checks). `cargo test -p document-edit find` (5).
- **2026-10-12 SLATE-18:** new documents (user decisions: build it; .docx is the default format). Ctrl+N (File → New), and a New Document button beside Open on the window `slate` shows without a file, open an Untitled window. Its document is Slate's built-in blank Word document (`docx::blank()`, written by `scripts/blank_docx.py` byte for byte the same each run: one empty paragraph; Normal, Title, Heading 1 to 6, Quote, and Hyperlink in Word's default look, Calibri 11 pt; no page setup), read from a private copy like any Word document, so the first save patches it and every style a heading or quote needs is already there.
  - The saver's path is optional: Save on an untitled document is Save As, suggesting Untitled.docx in the Documents folder (`XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR`). Saving registers the window under its new file, so opening that file again brings the window forward.
  - `slate` without a file still shows the start window (as Brick does), now with New Document beside Open; it doesn't open a blank document by itself.

- **2026-10-12 SLATE-8:** Slate saves .docx files by patching the package they were read from (`docx::save`, `docx::read_source`, `docx::DocxSource`). Ctrl+S saves over the file; Save As takes .docx only for a Word document. The app reads each Word document from a private copy in `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/slate` (`original-PID-N.docx`, removed with the window, swept at startup when its process is gone), and each save patches that copy, so the file may move or change meanwhile.
  - The reader records a source map: each `w:p` and `w:tbl` child of the body and of every table cell, its bytes in `word/document.xml`, the blocks it made, its list instance, and its pictures' `w:drawing`/`w:pict`. Saving matches the document's blocks to the source's with `document_model::match_blocks_by` (moved out of the Markdown writer; numbered items' numbers don't count, Word numbers lists). Elements whose blocks all match, in order, are copied byte for byte with everything between them; the others are replaced by blocks written from the model.
  - Written blocks start from the paragraph they replace (or the one copied next to them): its `w:pPr` with style, numbering, and alignment set where they differ, children in schema order; a run's `w:rPr` from that paragraph's closest run, with bold, italic, underline, strike, size, and colour set where they differ. When the paragraph style changed, looks a run had from the old style are left to the new one (a paragraph made Heading 4 is bold like Heading 4). A section the replaced paragraph ended stays on the last paragraph written for it, with its page break. Pictures reuse their drawing (with a new `docPr` id) or become new media parts; tables of the same shape keep their markup and patch their cells; Markdown-only blocks become paragraphs. Missing heading/title/quote styles, list definitions (`numbering.xml` made if absent), hyperlink and image relationships, and content types are added.
  - The check reads the temp file back and compares the text line by line. The returned source's blocks are the document's where a block reads back as the same kind and text, so a re-save copies what the last one wrote.
  - Follow-ups: a rewritten paragraph loses bookmarks, comments, fields, footnote references (their marks become text), and tracked changes inside it; patching runs instead of paragraphs would keep them. A new numbered list joins the list before it of the same kind (no restart override). A run size the model leaves unset can't be written under a style that sets one.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p docx --test save` (7, plus 1 ignored):
    - every corpus document (fixture, 6 POI samples) saved unedited, and saved again from that copy, has every part byte for byte;
    - fixture.docx with typing, italic, Heading 4 (a style it lacks), Enter in a numbered item, a cell edit, and a deleted paragraph keeps every other body child verbatim and every part but `document.xml` and `styles.xml` (the new style appended); it reads back as edited;
    - a second save from the first's source changes only the paragraph typed in;
    - a mark typed into every paragraph and cell of every corpus document reads back, other parts untouched;
    - a section break stays with the half of its paragraph split last; lists added to a document without numbering create `numbering.xml`; a new link and a pasted GIF add relationships, a media part, and a content type.
  - LibreOffice: `cargo test -p docx --test save -- --ignored` reads the edited fixture exactly as the original's dump with the edits made by hand (renumbered list, Heading 4 bold, italic run, "Pears", deleted paragraph gone).
  - `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (section 21: Ctrl+S on fixture.docx changes only `word/document.xml`, parts in order, the edit reopens; killed processes' copies are swept).

- **2026-10-12 FRAME-15:** Frame does the spec's reference task in fewer input actions than PowerPoint: 15 on the canvas and 19 in the outline, against PowerPoint's 21. Measured on the task the spec names: "Quarterly review" with a subtitle; "Highlights" with Growth, Margins, and Up 3% at the second level; "Revenue" with a 400×200 PNG from a file; "Next steps" with Hire and Ship; saved as .pptx. The user chose to build both a canvas and an outline flow:
  - Canvas flow, as PowerPoint's (`deck-edit` `canvas.rs`: `next_placeholder` and `relevel`; app `editing.rs` and `slide_list.rs`):
    - A new deck opens, and a slide New Slide adds (Ctrl+M, the button, a picked layout, the list's menu) opens, with the caret in its title.
    - Ctrl+Enter goes to the end of the slide's next title, subtitle, body, or content placeholder, in shape order, skipping date, footer, slide number, and header. With nothing edited it goes to the first. After the last it adds a slide as Ctrl+M does.
    - Tab and Shift+Tab, with the caret at the start of a paragraph or a selection over several, move the paragraphs a level with that level's looks, as one step. Elsewhere they do nothing, and they never move focus off the canvas.
  - Outline: Ctrl+Enter (`OutlineKey::NewSlide`) adds an empty Title and Content slide after the caret's slide; no text moves. Insert while the outline is shown works on the caret's slide.
  - Bug fixed: after Insert the keys went to the hidden canvas, so the next Return hit a header button and opened a new window. The keys now go to the outline when it's shown (`insert::to_page`).
  - PowerPoint's count (the user's call, since PowerPoint isn't available here): derived from Microsoft's documentation, not timed by hand. Counting rules, the same for Frame:
    - Each click, key, or chord counts as one action, and each keytip key as one.
    - Typed titles, bullets, and file paths don't count.
    - The count runs from launch, so PowerPoint's Start screen counts.
  - PowerPoint's shortest documented path, 21 actions:
    - Enter (Blank presentation is focused on the Start screen); Tab to the title; Ctrl+Enter to the subtitle.
    - The New Slide arrow, Title and Content, and a click in the title (3). Microsoft documents Ctrl+M and Ctrl+Enter as keeping the title slide's layout.
    - Ctrl+Enter to the body; Enter; Enter; Alt+Shift+Right; Ctrl+Enter for a new slide.
    - Insert, Pictures, This Device, and Enter.
    - Ctrl+M, a click in the title, Ctrl+Enter, and Enter.
    - F12, then Enter. Ctrl+S on a new file opens the OneDrive-first save pane, which costs 2 or more extra actions.
  - Sources (support.microsoft.com, read 2026-10-12): [shortcuts](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/accessibility/powerpoint/use-keyboard-shortcuts-to-create-powerpoint-presentations), [basic tasks with a screen reader](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/accessibility/powerpoint/basic-tasks-to-create-a-presentation-in-powerpoint-with-a-screen-reader), [add slides](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/powerpoint/training/add-rearrange-duplicate-and-delete-slides-in-powerpoint), [insert a picture](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/powerpoint/training/insert-a-picture-in-powerpoint), [pictures with a screen reader](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/accessibility/powerpoint/use-a-screen-reader-to-insert-and-edit-pictures-and-tables-in-powerpoint), [customize the save experience](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/collab-files/customize-the-save-experience-in-office), [save with a screen reader](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/accessibility/powerpoint/use-a-screen-reader-to-save-your-presentation-in-powerpoint), [outline view](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/powerpoint/create-and-print-a-presentation-in-outline-view).
  - Other PowerPoint routes:
    - The content placeholder's picture icon: 19. Microsoft only implies that the icon opens the dialog.
    - Keytips Alt, N, P, D for the picture: 22.
    - Ctrl+S to save: 23 or more.
    - Outline view: 26, or 24 with the icon.
    - Third-party sources report that Ctrl+Enter after a title slide gives Title and Content. If so, PowerPoint takes 19, or 17 with the icon. Frame's canvas path, at 15, is below every variant.
    - A hand count in PowerPoint can replace `powerpoint=21` in `scripts/check_frame.sh`.
  - Follow-ups:
    - In the outline, Tab on a new, still empty title does nothing when the slide before has an empty body. `demote`'s body edit is a no-op, and an empty body has no outline line for the caret. The paths type the title first; PowerPoint lets Tab come first.
    - On the canvas, Tab at the start of a title placeholder also changes its level. PowerPoint inserts a tab there.
  - Acceptance:
    - `scripts/check_frame.sh` prints `FRAME OK` on this story's binary, every earlier check included. 17 (canvas) counts 15 and 18 (outline) counts 19, both below 21. LibreOffice reads each saved deck as exactly the task, with no extra paragraphs: per slide, the title and subtitle or bullets with their levels, and on slide 3 an `image/png 400x200` picture.
    - `cargo test -p deck-edit --test canvas` (5): Ctrl+Enter's order and what it skips; Tab with looks per level, over several paragraphs as one step, and at the limits.
    - `cargo test -p deck-edit --test outline` adds `ctrl_enter_starts_an_empty_slide_after_the_carets`.
- **2026-10-12 SLATE-7:** Slate saves .txt and .md files. Ctrl+S (File → Save) saves over the file, and Ctrl+Shift+S (File → Save As…) saves as .md or .txt by the extension chosen. The bytes are made and written off the UI thread through `suite_file::save_atomic` (temp file, fsync, verify by re-reading, rename); editing goes on meanwhile, and a save asked for during one waits for it.
  - Writers in `document-model` (a subagent built them against a fixed contract; integrated here): `encode`, `write_text`, `write_markdown`, `Unencodable`, `MarkdownSource`, `read_markdown_source`.
    - Plain text is written back in its encoding (UTF-8 with or without BOM, UTF-16 LE/BE, Windows-1252), line ending, and final newline. Text the encoding can't hold is refused with an alert naming the character; Save As then writes UTF-8.
    - Markdown keeps a source map: each top-level block's line start in the file read, and the blocks as shown (pictures loaded, runs merged). At save, the current blocks are matched to those by common prefix and suffix, then the LCS of equal blocks. Matched blocks are copied byte for byte with the text up to the next block, and only the rest is written fresh. No edit tracking is needed, and undoing back to the original saves the original bytes.
    - A matched block is still written fresh where its source would read otherwise in its new place: a numbered item joining a list, a changed list level, or a reference definition that was deleted. A moved picture reuses its source, keeping its URL.
    - What Markdown can't hold is dropped (font sizes, colours, underline, empty paragraphs), listed in `write/markdown.rs`. Whitespace at the edge of a bold or italic stretch reads back outside it (CommonMark's flanking rules).
  - `document-edit` gains `History::position`: an id for the document the history is at. A new edit gives a new id, typing joined to a step included; undo and redo give a position's id back; dropping steps past the history limit moves a base id. The window marks its title "• " while the position differs from the one saved. Closing a window, or Quit (which now closes each window), asks to save, discard, or cancel then. Word documents (SLATE-8) are offered Save As instead of Save.
  - Ctrl+S and Ctrl+Shift+S are handled by the page's key handler, like Edit and Format; the menu only shows them. The app accelerator `<Control><Shift>s` didn't fire for the shifted key wtype sends (Frame matches the key itself too).
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p document-model` `tests/write.rs` (17):
    - every text fixture writes back byte for byte (encodings, CRLF, CR, no final newline, BOM only, empty);
    - every Markdown fixture writes back unchanged and, written fresh, reads back the same blocks;
    - one edited paragraph, item, heading, cell, or code block changes only its lines;
    - deleted, inserted, and moved blocks read back; a CRLF file gets CRLF in fresh blocks; Windows-1252 refuses "仮";
    - inline looks read back;
    - 3,000 seeded random edit sets over fixture.md, its CRLF form, and a list-heavy document read back wherever the fresh writer does, with re-saves through the returned source byte-stable.
  - `cargo test -p document-edit` `tests/position.rs` (3): undo and redo return to positions, joined typing is a new position, and undoing past the history limit isn't the opened document.
  - `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (103 checks, 16 new, section 20):
    - a Windows-1252 CRLF file without a final newline, edited and saved, is `b'caf\xe9 one\r\nsecond lineX'`, replaced by a rename (new inode, no temp file), and its title goes from "• w.txt" to "w.txt";
    - "仮" is refused with the alert "Windows-1252 has no “仮”…" and the file stays the same;
    - Save As as.md writes UTF-8 Markdown `b'caf\xc3\xa9 one\r\n\r\nsecond lineX\xe4\xbb\xae'` and retitles the window;
    - a UTF-16 LE file saves as `b'\xff\xfeh\x00i\x00!\x00\n\x00'`;
    - in a Markdown file with a setext heading, `__bold__`, `* ` and `1)` lists, editing one paragraph changes exactly its line, and undoing saves the original bytes;
    - Ctrl+W with changes asks "Save changes to “m.md” before closing?", and Enter saves and closes.
  - Perf unchanged: typing p99 17.9 ms, whole re-wrap max 283 ms, scroll p99 9.3 ms.
  - Found:
    - The check's image has no diffutils (`diff`, `cmp`), so comparisons use Python.
    - fsync on docker's overlay filesystem can take seconds, so the check waits for the title's mark to clear rather than sleeping.
    - Inserting File → Save and Save As shifted the F10 menu walk in `text_width`.
  - Follow-ups:
    - A document saved in another format keeps showing as it was read (a .txt saved as .md stays plain) until reopened.
    - Save As over a file open in another Slate window leaves that window showing the old text.
- **2026-10-12 FRAME-13:** Frame has an outline view. `deck-edit` (`outline.rs`): `outline(deck)` projects each slide to its title (the title placeholder's first paragraph), its body placeholder's paragraphs (body or subtitle) with their levels, and a count of its other items (own shapes but empty placeholders and footers); `typing`, `erase`, and `key` (Enter, Tab, Shift+Tab, Backspace, Delete) turn outline edits into the canvas's commands plus `BodyEdit` (a shape's paragraphs replaced), built step by step on a copy of the deck when one key makes several (`Edit::Steps`, whose change is the slide list's when any step's is), and return where the caret goes. New slides take Title and Content; moved text takes its new place's looks per level (`rebased`, shared with layout changes) and keeps its own; Tab on a title only joins a slide holding nothing else. `pptx::save` writes a paragraph's `lvl` when it isn't what its markup says. The window (`src/outline.rs`): an Outline toggle in the header bar (Ctrl+Shift+O) puts a `GtkTextView` in the canvas's place, one buffer line per outline line (titles numbered and bold, bullets indented by level, counts dim and not editable); its insert-text and delete-range handlers stop GTK's edit and apply the outline's instead (input methods keep working), keys with a meaning of their own go to `key`, and the buffer re-renders the changed lines after every change from anywhere; moving the cursor into a slide shows it in the slide list. One undo history with the canvas.
  - Deviations: a title shows its first paragraph only; a slide's second body placeholder (Two Content's right side) counts as another item; pasting several lines into the outline types them as lines with Enter between; Tab on a title doesn't join a slide that holds other items (nothing happens).
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p deck-edit --test outline` (5): the projection with titles, levelled bullets, and counts; typing an outline after slide 1 (Enter, Highlights, Enter, Growth, Tab, Enter, Margins, Enter, Tab, Up 3%) makes one Title and Content slide with first-level Growth and Margins at 32 pt and Up 3% a level down at 28 pt, Shift+Tab twice makes Up 3% a slide's title, twenty undos give the deck as read; Shift+Tab on a first-level bullet takes the bullets after it to the new slide; a slide holding other items keeps them through title typing and Enter and doesn't join the slide before, count lines don't take typing; Backspace joins bullets and drops an empty slide, a selection across a title and its bullets doesn't delete. `cargo test -p pptx --test save -- --include-ignored` (11): `outline_edits_save_and_read_back` (both decks read back as edited, outlines equal) and `libreoffice_reads_the_outline_edits` (LibreOffice reads the new slide's paragraphs as `0 Highlights, 0 Growth, 0 Margins, 1 Up 3%`, the slide made from Content with `1 Level 2` under it, slide 1 and every later slide as before; fixture slide 3 with its title "Shapes!" and every other line of it as before, and the new slide "Next"). `scripts/check_frame.sh` FRAME OK (186 checks, 33 new, section 15): the Outline button, typing the same outline by keyboard (11 slides, slide 2 Title and Content holding the four lines), Shift+Tab twice and two Ctrl+Z, canvas typing showing in the outline and undone from it, Home past the slide number, Ctrl+S writing the new slide with `lvl="1"`, a slide with shapes showing its count.
- **2026-10-12 FRAME-12:** Frame inserts.
