- **2026-10-12 SLATE-15:** Slate's page speaks to screen readers through `GtkAccessibleText` on GTK 4.14. The text, caret, selection, and text at an offset by character, word, sentence, line, and paragraph came with SLATE-11 to SLATE-13. Added here:
  - Run attributes: the default attributes come from the page's font, and an offset's attribute run comes from the leaf's Pango layout as drawn. Its attribute iterator gives the run's range in characters and the merged font (family, size in points, weight, style) plus underline, strikethrough, and foreground colour, so what is heard is what is drawn, heading and Markdown looks included.
  - Insert and delete events naming exactly the text each edit, undo, or redo changed. `document-edit` gains `Edit::span` and `History::next_span` (a subagent added these against a fixed contract): the blocks an edit or the next undo or redo will replace, computed by the same code as the `Change` it returns. Before applying, the page keeps those blocks' accessible text (a cell's: its whole table's; new `Flow::block_chars`). Afterwards it diffs that text against the new text by common prefix and suffix, then emits `update_contents(Remove)` and `update_contents(Insert)`. GTK's bridge reads a removal's text during the call, so `contents` serves the removed text for that range meanwhile. Typing one character announces exactly that character, not the paragraph.
  - Recorded in `docs/specs/slate.md`: what needs GTK above 4.14 (Ubuntu 24.04's). Character and range extents and the offset at a point need GTK 4.16 (gtk4-rs `v4_16`); screen readers setting the caret or selection need GTK 4.22 (`v4_22`). Both stay out while the suite's minimum is 4.14 (ADR 0002, ADR 0007).
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (87 checks, 13 new, section 19, through Atspi on a11y.md "Plain **bold** *italic* text" / "Second paragraph line"). The count is 44; the caret is at 44 after Ctrl+End; the character at 6 is `b`, the word at 7 is `bold ` (6-11), the line at 30 is `Second paragraph line` (23-44), and the paragraph at 3 is `Plain bold italic text\n` (0-23). The attribute runs are: at 7, 6-10 weight 700; at 12, 11-17 style italic, weight 400; at 1, 0-6 weight 400, style normal. An Atspi listener receives `text-changed:insert 44 1 "X"`, then `text-changed:delete 44 1 "X"` for Backspace, `insert 44 1 "\n"` for Enter, and `delete 44 1 "\n"` for its undo. `document-edit` `tests/span.rs`: across 21 mixed applies and every undo and redo, the span taken beforehand equals the returned Change's old range. Perf unchanged (typing p99 17.4 ms, whole re-wrap max 282 ms, scroll p99 10.1 ms).
- **2026-10-12 SLATE-14:** Slate selects and uses the clipboard across paragraphs and tables. Shift with every caret movement extends the selection from its anchor. Shift+click extends it. Presses counted against GTK's double-click time and distance make a double click select a word (Pango word boundaries) and a triple click a paragraph. Ctrl+A selects everything. The page keeps the anchor as made, and the flow holds the selection with any end that runs into a table (from outside, or from another cell) moved to that table's edge, so the highlight shows what cut and copy take; going back into the anchor's cell shrinks it again. `document-edit` gains `Fragment` (the clipboard's unit, in the same paste semantics as text edits), `copy`, `delete`, and `paste` (a subagent built these against a fixed contract; integrated here). A range whose ends are in different containers works on the top-level blocks: the whole table goes, the partial paragraphs keep their blocks, and one `BlocksEdit` holds only the touched blocks before and after. Ctrl+X/C/V (also Shift+Delete, Ctrl+Insert, Shift+Insert, and the Edit menu's new Cut, Copy, Paste, Select All) put a union on the clipboard: `application/x-slate-fragment` (JSON) for Slate windows and plain text for other apps (a table's cells one a line). Paste reads Slate's format first, else text, which pastes as paragraphs in the caret's look. Typing or Enter over a selection that runs into a table replaces or deletes it whole. Cut and paste are one undo step each.
  - New dependencies: `serde` (derive, rc) and `serde_json`, for the clipboard's rich format. `document-model`'s block types derive `Serialize` and `Deserialize`. Pictures serialize their bytes as a JSON array.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p document-edit` (`tests/clipboard.rs`, 16): copy within a paragraph, across paragraphs and list items, through a table, from a cell to the paragraph after its table, and between two cells, with their plain text and a JSON round trip; delete of each as one step with exact undo and redo; paste mid-paragraph (looks kept, tail joined), at the top level with a table, into a cell (the table flattened), and over a selection; plain text in three lines gives three paragraphs; cut, then paste elsewhere, then undoing twice gives back the original. `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (74 checks, 18 new, section 18, read through `wl-paste` and AT-SPI). In sel.md: Shift+End copies `Alpha beta gamma`; Shift+Down adds `Before`; another Shift+Down into the table copies the whole table (`…|Before|a|b|c|d`); Shift+Ctrl+End copies to `After`. A double click on "beta" copies `beta` and a triple click the paragraph. Ctrl+X of the first line leaves it empty, and undo and redo work. Ctrl+A, Ctrl+X empties the page, Ctrl+V brings everything back, table included, and undo empties it again. In a second Slate process, a copy of bold text offers `application/x-slate-fragment` and `text/plain` (plain text `HHHHHHHH`), and pasted into target.md it stays bold (216% of the plain line's ink). Text from `wl-copy` pastes as paragraphs, and undo takes it back. Perf unchanged: typing p99 18.7 ms, re-wrapped frame max 10.2 ms, whole document max 296 ms, scroll p99 10.1 ms.
  - Found: the check's AT-SPI helper read only the first Slate process; `a11y page NAME` now picks the page of the document named NAME. Word segmentation in screenshots needs a gap over 6 px (letters sit up to 4 px apart). `dnd_helpers.py click X Y N` makes N clicks in a row. Docker containers from `archlinux:latest` are shared with the Frame agent's checks: one of this story's runs died midway, killed from outside. Earlier I had killed every `archlinux:latest` container to clear a cancelled run of mine, which may have killed a Frame check too. Never kill containers by image.
- **2026-10-12 FRAME-11:** Frame has an inspector. Model: `Source::Edit` (a look set by an edit, which a save writes) with `Source::is_own`; where each look is set now also for paragraphs (`Paragraph::sources`: alignment, bullet), shapes (`Shape::sources`: fill, outline), and backgrounds (`Slide::background_source`), read by `pptx`; `Shape::slot` (`<p:ph type idx>`) and `TextBody::levels` (each level's looks from the body's list styles). `deck-edit`: `LookEdit::text` (font, size, bold, italic, underline, colour over a range; runs split at its ends, fields kept whole; a whole paragraph's mark too) and `LookEdit::paragraphs` (alignment; bullets on as a "•" in Arial hanging in `BULLET_MARGIN` when the paragraph had no margin, or off), `ShapeLookEdit` (fill, outline; not tables), `BackgroundEdit`, and `LayoutEdit` (each new placeholder takes the slide's placeholder text by slot, then type, then family; moved text takes the new placeholder's looks per level but keeps its own; empty placeholders nobody took go, the layout's new ones come with fresh ids; `Change::Look`, `Change::Slide`). `pptx::save` writes `Source::Edit` run looks into `rPr`/`endParaRPr` and paragraph looks into `pPr` (bullets with `marL`/`indent`) in schema order from the raw elements; changed fills (none or solid) and outlines (`a:ln` with width, colour, `prstDash`, other line children kept) into `spPr`; a background as `p:bg`; a layout change by retargeting the slide's layout relationship, rewriting changed `p:ph`, removing dropped shapes, and inserting new placeholders; and its read-back check compares what it wrote. The window (`src/inspector.rs`, a sidebar right of the canvas) rebuilds its rows from the selection at idle without taking focus: Slide (Layout list with thumbnails, Background), Text (Font, Size, Bold, Italic, Underline, Colour, alignment, Bullets), Shape (Fill, Outline colour and width, Geometry; a picture's format and pixel size; a table's rows and columns), inherited values captioned "From the layout/master/presentation"; colours from a popover of 20 swatches; each change is `seal` + `apply`, one undo step, the text selection kept.
  - Deviations: with only a caret, text looks apply to the whole text (PowerPoint applies them to the word); the slide rows have no Hidden switch; table cells and geometry are read-only; the palette has no theme colours, and fills and outlines the inspector sets are solid colours; after a layout change, paragraph margins and spacing set on the moved text show the new layout's until the deck is reopened (the file keeps them); moved placeholders take the new layout's box, written as their own.

- **2026-10-12 SLATE-13:** Slate shows input-method composition. The page answers the input method context's signals. A changed preedit (its text, looks, and cursor) is laid out inside the caret's paragraph: the Pango shaper splices it into that leaf's layout at the caret, underlined, outside the layout cache, and the new `Flow::remeasure` measures the leaf again (down through a table to the cell's node), so the paragraph grows and later text moves, while the document stays as it was. The caret shows at the preedit's cursor. Commit clears the preedit and types the text as an edit. Retrieve-surrounding gives the caret's paragraph, the caret's byte offset, and the selection's other end when it is in that paragraph. Delete-surrounding becomes an undoable edit in the paragraph. Moving the caret or clicking resets the context, ending the composition. An edit made while composing (a delete-surrounding) shows the preedit again in the rebuilt leaf.
  - The input method in the check: Arch's GTK 4 has only the Wayland input-method module (no IBus module), so the check's input method speaks Wayland. `scripts/ime_helpers.py` is a `zwp_input_method_v2` client on sway's seat (the protocol XML is vendored from wlroots, MIT, in `scripts/protocols/`). It takes commands from a FIFO (preedit, commit, read surrounding text, delete), so composition is exact, with no dictionary or candidate choice, and it reaches the window through sway's relay to the text input (`zwp_text_input_v3`) as a real input method does.
  - Found: sway relays only to a text input that existed when its window got keyboard focus, and in the headless check the seat has a keyboard only while a `wtype` runs. Section 17 keeps a `wtype` asleep for its duration, starts the input method, then opens a second window and focuses the document's window again. Without the focus change the text input never gets `enter`, and GTK never enables it.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (56 checks, 6 new, section 17, ime.md "Hello world", caret at the end): preedit かな draws 118 dark px right of the text with a 30 px continuous underline, and the screenshot shows the caret after it; while composing, the accessible text is still `Hello world`; commit gives `Hello world仮名`; the input method reads `Hello world仮名|17|17` (text, caret, anchor in bytes); deleting 3 bytes before the caret gives `Hello world仮`. `apps/slate/crates/flow/tests/flow.rs` `a_leaf_measured_again_takes_its_new_height_in_place`: a paragraph and a cell's text laid out two lines taller, then remeasured, grow the document by exactly four lines and move only what is below them. Perf unchanged (load average 7): typing p99 18.8 ms, re-wrapped frame max 10.4 ms, whole document max 291 ms, scroll p99 12.5 ms.
- **2026-10-12 FRAME-10:** Frame moves, resizes, and nudges shapes. `deck_edit::FrameEdit` (a shape's box from and to; `Change::Shape`) is the undoable step; `deck_edit::snap(deck, slide, moving, frame, grab, tolerance)` snaps a dragged box (`Grab::Move`, or `Grab::Resize` with the edges held) to the nearest line within the tolerance on each axis: the slide's edges and centre, the layout's placeholders' edges and centres, the slide's other shapes' edges and centres, and, when moving, equal spacing (after, before, or centred between two boxes that overlap it across the axis); it returns the box and its guides (a line along each matched edge or centre spanning both boxes, and both gaps for equal spacing). The canvas (`src/arrange.rs`) drags with a `GestureDrag`: a handle of the selected shape resizes it, the border of the shape whose text is edited moves it, anywhere else on a movable shape (the slide's own, with an id, not grouped: `deck_edit::movable`, model `Shape::grouped`) moves it; past 3 px the drag claims the press, so clicks now act on release and a drag never moves the caret. The preview draws the slide with the dragged box and magenta guides, each on whole pixels; the tolerance is 6 screen pixels in EMU; Alt drags freely; the arrows nudge the selected shape one screen pixel, Shift ten. A click on a shape without text selects it (blue outline, eight handles); Escape or a click on empty space lets go; Escape while editing text leaves its shape selected; typing into a selected shape with a text body adds to its end. `pptx::save` writes moved and resized shapes' `a:off`/`a:ext` (a `graphicFrame`'s `p:xfrm`), adding `<a:xfrm>` first in `spPr` for a placeholder that took its box from its layout, and its read-back check compares the boxes it wrote.
  - Deviations: turned shapes move but don't resize (handles would resize along the slide's axes), and snapping aligns their unturned boxes; shapes in groups and SmartArt drawing shapes don't move; resizing doesn't keep the aspect ratio with Shift.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p deck-edit --test frames` (6): a box snaps to the slide's centre (guide the slide's full height) and edges, not past 6 px of tolerance (no guides); to another shape's edge and centre; to the layout body placeholder's bottom; to equal spacing after, before, and centred between two boxes, with both gap guides; a resize moves only the held edge onto the slide's centre or edge; moves and resizes undo and redo, and layout shapes don't move. `cargo test -p pptx --test save -- --include-ignored` (4) now also moves slide 1's title (no `xfrm` of its own) 1 M right and narrows the picture slide's picture by 1 M: LibreOffice's dump of the saved deck has the title 1 M right and the picture 1 M narrower (within 720 EMU) and every other line as before; slide 9's part is the only other part that changes. `scripts/check_frame.sh` FRAME OK (106 checks, 8 new): on the fixture's slide 3, dragging the blue rectangle so its centre is 3 px right of the slide's, mid-drag the column at the slide's middle has 550 guide pixels (none before), and it lands centred (0 px off); Ctrl+Z puts it back; with Alt held it lands 2 px off; Shift+Right and Right nudge it 11 px; Ctrl+Z undoes the last (10 px).
- **2026-10-12 SLATE-12:** Slate edits inside table cells. `document-edit` gains `Container` (the document, or a cell of a top-level table), and a `Pos` carries its container (built by a subagent against a fixed contract, integrated here). `Pos` orders in document order, so a cell position sits between the paragraphs around its table. Every edit acts on its container's block list and records it, so undo and redo act on the same cell. A `Change` names its container, and the page splices only the cell's table into the flow. Edits never cross a cell's edge: a replace or toggle across containers does nothing, and Backspace or Delete at a cell's edge does not join. `flow` gains `leaf_at(path)` and a leaf-based `text_place` that walks into cells with paint's geometry; the top-level-only `block_leaf` and block-based `text_place` are gone. The caret (`view/caret.rs`) now walks the flow's leaves in document order, so Left, Right, and word moves cross from cell to cell and into and out of tables. Up and Down hit-test their way to the next line above or below, wherever it is, keeping their column. Tab in a cell goes to the next cell, past the last cell out of the table, and Shift+Tab goes back.
  - Decision: tables nested inside a cell (DOCX only; Markdown can't nest them) aren't editable, and the caret skips them. A `Pos` names one level of table, and nesting would make it a path.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p document-edit` (20 tests, 7 new): typing and Backspace in a cell edit only that cell; Enter in a cell adds a paragraph there and keeps the table 2x2; edits don't cross out of a cell; positions order as in the document; 13 steps mixing cell and top-level edits (renumbering a list in a cell, a top-level delete across the whole table) each undo and redo to the exact document; 6 steps on `fixture.docx`'s table do the same. `apps/slate/crates/flow/tests/flow.rs` `a_cells_growth_relays_out_only_its_table`: a cell grows by two lines; exactly the table's five cell paragraphs are shaped again, the paragraph before the table keeps its place, the one after moves down by exactly two lines, and `text_place` matches where `paint` draws every leaf, before and after. `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (50 checks, 4 new, section 16, read through AT-SPI) on table.md (Before / a b / c d / After): End and Down into a, Tab to b and c, Right twice into d, Tab out to After, Up into c, Shift+Tab back to b, Backspace, a no-op Backspace at the cell's start, and Enter, each followed by a typed digit, give `Before|a1|8|92b|63c|4d|5After`. Undo to the end gives the opened text and redo to the end the edited one. The screenshot shows the grown cell making its row taller. Perf after the change (spike's box, load average 5.5): typing p99 18.4 ms; flow re-wrapped frame max 10.9 ms, whole document max 323 ms, scroll p99 13.7 ms.
- **2026-10-12 SLATE-6:** Slate edits in place. New toolkit-free crate `apps/slate/crates/document-edit` (built by a subagent against a fixed contract, reviewed and integrated here). A text edit replaces a stretch of the document and holds the removed and the inserted fragment, never a snapshot; a cross-block removal keeps the first block's looks and brings whole blocks between (tables included) back on undo. A looks edit holds each changed block's looks before and after. Ordered lists are renumbered as part of the step, so undo restores labels exactly. `History` keeps 1,000 steps and joins typing a word at a time. `normalize` merges runs at open (`Paragraph::merge_runs`, new in document-model) so undo gives paragraphs back exactly. `flow` gains `Flow::edit`, which rebuilds only the spliced top-level blocks (leaf numbers and paths shift, a list item's spacing follows its new neighbour), keeps the scroll anchor, and has the shaper forget the replaced leaves' layouts: the shaper's cache is now keyed by a stable `LayoutKey`, not `LeafId`. The page (`view::Page`, `view/caret.rs`) has a caret in model positions, a `GtkIMMulticontext` for typing, the keys below, and the menu's new Edit and Format sections (after View, so the Text Width path is unchanged).
  - Keys (user-visible choices, recorded in `docs/specs/slate.md`): Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I toggle over the selection, or for the next typed text at the caret; Ctrl+0 normal text and Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+6 headings; Ctrl+Shift+8 bullets and Ctrl+Shift+7 numbers (as in Google Docs); Tab and Shift+Tab nest list items; Shift+Enter breaks the line; Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z, and Ctrl+Y. A paragraph command given at the caret leaves the caret where it was (the edit itself would select the changed blocks). Plain text has no formatting. Page Up and Page Down scroll without moving the caret; Space now types.
  - Scope: the caret and edits stay in top-level paragraphs, list items, and code blocks; clicks in a table or on a picture put the caret in the next editable block. Tables are SLATE-12, Shift-selection SLATE-14, preedit SLATE-13, and text-change events for screen readers SLATE-15 (the caret position is reported now). There is no save yet (SLATE-7, SLATE-8), so closing a window drops its edits without asking.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p document-edit` (13 tests: typing looks at run boundaries, a cross-paragraph replace, Enter after a heading, in a numbered list with renumbering, and on an empty item, Backspace at an item's and a paragraph's start, Delete joining, bold and italic set and clear, styles, lists both ways, indenting, a 10-plus-step sequence on `fixture.md` where every undo equals the document before its step and every redo the one after, and typing "hello world" as 2 undo steps); `apps/slate/crates/flow/tests/flow.rs` `an_edit_rebuilds_only_its_blocks_and_keeps_the_viewport` (typing far above the viewport reshapes one block and moves nothing on screen; Enter and a join renumber leaves and paths past a table; the result equals a flow built from the edited document). `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (46 checks, 10 new). Section 14 types through `wtype` into the input method and reads the page through AT-SPI: " More", Enter, "Second" give `Title|First paragraph. More|Second`; 7 Backspaces give `Title|First paragraph. More`; undo to the end gives the opened text; redo to the end gives the edits back; Home, End, Ctrl+Left, Left, Right, Ctrl+Right, Up, and Down with typed marks give `Title!|[First paragraph._ |M^ore~]?`. Section 15 works in screenshots: Ctrl+1 draws the line 220% as tall, Ctrl+Shift+8 moves the text 28 px right behind a bullet, and Ctrl+B over a dragged selection gives 216% ink; three Ctrl+Z give back the unformatted lines exactly and three Ctrl+Shift+Z the formatted ones. Typing latency: `apps/slate/tests/flow_perf.rs` `typing_in_a_150000_word_document_shows_each_key_in_time` runs `slate FILE --bench-typing` (new hidden flag), which types 100 characters 80–150 ms apart mid-document through the input method's path and times each to the after-paint of the frame showing it (not compositor input delivery). On the spike's box at 1600x1000: p50 3.7 ms, p99 18.5 ms (target < 25), frame CPU p99 4.6 ms. SLATE-11's flow test still passes: re-wrapped frame max 9.6 ms, whole document p50 279 / max 289 ms, scroll p99 10.3 ms. An A/B against the SLATE-11 binary (three alternating runs each) shows no regression.
  - Found: the flow test's one-frame check takes the worst of 10 frames, so desktop load can push a single frame over 16.7 ms (twice here, 16.9 and 18.1 ms, with load average 5–6; the same runs' medians were 8.3 ms, and the SLATE-11 binary showed the same kind of outliers). Two worktrees sharing one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` overwrite each other's release artifacts for local crates (their hashes use workspace-relative paths); keep target directories separate. In `check_slate.sh`, a `wtype` that sends a key straight away can lose that first key while the window takes the new virtual keyboard's keymap (seen as a missing space, Backspaces, and the file dialog path's `/`). Every key now goes through `keys`, which pauses first, and repeated keys go in one `wtype`.
  - Follow-up: Bricks (`commands::UndoStack`), Frame (`deck-edit::History`), and Slate (`document-edit::History`) each keep their own undo history. Per the suite rule, that's a candidate for a shared `suite-*` crate; not done here, since it would touch the other apps.
- **2026-10-12 FRAME-8:** Frame saves `.pptx` by patching the package it was read from (ADR 0006, as Bricks' ADR 0010 does for XLSX). At open the package is copied to `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/frame` (`pptx::Original`, removed with the deck) and read from the copy; every save diffs the deck against the deck as read and patches that copy, so saving twice, or undoing past a save and saving again, finds every part it was read with. `pptx::save` copies untouched parts as compressed bytes; writes an edited shape's paragraphs from the model into its slide part (found by `cNvPr id`, the new `Shape::id`), each run and paragraph with the markup it was read with (`Run::markup`, `Paragraph::markup`: raw `rPr`/`pPr`/`endParaRPr` and field start tags, sliced from the part by the XML tree's new byte spans), a typed run's paragraph-mark properties written as `rPr`; gives a new slide the part PowerPoint writes for one on its layout (`blank_slide`, shared with the reader, now a complete part) and a duplicate a copy of its source's part, relationships, and notes; drops a deleted slide's part, relationships, and notes; and rewrites `presentation.xml`'s `sldIdLst` (new ids above the highest), its `p14:sectionLst` (sections stay contiguous: the longest run of slides still in section order keeps its sections, the rest join the section before), its relationships, and `[Content_Types].xml`. It goes through `suite_file::save_atomic` (moved there from Bricks' `file-format`, now that two apps save; temp files end `.cronkite-save`) and reads the written file back (slide count, every written shape's text). In the window: Ctrl+S and a header Save button, Ctrl+Shift+S through the file dialog (the window takes the new file's name); the save runs off the UI thread with the window insensitive; a failed save alerts and leaves the file.
  - Also: slide-number fields renumber when slides are added, deleted, or moved (`Run::slide_number`, `Presentation::number_slides`; FRAME-7's deviation); merged runs must be written the same (`Paragraph::merge_runs` compares markup, so `lang` differences keep runs apart); SmartArt drawing shapes, which a save can't write, aren't editable (FRAME-6's deviation).
  - Deviations: `docProps/app.xml` keeps the slide count and titles it had; a custom show or a slide-to-slide hyperlink that names a deleted slide still names it; a duplicate shares its source's charts, embedded objects, and comments parts; in an edited paragraph, content the model doesn't read (math, `mc:AlternateContent`) is lost; a `.pptm` saved as `.pptx` keeps its macros' content type; closing with unsaved edits doesn't ask.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p pptx --test save -- --include-ignored` (4): `edits_save_and_everything_else_is_copied` (layouts.pptx: typing in slide 1's subtitle with a new paragraph, a new slide with typed title and bullets, slide 10 duplicated with its notes part and typed into, Two Content deleted, the picture slide moved third: the saved deck reads back as the deck, slide by slide (layout, notes, each shape's paragraphs: text, looks, sources, bullets); every part but `[Content_Types].xml`, `presentation.xml` and its relationships, and slide 1 is byte for byte as it was; slide 4 and its relationships are gone; exactly the new slides, their relationships, and the copied notes are new; a second save from the original with one more edit differs from the first only in that slide), `sections_keep_their_slides`, `an_unedited_deck_saves_as_it_was`, and the ignored `libreoffice_reads_the_saved_deck_as_the_original_plus_the_edits` (`scripts/lo_pptx.py dump` of the original and the saved deck: every slide read from the file dumps as LibreOffice dumped it before, slide 1 but its subtitle's paragraphs, which read "subtitle here" and "second line" at 32 pt 8B8B8B Calibri; the new slide reads "Agenda", "Point one", "Point two"; the copy reads as slide 10 but its title "Headers & Footers (copy)"). `scripts/check_frame.sh` prints `FRAME OK` (98 checks, 11 new, section 11): on a copy of layouts.pptx, typing in the subtitle, Ctrl+M and a typed title, Ctrl+S: "Saved “save.pptx”"; the file's slide 1 part holds "subtitle saved", `presentation.xml` lists 11 slides, `slide11.xml` holds "Agenda"; reopened: "Slide 1 of 11", "subtitle saved", slide 2 "Slide 2, Title and Content" with "Agenda"; Ctrl+Shift+S and a typed path: "Saved “saved-as.pptx”", the window titled saved-as.pptx, and that file reopens with 11 slides.
- **2026-10-12 GRID-6:** empty cells fill the window past the data on every sheet, as in Excel and Calc (user decisions: fill the window and grow as you go, not a fixed 1,048,576 x 16,384 sheet; every sheet, not just new ones). Before, a blank sheet was one cell: the data plus SAVE-2's one editable edge row and column.
  - `GridView::reach` is what the grid draws, scrolls, and moves within: `bounds` (data plus edge), then enough rows and columns to fill the view and one more screen past where it is scrolled, and to reach the cursor and the selection's far corner. Every scroll step reconfigures the adjustments, so scrolling or arrowing to the end adds a screen and scrolling back takes it away. Rows past the data wait for indexing. `reach_limit` caps the empty cells at Excel's sheet (1,048,576 rows, XFD), or the data's size where bigger: a CSV taller than Excel keeps just its edge row, and a name-box reference can't make a trillion-row file.
  - `bounds` keeps its meaning: whole rows, whole columns (Shift/Ctrl+Space, header clicks), and select-all cover the data and its edge; their highlight runs to the window's edge. Header clicks and the name box's `Z:Z` / `500:510` may land past the data. End keeps the row; Ctrl+End still goes to the last cell with data (A1 on a blank sheet).
  - Writing past the data: `CsvTable::paste` (and `paste_styles`) no longer clamp the first row to the edge row: typing or pasting at E40 of a 3-row file adds the rows up to it in the same undo step. Empty text past the data, or right of a row's end, changes nothing (before, F2+Enter on an empty edge-column cell stored "" and widened the row on save). Formatting cells or whole rows past the data adds the rows they need, worked out on a snapshot that has them, in one undo step with the styles. Insert Rows/Columns with the selection wholly past the data does nothing (it used to append blank lines at the edge row). Delete with the cursor past the data focuses a cleared row (it could index past the row map).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/commands/tests/past_data.rs` (E40 on a 3-row CSV saves 36 empty rows then `,,,,x`, one undo restores the file; a blank sheet grows to a cell typed at C5); `grid_view::tests::empty_cells_reach_a_screen_past_the_view_and_the_cursor`; `scripts/check_reach.sh` REACH OK (blank sheet: the cursor moves to M31; E40 makes 39 rows, Ctrl+Z gives 2 back, Ctrl+End B3, Ctrl+S 40 lines; 20 Page Downs reach A442; 200 wheel notches then a click reach E613; Ctrl+B on C45 adds rows and bolds, one Ctrl+Z takes back both; Delete from A60 up into the data adds no rows; no panic). Negative control: the pre-story binary fails 11 of its 15 checks (the blank sheet stays on A1; the cursor stops at row 4).
  - Scroll timing unchanged (`scroll_perf`, this desktop, software rendered as in the SLATE-5 caveat): 1 GB frame CPU p99 12.2 ms, 59.9 fps, end row 9,548,401; main before this story 12.5 ms, 59.5 fps, same end row.
  - Follow-ups: a row height set past the data is dropped at the next edit (`sizing` only; it has no row identity until the row exists); a type override on a column past every filter and header column is lost on reread (true of the edge column before); Find Next from below the data checks the last row before wrapping; a column header click while scrolled into the empty rows puts the cursor on the edge row.
- **2026-10-12 FRAME-7:** Frame adds, duplicates, deletes, and moves slides. The model lists the deck's layouts (`Presentation::layouts`: name, type, part, master, and `slide`, the new slide the layout makes) and each slide's layout and part (`Slide::layout`, `Slide::part`); `pptx` reads layouts master by master in `presentation.xml`'s order and makes each new slide by reading the slide XML PowerPoint writes for one (an empty placeholder per layout placeholder but date, footer, and slide number) through the same slide reader, so new slides inherit frames, text styles, bullets, backgrounds, and the layout's and master's shapes exactly as read slides do; a layout that can't be read is left out. `deck-edit` gains `SlideEdit` (insert, remove, move; each holds the slide it puts in or takes out) and `next_layout`, Ctrl+M's rule: the current slide's layout, or after a `title` slide its own master's `obj` layout (`tx` in older and LibreOffice decks). `Change` is now `Text { shape, caret }` or `Slides { show }`. The window (new `slide_list.rs`): New Slide button (Ctrl+M) with a menu of the layouts as thumbnails (empty placeholders outlined) and names; Ctrl+D; Delete or Backspace in the list; Ctrl+Up and Ctrl+Down; dragging a thumbnail (a `DragSource` per row, a `DropTarget` on the list: lower half of a row drops after it); a context menu in the list (New, Duplicate, Delete Slide); the list is rebuilt after each slide step; the canvas's accessible name says the layout ("Slide 2, Title and Content"). Undo, redo, and showing a change moved from `editing.rs` to `DeckWindow`.
  - Found: a `PopoverMenu` parented to the `GtkListBox` made `remove_all()` loop forever (it only removes rows), so the window hung at the first rebuild; the context menu now hangs off the box around the list. A press held past the double-click time is a long press to GTK's list box, so a thumbnail "click" made by `dnd_helpers.py drag X Y X Y` (1.9 s) never selected anything; `dnd_helpers.py click` makes a quick one, and the check's clicks use it.
  - Deviations: slide-number fields keep the number they were read with when slides move (the model holds them as plain text); a duplicate keeps its source's `part`, for FRAME-8 to copy. The context menu isn't driven by the GUI check (its actions are the ones Ctrl+M, Ctrl+D, Delete, and the New Slide button run).
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p pptx --test layouts` (layouts.pptx's 11 layouts with names and types in order, each slide's layout; themes.pptx's 77 layouts over 7 masters; Title and Content's new slide has a title and a body placeholder, empty, at slide 2's frames, typing slide 2's looks, the bullet kept, the layout's and master's shapes and background); `cargo test -p deck-edit --test slides`: `ctrl_m_takes_the_slides_layout_or_title_and_content_after_a_title_slide` (layouts.pptx, the fixture's `tx`, themes.pptx per master), `slide_steps_undo_and_redo` (add, type its title, duplicate, delete, move, add on Blank: each undo and redo equals the deck saved at that step; shown slides 1, 1, 2, 0, 0, 11), `undoing_shows_the_slide_it_changes_back`. `scripts/check_frame.sh` prints `FRAME OK` (87 checks, 25 new, section 10, on layouts.pptx): Ctrl+M on the title slide gives "Slide 2 of 11", "Slide 2, Title and Content", and an 11th thumbnail; the New Slide button gives "Slide 3, Title and Content"; the menu lists the layouts (5 checked by name) and Two Content gives "Slide 4, Two Content"; Ctrl+Z three times returns to "Slide 3 of 12", then "Slide 1 of 10" with no 11th thumbnail; Ctrl+Y gives slide 2 back; Ctrl+D on slide 3 gives "Slide 4, Section Header", Ctrl+Up twice "Slide 2, Section Header"; a click on thumbnail 1 and Delete give "Slide 1 of 10" without "Centered Title", Ctrl+Z "Slide 1, Title Slide"; dragging thumbnail 1 to thumbnail 3's lower half gives "Slide 3, Title Slide", Ctrl+Z puts it back.
- **2026-10-11 SUITE-4:** slate.cronkite.cc and frame.cronkite.cc (user request). Branch `story/suite-4-app-pages` (3 commits, closed on 2026-10-12 with an `ours` merge; this note and the README and CLAUDE.md lines below are what carried over).
  - Built: placeholder pages in `sites/slate/` and `sites/frame/`, each "In development" with Bricks' installer as what's available. It also built `scripts/ship.sh page NAME` (cronkite, slate, frame) to replace `suite-site`. That command named each Pages project after its folder and added the custom domains through the Pages API.
  - Deployed on 2026-10-11 (user approved): projects `slate` (slate-4f6.pages.dev) and `frame` (frame-dxe.pages.dev).
    - The user added proxied CNAMEs `slate → slate-4f6.pages.dev` and `frame → frame-dxe.pages.dev`, and both domains served their pages (200).
    - A custom domain added through the API gets no DNS record, unlike one added in the dashboard: the names were NXDOMAIN at Cloudflare's nameserver until the user created them. The local systemd-resolved cache kept that NXDOMAIN; `resolvectl flush-caches` cleared it.
  - Superseded before it merged:
    - The Slate (SLATE-9, v0.1.0) and Frame (FRAME-9, v0.1.0) releases replaced the placeholders with download pages at the same domains, deployed by `ship.sh slate|frame site` into the same projects.
    - `suite-site` stays for cronkite.cc, the one static page left.
    - The custom-domain API calls weren't needed again: all four domains are attached.
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-11:** Slate shows documents in its own Pango flow (ADR 0007); the GtkTextView viewer (`view::Column`, its text tags, embedded grids and pictures) is gone. New toolkit-free crate `apps/slate/crates/flow`: every non-table block at any depth is a leaf numbered in document order; top-level heights sit in a Fenwick index, estimated from per-look line metrics until measured; the scroll position is a block and an offset into it; tables (homogeneous columns, rows as tall as their tallest cell, nested tables), pictures fitted to their column, page breaks, and rules lay out in the flow; hit-testing, selection, plain-text copy, and the accessible text (leaves joined by `\n`, cells and alt text included) work in model positions. The app's `view::Page` (a `GtkWidget` with `GtkScrollable` and `GtkAccessibleText`) draws it through `snapshot` with a Pango shaper (one cached `pango::Layout` per leaf), lays out the viewport in `size_allocate` and the rest in idle slices of at most 4 ms, selects by dragging (autoscrolling past the edges), copies with Ctrl+A and Ctrl+C, opens links on Ctrl+click, and scrolls with the arrow keys, Page Up and Page Down, Space, Home, and End. Page breaks are child `GtkSeparator`s named "Page break" that draw their own dashed line.
  - Decision (user, mid-story): the page uses one document font, Liberation Sans (metric-compatible with Arial), else the system's sans, at 11 pt, whatever the desktop font. With the desktop's Adwaita Sans (a variable font), re-wrapping the 150,000-word document took p50 540 ms / max 721 ms, missing the 500 ms target. With Liberation Sans, on the same build, it took 279/287 ms, matching the spike's 286 ms (which shaped `Sans` = Liberation Sans). Recorded in `docs/specs/slate.md`.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (36 checks, 2 of them new: Ctrl+A, Ctrl+C in w.txt puts the file's lines on the clipboard, read with `wl-paste`; a virtual-pointer drag from left of looks.md's plain line to the right of the bold line below it copies `HHHHHHHH\nHHHHHHHH`). Every viewer check passes unchanged on the flow: formats, the accessible text, headings and bold measured in pixels, Open, drop, alerts, text widths to the pixel in three windows, restart, narrow window, and the page-break marker. Screenshots checked by eye: nested and merged DOCX tables, the Markdown table's alignment, pictures, code bands, list markers, the page-break line, and the selection highlight. Flow unit tests with a fake shaper (`apps/slate/crates/flow/tests/flow.rs`, 10) cover the height index, estimates replaced without moving the viewport, width changes that keep the top block, hit-testing, tables, pictures, page breaks, links, time-sliced layout, and plain-text copy. Perf: `apps/slate/tests/flow_perf.rs` runs `slate FILE --bench-flow` (new hidden flag, as Frame's `--bench-draw`) on the spike's document written as Markdown (150,033 words). On the spike's box (Hyprland 0.56.2, llvmpipe, a window rule floating it at 1600x1000, so the page is 1600x928 and Full is 1552 px), 10 Narrow ↔ Full switches: the new column shows in the next frame every time (max 9.8 ms CPU, target one frame); the whole document re-wraps in p50 265 / max 280 ms (target < 500); 900 scrolling frames at 37 px take p50 8.0 / p99 10.2 ms CPU (target p99 < 16); open to full layout takes 364 ms. This isn't Bricks' reference box.
- **2026-10-11 FRAME-6:** Frame edits slide text on the canvas (ADR 0006). New toolkit-free crate `apps/frame/crates/deck-edit`: a `TextEdit` (typing, delete, Enter as a new paragraph with the same looks, Shift+Enter as a line break) replaces a stretch of one shape's text and holds the text it removes and the text it puts in, never a snapshot, so it applies and undoes alike; applying, undoing, and redoing return a `Change` naming the slide, the shape, and the property (`Text`) for FRAME-8's patched save. `History` keeps 1,000 steps and joins typing into one step per word; a click, a caret move, or an undo ends the step. The model gains ADR 0006's provenance for text: `Run::sources` says for each look whether it's the shape's own or comes from the layout, the master, or the presentation (the reader tracks it through the list-style chain); `Paragraph::end` is the paragraph mark's whole look (was `end_size`), which an empty paragraph types in; bullets stay on empty paragraphs and show once there's text (`Paragraph::shown_bullet`), keep "follows the text" for font, size, and colour, and numbers are counted by `TextBody::number_bullets` (moved from the reader) so Enter renumbers. Neighbouring runs that look the same merge at read (`Paragraph::merge_runs`; LibreOffice's dump comparison is per character, unchanged), so an undone edit gives the body back exactly. Typed text takes the look of the character before the caret, of the text it replaces, or of an empty paragraph's mark.
  - The window: a click in a text box or placeholder puts the caret where it lands (Shift+click selects), typing comes through a `gtk::IMMulticontext`, and the keys are the spec's (FR1 "Slide text"); the canvas outlines empty placeholders and the edited box (dashed) and draws the selection and the caret in the text's colour; the thumbnail and the canvas description follow each edit; undo goes to the slide it changed. The renderer lays a shape's text out once for drawing and for the caret (`render::place_text`: Pango hit-testing, caret, selection, visual Left and Right, Up and Down by line, Home and End by line). Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z, and Ctrl+Y are handled in the window's capture-phase key controller, as in Bricks: an application accelerator for Ctrl+Shift+Z didn't fire in the container check.
  - Deviations: no drag selection, double- or triple-click selection, clipboard, or input-method preedit yet (composed text shows when committed); text in all capitals keeps a letter whose capital is longer (ß) as written, so caret offsets match the text; SmartArt drawing text edits on the canvas like any shape's, though FRAME-8 has to decide whether it can save it.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p deck-edit` (4 tests on `layouts.pptx` and the fixture): `typing_in_a_placeholder_keeps_its_layouts_style` (slide 1's subtitle, colour from the Title Slide layout and size from the master: typed text joins its run with the same look and sources, the frame doesn't move; emptied and retyped, it takes the paragraph mark's look, the same), `typing_in_a_text_box_takes_the_look_before_the_caret`, `undo_and_redo_restore_each_step` (7 steps: two typed words, Enter, a line break, two Backspaces that join paragraphs, typing over a selection across paragraphs; each undo and redo equals the body saved at that step), `new_paragraphs_take_their_numbers`. `scripts/check_frame.sh` prints `FRAME OK` (62 checks, 13 new, section 9): in layouts.pptx a click at the end of the subtitle and Left keep "Slide 1 of 10"; typing " here" makes the description "subtitle here" and the subtitle area 1063 → 1713 dark pixels; Ctrl+Z gives "subtitle " then "subtitle"; Ctrl+Shift+Z and Ctrl+Y give them back; Enter and "second" add a paragraph; Ctrl+A, Delete, and "Typed" refill the placeholder; Escape and Page Down show slide 2; in the fixture a click at the end of slide 3's text box and "!" give "…in Liberation Serif!". FRAME-4's drawing benchmark after the renderer change (headless sway, pixman, 1030x646, 300 redraws of each deck's densest slide): worst frame CPU p99 9.67 ms (themes.pptx slide 6; 9.82 ms at FRAME-4), target < 16 ms.
  - Found: in `cargo test --workspace` here, `csv-engine`'s `nasty_corpus` fails because its test binary was built with `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR=/tmp/ci-clone/...` (it can't find `/tmp/ci-clone/scripts/gen_corpus.py`): a stale build sharing this `target/`, presumably from the CI note's clean clone. `cargo test -p csv-engine` passes; a `cargo clean -p csv-engine` should clear it. Not a code problem.
- **2026-10-11 M4 gate:** approved by the user on container evidence, not VMs. `scripts/check_install.sh` installs, updates from v0.1.0, and uninstalls as a normal user in clean Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora containers, and `gio open` (the call a file manager's double-click makes) opens a CSV in Bricks (PKG-1, PKG-4). On the live site, `https://bricks.cronkite.cc/install.sh` returns 200 and points to `https://dl.cronkite.cc`, whose `latest.json` names v0.2.1, and that tarball returns 200 (2,004,663 bytes). Unproven: clean VMs with a desktop, and a real double-click in a file manager (Nautilus, Dolphin). Both stay open as follow-ups. PKG-2 (Flatpak) is still deferred.
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-5:** both engines spiked in `spikes/slate-5/` on the same 150,000-word document (1,740 paragraphs) with the same looks; the user chose the custom Pango flow (ADR 0007, Accepted). At 1600x1000, Full width (1552 px): typing p50/p99 11.2/19.7 ms (GtkTextView) vs 4.8/18.7 ms (flow); re-wrap of the whole document p50 489 ms vs 286 ms (at 3800x2080: 913 vs 341 ms); open to full layout 865 vs 623 ms. Deciding factor: GtkTextView keeps a second copy of the document (a two-way offset map past list markers and U+FFFC anchors) and can't select or copy across a table; the flow draws the model directly but costs an estimated 14-19 weeks of editor work and, for full accessibility, gtk4-rs `v4_16`/`v4_22` above the suite's 4.14 minimum. The flow's AT-SPI text (contents, caret, text at offset, change events) works on 4.14.
  - Caveat: every run was software rendered (llvmpipe) because the NVIDIA kernel module (615.71.09) and nvidia-utils (615.78.08) don't match; the user accepted on these numbers. Rerun `cargo build --release && python3 measure.py` in `spikes/slate-5` after a reboot to record GPU numbers; the ADR's revisit trigger names a reversed re-wrap result.
  - Found: GtkTextView asserts (`onscreen_validated`) when margins or tags change inside its own size-allocate, e.g. from a hadjustment page-size notify; Slate's `Column::fit` uses that hook. Not reproduced in Slate (fixture.docx at Medium, 20 floating resizes between 620 and 1600 px, no warning or exit), so no fix now; if it ever shows, defer `fit` to an idle as the spike does.
  - SL1's engine work is split: SLATE-11 (the flow shows documents, tables and pictures included, replacing the GtkTextView viewer), SLATE-6 (caret, typing, formatting as undoable commands, now after SLATE-11), SLATE-12 (editing in tables), SLATE-13 (IME composition), SLATE-14 (selection and clipboard), SLATE-15 (accessibility). Not yet in the backlog though the spec's SL1 lists them: crash recovery and find and replace.
- **2026-10-11 CI:** the `check` job had failed on every push to main since v0.1.0: APP-8's `well_formed_files_have_no_problems` (`apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/malformed.rs`) read the gitignored `corpus/nasty`, which only the `bench` job generates, and `cargo test` stopped at that binary. The test now generates the nasty corpus into a temporary directory with the real generator (`gen_corpus.py --max-mb 0`), as ENG-3's `nasty_corpus` test does. A clean clone without `corpus/` passes `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` (it was the only test needing `corpus/` outside `--ignored`).
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-10:** Slate's column is pageless with a text width setting (ADR 0005). The window's primary menu (header bar button, F10) has a View section with a Text Width submenu of radio items Narrow, Medium, Wide, Full, backed by the stateful `app.text-width` action (string state), so every window of the process switches at once; other Slate processes (each `slate FILE` is one, the app is `NON_UNIQUE`) follow through a `gio::FileMonitor` on the settings file, read off the UI thread. The choice is saved to `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/slate/settings` (`text-width=wide`), written on a writer thread of its own (temp file in the same directory, fsync, rename; the newest of queued changes wins; shutdown waits for it); it's read at startup, and a missing or bad file means Medium (`apps/slate/src/settings.rs`). `view::column_width(setting, view width)`: the setting's width (480, 624, 816), or the view less 24 px a side when that is narrower (Full always), never under 120 px; `view::Column` (was `Page`; `PAGE_WIDTH` gone) centres it with margins and resizes its embedded widgets when the width changes rather than re-rendering: rules and page-break lines take the column's width, tables split it into homogeneous columns (nested tables follow their cell), pictures scale down to fit and back up to their size. Page breaks stay a thin dashed line named "Page break" to screen readers (already so in SLATE-4).
  - Deviations: no menu bar, so "View → Text Width" is the primary menu's View section and its Text Width submenu. Slate makes `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/slate/` at startup so the settings file can be watched before it exists.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (34 checks, 19 of them new; Arch container, headless sway, 1280x720): col-a.md and col-b.md (an 800-letter word that wraps at every width, a rule, a table) in two windows of one process and col-c.md in a second process, tabbed, measured in screenshots (the rule's grey run gives the column; the widest ink row above it the text): Medium 624/618 px (rule/text), then F10 → Text Width in col-a's menu: Narrow 480/474, Wide 816/811, Full 1232/1227 in all three windows, each centred to the pixel; after Ctrl+Q the settings file reads `text-width=full` and relaunched slate shows 1232/1227; Wide is 816 in a 1280 px window and 592/586 once the window floats at 640 px, where Narrow stays 480/474; `slate fixture.docx` exposes 1 accessible object named "Page break". The SLATE-4 binary (f18539e) passes the 15 old checks and fails 14 of the 19 new ones (it passes Medium in three windows, Ctrl+Q, and the page break's name, which SLATE-4 already set). Unit tests: `settings` (5: each width round-trips, missing or bad values mean Medium, spaces, other keys, and CRLF are tolerated, the ADR's widths, an atomic write leaves no temporary) and `view::column_width` (4: wide windows get the setting's width, Full is the window less 48, narrower windows narrow the column, the 120 px floor). Screenshots inspected at all four widths: fixture.md (tables, rule, picture), fixture.docx (tables with a merged cell and a nested table, the page break line, picture), IllustrativeCases.docx (justified text and tables of figures), WithGIF.docx, SampleDoc.docx (page break), and a 1000x200 picture (480x96 at Narrow, 624 and 816 wide at Medium and Wide, its own 1000 at Full); tables, rules, and page-break lines fill the column at each width and nothing overflows.
- **2026-10-11 SUITE-3:** two product decisions by the user. **Slate is pageless:** one endless column, never paginated, with View → Text Width (Narrow 480, Medium 624, Wide 816, Full) as a user setting, not a document property; page breaks stay as markers in the flow, DOCX page setup is kept by the patched save, and paginated printing and PDF export are non-goals (new ADR 0005; ADR 0003's pagination part superseded; spec `docs/specs/slate.md` "Pageless"). New SL0 story SLATE-10 builds the width setting; SLATE-5 now decides only the editing engine for the flow and depends on SLATE-10. **Frame is easier to use than PowerPoint**, by both directions offered: Keynote-like direct editing (header bar instead of a ribbon, one inspector for the selection, snapping and smart guides, layouts as thumbnails, pictures filling empty placeholders, shrink-to-fit text, File → New from a built-in blank deck, undo instead of confirmations) plus an outline view of titles and bullets that edits the same slides (new ADR 0006: a toolkit-free `deck-edit` crate holds the commands, outline projection, and snapping; spec `docs/specs/frame.md` "Easier than PowerPoint"). FR1 gains FRAME-10..15; FRAME-15 proves the claim by counting the spec's reference task's input actions in Frame against a hand count in PowerPoint.
- **2026-10-11 FRAME-5:** Frame plays decks full screen. F5 (or the header bar's play button) starts the slideshow from the first slide, Shift+F5 from the current one (the next shown one when it's hidden): a fullscreen, undecorated window of the same `SlideView` renderer, the slide scaled to fit and letterboxed in black (a slide within a pixel of filling the screen fills it, so no sliver of black shows). Space, Enter, Right, Down, Page Down, and a click go forward; Left, Up, Page Up, and Backspace back; Home and End to the first and last; hidden slides are skipped. Escape, or going past the last slide, ends the show, back in the deck's window on the slide it showed. The show's slide says its text to assistive technology like the canvas ("Slideshow slide N", its text as the description).
  - Deviations: going past the last slide ends the show at once (PowerPoint shows a black "end of slide show" screen first); the spec says so now.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_frame.sh` (now with the slideshow's checks) prints `FRAME OK` on this story's binary: on the fixture, Shift+F5 on slide 4 shows slide 4; F5 on slide 3 shows slide 1, and the screenshot is the slide (corners and top edge white, 97% of the screen white); Space shows slide 2 ("Levels and alignment"); Escape returns to the window at "Slide 2 of 5" with no slideshow left; Shift+F5 on slide 4 then Space skips hidden slide 5 and ends the show at "Slide 4 of 5"; SampleShow.pptx (4:3) shows with black bars left and right on the 16:9 screen. FRAME-4's binary passes the viewer's checks and fails 14 of the slideshow's (the scaffold fails all).
- **2026-10-11 FRAME-4:** `frame` is a GTK 4 viewer. `frame FILE`, Ctrl+O (a `gtk::FileDialog` filtered to .pptx/.pptm, the portal's or GTK's own), and files dropped on any window open a deck in its own window (a file already open brings its window forward); the deck is read and its pictures decoded into GDK textures off the UI thread (`gio::spawn_blocking`) while the window says "Opening…"; a file that can't be read gives an alert naming it and the reason (.ppt, .odp, Keynote: "Save it as .pptx first"), then its window closes. Without a file a start window says how to open one and closes once a deck opens. The window has a sidebar of numbered thumbnails (hidden slides dimmed, clicking selects), the canvas (the slide scaled to fit, letterboxed on a tint of the theme's foreground), and a status bar ("Slide N of M"); Page Down/Up, arrows, Home, and End move; the title is the file name; the canvas's accessible label is "Slide N" and its description the slide's text. One renderer (`apps/frame/src/render.rs`, a `SlideView` widget drawing through `snapshot`) serves thumbnails and canvas: backgrounds, preset geometry as GSK paths (rect, roundRect with its adjust, ellipse, line and connectors, triangle, right triangle; others their bounding rectangle), rotation and flips (text and tables turn but don't mirror), solid and picture fills, outlines with width and dashes, pictures with their crop, tables (rows grow to fit their text; fills, text, then borders; merged cells span), labelled dashed outlines for unsupported content (or its preview picture), and text through Pango: runs as attributes (font, size, bold, italic, underline, strike, colour with alpha, super/subscript, caps), bullets placed at the first line's indent and baseline, margins and hanging indents, alignment, line spacing (multiples and points, autofit's reduction), space before/after, insets, anchoring, `anchorCtr`, autofit's font scale, unwrapped text, vertical text. `frame FILE --bench-draw FRAMES` redraws the deck's densest slide every frame and prints frame CPU times.
  - Changed in the reader: a SmartArt drawing part's text lays out in its `txXfrm` box (model `Shape::text_frame`), as PowerPoint and LibreOffice draw it, not in the whole circle; Symbol-font bullets written as private-use code points (`U+F02D`, LibreOffice's) map to Unicode like Latin-1 ones (`−`, `•`).
  - Deviations: gradients and patterns draw as their stand-in colour (spec updated); freeform geometry draws as its bounding rectangle, as the spec says of other presets. Text uses the fonts the system has (the container has only DejaVu), so it wraps wider than in PowerPoint (ADR 0004).
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_frame.sh` prints `FRAME OK` on this story's binary: all 10 test decks open from the command line ("Slide 1 of N", N each deck's slide count); Page Down shows "Slide 2 of 5" and the canvas description holds slide 2's lines, not slide 1's; on slide 3 the centre of the 4472C4 rectangle and on slide 4 the picture's red and blue halves are those colours where the fixture puts them (found inside the canvas's accessible extents); Ctrl+O with a typed path opens shapes.pptx in a second window; layouts.pptx dropped on the start window opens and the start window closes; old.ppt and a damaged .pptx alert with their names and reasons. The scaffold (`/tmp/frame-scaffold`) fails every check. `cargo test --release -p frame --test draw_perf -- --ignored --nocapture` (headless sway, pixman, GSK's software renderer, no GPU): densest slide of each of the 10 decks, 300 redraws each at 1030x646: worst frame CPU p99 9.82 ms (themes.pptx slide 6, 40 shapes; building the snapshot 0.54 ms of it, the rest software rendering), grouping.pptx's 67 shapes 6.01 ms; target < 16 ms. Screenshots of the fixture and the POI decks looked right (compared with LibreOffice's PNG export for themes, table-with-theme, smartart-simple).
  - Follow-ups: custom geometry paths (`custGeom`) and more presets; gradient fills drawn as gradients.
- **2026-10-11 FRAME-3:** Frame reads what shapes look like. The model gains `Fill` (none, solid, picture, or a stand-in colour with a label for gradients and patterns), `Outline` (width, colour, dash), `Image` (format sniffed from the bytes, `Arc` bytes shared across slides), `Picture` (image and `srcRect` crop), `Table` (column widths, row heights, cells with text, fill, four borders, spans, merges), `Background` (solid, picture, or a labelled stand-in), and `Unsupported` shapes carry the preview picture the file keeps (a video's poster frame, an OLE object's preview). `pptx` resolves fills and outlines from `spPr` through placeholders and the shape style's `fillRef`/`lnRef` into the theme's `fillStyleLst`/`lnStyleLst`/`bgFillStyleLst` with `phClr` (theme fill pictures through the theme's relationships), `grpFill` from the group, backgrounds slide → layout → master (`bgPr` or `bgRef`); pictures by `r:embed` (PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP drawn; EMF, WMF, SVG, TIFF become "Picture (EMF)" etc.); video and audio pictures, OLE objects (the `mc:Fallback` preview), charts become labelled shapes; SmartArt draws from the drawing PowerPoint saves with it (`diagramDrawing`, found through the data part's `dataModelExt`), placed in the graphic frame; tables apply the table style (`tableStyles.xml`, or PowerPoint's built-in default "Medium Style 2 - Accent 1" when only named) part by part (whole table, banded rows/columns, first/last column, last/first row, corners; outer vs inside borders per part) and then each cell's `tcPr` (fill, `lnL`/`lnR`/`lnT`/`lnB`, margins, anchor); cell text gets the style's `tcTxStyle` (bold, italic, font, colour).
  - Deviations: gradient and pattern fills and non-solid backgrounds keep a stand-in colour (a gradient's middle stop, a pattern's foreground) with a label, so the viewer can show more than an outline. LibreOffice reports table border widths on its own scale and gives cells covered by a merge a fill of its own default; borders compare by presence and colour, covered cells by text only. LibreOffice adds an invisible background rectangle to SmartArt drawings; the comparison skips it.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p pptx --test content` over 10 decks (now also POI's `smartart-simple.pptx`, `table-with-theme.pptx`, and `sample_pptx_grouping_issues.pptx` as `grouping.pptx`): `pictures_carry_their_bytes` (4 pictures: bytes equal a media part, format and pixel size equal LibreOffice's), `fills_and_outlines_match_libreoffice` (172 shapes: fill kind and colour with alpha, outline colour and width within 360 EMU), `backgrounds_match_libreoffice` (solid, gradient, and picture backgrounds), `table_cells_carry_text_fills_and_borders` (42 cells: text runs, fills, border colours), `unsupported_content_is_a_labelled_shape` (a chart), `smart_art_draws_from_its_saved_drawing`; `--test unsupported` (in-memory deck: video with its poster, EMF picture, OLE object with its fallback preview, chart). Group children land where LibreOffice reads them: `--test frames` now compares 182 shapes over the 10 decks, 39 of them in groups or SmartArt (largest difference 720 EMU).
- **2026-10-11 FRAME-2:** Frame reads text. The model gains `TextBody` (paragraphs, anchor, `anchorCtr`, insets, wrap, `normAutofit` font scale and line reduction, vertical text), `Paragraph` (level, alignment, margin and indent, line spacing, space before/after, resolved bullet, end-of-paragraph size), `Run` (text, font, size, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, colour with alpha, baseline, caps; a line break is a `\n` run), and `Color`. `pptx` resolves each property through PowerPoint's chain: `defaultTextStyle` → the master's `titleStyle`/`bodyStyle`/`otherStyle` by placeholder family → the shape style's `fontRef` → the master's and the layout's placeholder `lstStyle` → the shape's `lstStyle` → `pPr`/`rPr` (`defPPr` then `lvlNpPr`, `defRPr` per level); `bodyPr` inherits master → layout → shape; theme fonts (`+mj-lt`, `+mn-lt`, east Asian ones falling back to Latin); colours (`srgbClr`, `schemeClr` through the master's `clrMap` with layout and slide `overrideClrMapping`, `sysClr`, `prstClr`, `scrgbClr`, `hslClr`) with `lumMod`, `lumOff`, `tint`, `shade`, `alpha`, `sat*`, `hue*`, `gray`, `inv`, `comp`, computed with LibreOffice's arithmetic (gamma-2.3 linear space for tint and shade, HSL for the rest); bullets (`buChar` with Wingdings/Symbol characters mapped to Unicode, `buAutoNum` with arabic/roman/alpha schemes, counters per level that restart under a higher level or an unnumbered paragraph, `buNone`, `buFont`, `buClr`, `buSzPct`/`buSzPts`), slide-number fields, and hyperlinks in the theme's `hlink` colour, underlined.
  - Deviations: an orphan placeholder's text reads like a text box (`otherStyle`), as in LibreOffice, not with its master's title or body style. Sizes are the written ones; autofit's font scale is kept apart on the text body, as LibreOffice does.
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p pptx --test text`: `text_matches_libreoffice` compares every paragraph's text, level, and alignment and every character's size, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, colour, and font with LibreOffice's dump over 7 decks (the fixture and 6 PowerPoint-authored POI samples, now with `themes.pptx`, `backgrounds.pptx`, and `bar-chart.pptx`): 124 paragraphs, 146 runs. Colour unit tests reproduce LibreOffice's values for `tint` 40% of accent2 (`CCD3E0`), `shade` 50% of accent1 (`117802`), `lumMod` 60% + `lumOff` 40% of accent6 (`E9706D`), `lumMod` 50% of accent5 (`654E00`), and `alpha`; unit tests for autonumbering, bullet size/colour/symbol fonts, body properties, fields. The frame comparison now covers 109 shapes (largest difference 720 EMU).
- **2026-10-11 FRAME-1:** Frame reads decks. New crates `apps/frame/crates/presentation-model` (the model of ADR 0004: slide size, slides in show order, shapes with resolved frames, placeholder role, layer; no dependencies) and `apps/frame/crates/pptx` (zip + quick-xml): slide order from `presentation.xml` `sldIdLst` through its relationships, `sldSz`, hidden slides (`show="0"`), notes (the notes slide's body placeholder), every shape's frame (`xfrm` off/ext/rot/flipH/flipV) with placeholder inheritance slide → layout → master (by `idx`, then `type`, then title/body family), group transforms flattened (child space `chOff`/`chExt` scaled, flipped, and rotated about the group centre, nested groups composed), `mc:AlternateContent` (a Choice needing only p14/p15/a14/a16, else the Fallback), and the master's and layout's own shapes under the slide's (`showMasterSp`). `pptx::kind`/`refusal` name .ppt, password-protected, .odp, and Keynote files ("Save it as .pptx first"). `scripts/lo_pptx.py` (UNO, port 2170): `fixture OUT` builds a 5-slide Impress deck (title, levels, shapes with fills/outlines/rotation/flip, picture, group, table, hidden slide, notes), then patches it for theme colours with tint/shade/lumMod/lumOff/alpha, a frameless title, and a group scaled 1.5x and rotated 30 degrees; `dump FILE` prints LibreOffice's reading. Real-world decks: PowerPoint-authored `shapes.pptx`, `layouts.pptx`, `SampleShow.pptx` from Apache POI's test data (Apache-2.0; `apps/frame/crates/pptx/tests/data/README.md`).
  - Deviations: "within 1 EMU" can't be tested against LibreOffice, which keeps lengths in 1/100 mm (360 EMU) and reads text frames that grow with their text (`spAutoFit`) one unit larger on each side; frames match within 720 EMU (2 units), rotation within 0.05 degree. LibreOffice turns slide footer, slide-number, and field date placeholders into its own header/footer settings rather than shapes, so the comparison skips ours it has no shape for (Frame keeps them: PowerPoint shows them). A placeholder its layout has no match for (an orphan, `idx="4294967295"` on a Blank layout) inherits no frame, as in LibreOffice (ADR 0004 updated).
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p pptx --test frames`: `slides_and_frames_match_libreoffice` (4 decks, slide count, order by shape names, hidden flags, slide size within 360 EMU, 71 shapes' kinds and frames; largest difference 720 EMU), `placeholders_without_a_frame_take_their_layouts_or_masters` (layouts.pptx's `<p:spPr/>` placeholders, the fixture's frameless title), `group_children_take_the_group_transform`, `notes_match_libreoffice`; unit tests for group transforms, part-name resolution, XML entities, and format detection.
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-4:** `slate` is a GTK 4 application (spec SL0, ADR 0003). `slate FILE…` opens each file in its own window (a file already open is raised); without a file a start window offers Open. Ctrl+O (header button and menu) opens `gtk::FileDialog` filtered to Documents (.txt, .text, .md, .markdown, .docx by MIME type and suffix) with All files as the other filter; files dropped on any window open the same way. A new window titled with the file name says "Opening…" (spinner) while `gio::spawn_blocking` reads the file and decodes its pictures (`apps/slate/src/open.rs`); the reader is picked by extension. A file that can't be read gets an alert, "Cannot open “NAME”" with the reason (.doc, .odt, .rtf, and an OLE2 file named .docx: "Save it as .docx first"; a damaged .docx: the reader's reason as a sentence; other extensions name what Slate opens), and its window closes after Close; the start window closes once a document shows. The page (`apps/slate/src/view.rs`) is a read-only, selectable `GtkTextView` on white, a 624 px column (6.5 in at 96 dpi) centred by margins that follow the window's width; one text tag per distinct look: headings 1-6 and Title scaled and bold (Markdown and text; a DOCX's runs already carry its styles' sizes and weights, so its headings only get spacing), quotes indented (italic and grey in Markdown), bold, italic, underline, strike, inline code (monospace on grey), font size and colour, alignment, links blue and underlined (pointer cursor; Ctrl+click opens them with `gtk::UriLauncher`), list markers (bullets by level, numbered labels from the model, ☐/☑ task boxes) hanging in a 28 px indent per level, code blocks monospace on a grey band, raw Markdown in grey monospace. Tables are embedded `gtk::Grid`s (homogeneous columns over the column width, spans, header rows bold in Markdown) whose cells are text views drawn the same way (nested tables too); pictures are embedded `gtk::Picture`s at their size, scaled down to fit the column, with their alt text as the accessible text (a picture that doesn't decode shows "[picture: ALT]"); page breaks draw a dashed line, rules a thin one. Status bar: format and word count ("Word document · 1,028 words"). The page's text reaches screen readers through GTK's text view accessibility.
  - Deviations: none from the spec. AT-SPI quirk found: GTK counts embedded widgets in a text view's character count but leaves them out of its text, so `get_text(0, get_character_count())` misses the last few characters of a page with tables or pictures; the check reads to the end (`-1`).
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_slate.sh` SLATE OK (15 checks, Arch container, headless sway): `slate w.txt` (Windows-1252, CR) titled w.txt with accessible text equal to the file's lines; `slate fixture.md` titled fixture.md with 18 of 18 constructs in the accessible text (table cells and the picture's alt text included); `slate fixture.docx` with 39 of 39 paragraph lines of its LibreOffice dump; `# HHHHHHHH`, `HHHHHHHH`, `**HHHHHHHH**`: the heading's ink band is 200% of the plain line's height and the bold line has 191% of its ink; Ctrl+O from the start window and a typed path open fixture.md; a .docx dragged (wlr virtual pointer) onto the w.txt window opens in a window of its own; bad.docx ("It isn't a readable Word document (invalid Zip archive: Could not find EOCD).") and old.doc ("… Save it as .docx first.") alert with their names, and after Close the window is gone and slate has exited. The scaffold binary (`/tmp/slate-scaffold`, 8c5470e) fails 14 of the 15 checks. `apps/slate/src/open.rs` unit tests (4): .doc/.odt/.rtf/.DOC and an OLE2 file named .docx are told to save as .docx, a non-zip .docx says it isn't a readable Word document, an unknown extension names what Slate opens. Screenshots inspected on w.txt, fixture.md, fixture.docx, and the POI samples IllustrativeCases.docx (justified text, tables of figures, 1,028 words) and Numbering.docx (bullet levels, 1./a./i., 1.1.1., custom labels).
  - Follow-ups: list markers wider than the 28 px hang (e.g. `NEW-1-FORMAT`) push the text to the next tab stop; DOCX table column widths (`tblGrid`) and cell shading aren't used (columns are equal); the status bar doesn't show the encoding or line endings of a text file.
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-3:** new crate `apps/slate/crates/docx` (`zip` 8 + `quick-xml` 0.41, streaming): `docx::read(path)`/`read_from(reader)`. The main part comes from `_rels/.rels`; styles and numbering from its relationships. Run looks resolve as document defaults, then the paragraph style's `basedOn` chain, then the character style's chain (`rStyle`, else the default character style), then direct formatting; `b`/`i`/`u`/`strike`/`dstrike` take `w:val` off values (`0`, `false`, `off`), `sz` (half-points), `color` (`auto` resets). Paragraph styles map by name: Title, heading 1-9 (7-9 show as 6), Quote/Intense Quote (and LibreOffice's Quotations/Block Quotation); others are Normal. Alignment from `jc`. Lists from the paragraph's or its style's `numPr` (`numId` 0 ends a list; a style-only level comes from the level whose `pStyle` names it): counters per abstract numbering, `startOverride` on an instance's first use, deeper levels restart, labels from `lvlText` with decimal, zero-padded, letter, roman, ordinal formats and `isLgl`; `numStyleLink` followed. Hyperlinks by relationship or anchor, HYPERLINK fields; fields show their results, not codes. Pictures from `w:drawing` (`a:blip`, `wp:extent`, `wp:docPr` alt) and VML `w:pict`, bytes read once per part. Tables with `gridSpan`, `tblHeader`, nested tables. Content controls, smart tags, custom XML, and tracked insertions are read through; `mc:Fallback` and text boxes skipped. Script `scripts/lo_docx.py` (port 2160): `fixture OUT` builds the fixture through UNO (Title, headings, every run look, character styles, alignments, quote, bullet and numbered lists with levels, tab, line break, hyperlink, inline picture with alt text, table with bold header, merged cell, two-paragraph cell, nested table, page break) and stores it as MS Word 2007 XML; `dump FILE` prints each paragraph (tables' cells in order) with style display name, list level, label, text, and runs by bold/italic/underline/strike, descending into content controls.
  - Deviations, all where LibreOffice (the oracle) reads it so: ECMA-376's XOR of toggle properties between paragraph and character styles isn't applied (LibreOffice shows Strong inside a bold heading as bold; the fixture has that case); a page break splits its paragraph (a break at the start only moves the paragraph; the part before a break is kept even when empty; empty parts after one are dropped); an empty paragraph that only carries a section's `sectPr` is not a paragraph (a page-starting section adds a page break); footnote and endnote references show their marks (1, 2… and i, ii…). Decided here: tracked deletions are hidden (the text as if changes were accepted; LibreOffice shows deleted text), tables nested deeper than 32 join the cell around them (POI's 10,000-deep `deep-table-cell.docx` overflowed the stack before), a paragraph's pictures come before its text as Image blocks.
  - Acceptance: `apps/slate/crates/docx/tests/libreoffice.rs` compares every paragraph and table cell (style, list level, numbered label, text, runs by b/i/u/s; normalization rules with reasons in the test) of `tests/data/fixture.docx` (116 dump lines) and six Word-authored Apache POI samples in `tests/data/poi` (ComplexNumberedLists, Numbering, IllustrativeCases, WithGIF, SampleDoc, footnotes; README with sources and Apache-2.0) with their committed LibreOffice dumps: all equal. Also checked by hand against 16 more POI samples: all equal except tracked changes (2), a legacy form checkbox LibreOffice itself reads inconsistently, and the 10,000-deep table. `tests/model.rs`: size 36 half-points and colour C00000, link URL, centre/right/justify, the picture's exact PNG bytes, alt text and 120x80 px size, page break between the right paragraphs, merged cell span 2, a GIF from Word, non-zip and non-Word zips refused. `tests/speed.rs` (ignored, release): a generated 500-page document (3.1 MB of document.xml, 256,600 words, 6,599 blocks) reads in 11 ms (target < 1 s).
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-2:** Markdown reader `document_model::read_markdown` (`pulldown-cmark` 0.13, tables, strikethrough, task lists, and footnotes on; decoded like plain text, encoding and line ending recorded). Headings 1-6, paragraphs (soft break = space, hard break = `\n`), quotes (nested depth in `indent`), bullet/ordered/task items with their level and number (`label` `N.`), item continuation paragraphs and code with the item depth as indent, fenced and indented code blocks (language = the info string's first word), tables with header row and column alignment on the cell paragraphs, rules, and images (an image is a block: the paragraph splits around it). Runs carry bold, italic, strike, code, and link. Raw blocks with their exact source: HTML blocks, footnote definitions, and link reference definitions; the parser resolves reference definitions without emitting them, so they are put back among the top-level blocks by their source offset. Not in the ADR, decided here: inline HTML stays in its paragraph as a `raw` run (shown as written), a footnote reference stays as its source text `[^1]`, and `load_linked_images` reads local pictures (relative to the file, or `file:` URLs; at most 64 MiB) into the model off the UI thread, leaving remote URLs as links.
  - Acceptance: `apps/slate/crates/document-model/tests/markdown.rs`: `tests/data/fixture.md` (every construct above) reads to a 54-line block outline compared exactly (each heading level, each run look, nested quote, bullet levels 0-2, ordered numbering restarting at 7, both task states, both code block kinds, the 3x3 table with left/centre/right cells, rule, image, the three Raw kinds in document order with their source); Raw sources occur verbatim in the file; item continuation paragraphs and code keep level 1; `picture.png` loads as embedded PNG while a missing file and an https URL stay links.
- **2026-10-11 SLATE-1:** new crate `apps/slate/crates/document-model` (ADR 0003): `Document` (blocks + `Format`), `Block` (paragraph, list item, code block, table of cells of blocks, image, page break, rule, `Raw`), `Paragraph` (style normal/title/heading 1-6/quote, alignment, indent, runs), `Run`/`RunStyle` (bold, italic, underline, strike, code, raw, size in half-points, colour, link), `ListKind` (bullet, ordered with its label, task), `Image` (alt, embedded bytes with sniffed PNG/JPEG/GIF or a link, size), `Document::word_count`. `read_text`: a BOM picks UTF-8 (stripped) or UTF-16 LE/BE; else valid UTF-8; else Windows-1252 (`encoding_rs`). CRLF, CR, and LF each end a line; one paragraph per line. `TextInfo` records the encoding, the first line break met (a file mixing them records the first; a file without breaks records LF), and whether the last line ends with a break, which SL1's save needs and the ADR didn't name.
  - Acceptance: `apps/slate/crates/document-model/tests/text.rs`: the same four lines committed as UTF-8/LF, UTF-8 BOM/CRLF, UTF-16 LE BOM/CRLF, UTF-16 BE BOM/LF without a final break, and Windows-1252/CR (`tests/data/*.txt`, `-text` in git) read to identical paragraphs with the right `TextInfo` each; mixed `a\r\nb\rc\nd` splits into four lines; `\r\r` is an empty line; non-UTF-8 bytes 0x80/0x93/0x94 read as € “ ”.
- **2026-10-11 SUITE-2:** Slate and Frame are planned (user decisions): each gets a read-only viewer first (SL0, FR0), editing and saving after (SL1, FR1). Slate opens .txt, .md, and .docx and shows Markdown formatted (WYSIWYG, saved back as Markdown in SL1); Frame opens .pptx only. Specs `docs/specs/slate.md` and `docs/specs/frame.md`; suite ADRs 0003 (Slate's document model and readers; the SL0 viewer is a read-only GtkTextView, the editing engine is SLATE-5's decision) and 0004 (Frame's model resolved at read time; a custom snapshot canvas). `scripts/backlog.py` now keeps a milestone chain per app: `next slate` and `next frame` don't wait on Bricks' open M4 gate (`scripts/test_backlog.py`).
- **2026-10-11 SUITE-1:** the suite is named Cronkite (user decision). GitHub: the user's RSS reader repo `avdrav1/Cronkite` was renamed `avdrav1/cronkite-rss` to free the name, then `avdrav1/bricks` was renamed `avdrav1/cronkite` (origin updated; GitHub redirects both old URLs). Branding is docs and metadata only: README, CLAUDE.md, the architecture overview, the Cargo `repository` URL. The `suite-*` crate names and the `dev.bricks.*` app IDs stay. Accepted ADRs are unchanged.
  - Sites: `site/` moved to `sites/bricks/` (ship.sh, check_install.sh, the ship-it skill). New `sites/cronkite/`: the suite's install page (Bricks' curl installer, what it does, GTK 4 per distro, update, uninstall; Slate and Frame "in development"). It is static and deploys on its own: `scripts/ship.sh suite-site [--dry-run]` to the Pages project `CF_SUITE_PAGES_PROJECT` (`cronkite`; created on first deploy).
