  - Deployed on 2026-10-11 after the user removed cronkite.cc from Netlify: Pages project `cronkite` (https://cronkite.pages.dev). wrangler 4.149 hands new projects to Workers, and that failed on a plain folder of assets ("Missing entry-point"). `ship.sh` now passes `--force` on create, which makes a classic Pages project like `spreadsheet`. cronkite.cc and www.cronkite.cc were added to the project through the Pages API. The user pointed both DNS records at `cronkite.pages.dev` (CNAME, proxied), because the ship token has no DNS permission. Both serve the page (200). A proxied name that isn't a custom domain on the project gets a 522, which is what www showed before it was added.
  - Acceptance: the page renders at 1280 px and 390 px (light and dark) with no horizontal overflow and no console errors; Copy puts `curl -fsSL https://bricks.cronkite.cc/install.sh | sh` on the clipboard, and that URL returns 200. `scripts/ship.sh suite-site --dry-run` prints the project create and the deploy. The project-exists check matches names exactly: before that fix, `cronkite` matched the domain `bricks.cronkite.cc` in the table.
- **2026-10-11 decision:** GTK 4 is the toolkit for the whole suite (user decision, skipping the comparative GUI prototype): suite ADR `docs/adr/0002-gtk4-for-the-suite.md`. `gtk` (gtk4-rs 0.11, `v4_14`) is now declared once in `[workspace.dependencies]`; only apps, `suite-ui`, and `suite-platform` may depend on it. Unproven for Slate's paginated layout and Frame's slide canvas: their first drawing stories measure frame time and latency as DEC-1 did.
- **2026-10-11 SUITE-0:** the repo is now a monorepo for the suite (user decision): Bricks, Slate, and Frame in one Cargo workspace (suite ADR `docs/adr/0001-use-rust-monorepo.md`, `docs/architecture/overview.md`). Bricks moved to `apps/bricks/` (was `app/`), its crates to `apps/bricks/crates/` (were `crates/`), and its docs to `apps/bricks/docs/` (were `docs/`); package names and the workspace version stay, so `-p xlsx` and `scripts/ship.sh bump` work as before. Paths in this file were rewritten. Shared `crates/suite-{core,ui,platform,file}`; Bricks uses `suite_core::AppKind`, `suite_ui::startup_message`, and `suite_file::document_name` (window title and crash list) and logs `INFO bricks: application starting` to stderr through `tracing`. Slate and Frame are terminal scaffolds. Edition stays 2021 (2024 is a separate change). No GUI toolkit is chosen for the suite; next: a comparative GUI/rendering prototype for all three apps.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-8:** a real workbook (the 2022 FIFA World Cup sample the user opened in v0.2.0) drew wrong in four ways, all fixed in the grid. (1) Its titles use Excel's Center Across Selection (`centerContinuous`) over B:M; Bricks centred them in B alone, so they spilled left into A and were cut at the window edge. Text so aligned now centres over the run of empty cells beside it with the same alignment (`across_run`, at most 256 columns each side; the cells keep their own fills and borders); like overflow, it draws only while its own cell is on screen. (2) The sheet has `showGridLines="0"`: `FileLayout::hide_grid_lines` reads the first `<sheetView>`, and such a sheet draws no grid lines (stripes stay). The patched save already copied `<sheetViews>` as written. (3) Text in a row barely taller than it (Excel's 14.4 pt rows with a fallback font a little taller than Calibri) was pushed 3 px down and cut at the bottom: `text_y`'s inset now shrinks to half the room left, and text taller than its row is cut on the side Excel cuts. Default-height rows draw exactly as before. (4) Wrapped headers broke words with hyphens (`Numb-`, `Appearan-`): Pango's default; Excel breaks without one, so wrapped layouts set `insert_hyphens` off. Also, fills snap to whole pixels: neighbours with fractional edges left a light seam between them.
  - Acceptance: `grid_view::tests` (text_y insets with room, in a 19.2 px row with a 17 px line, and taller than the row; `across_run` stops at cells that don't join and at the sheet edges, and is bounded); `crates/xlsx/tests/styles.rs` `grid_lines_off_and_centre_across_read_per_sheet` (rust_xlsxwriter book: one sheet hidden, one not; CenterAcross reads as `CenterContinuous`). `scripts/check_styles.sh` STYLES OK with new step 6 on a hand-built workbook: the title's glyphs sit at 501/1000 of the yellow B2:H2 run, and grid lines off changes 10,032 pixels; the v0.2.0 binary fails both (71, and 0 pixels). `styled_scroll` p99 9.48 ms (8 cores, while a GUI check ran).
  - Not Bricks: the user's machine has neither Calibri nor Carlito, so fontconfig draws Calibri in Liberation Sans, about 10% wider; headers that fit in Excel (`Player DOB`, `Appearances`) are cut. Installing Carlito (metric-compatible with Calibri) fixes it; Aptos has no metric-compatible free font.
- **2026-10-11 M5 gate:** approved by the user with two claims unproven: no real-world .xlsx with formulas on 3+ sheets was tried (the user's real workbook has one sheet and no formulas; it opens, saves, and reads back in LibreOffice identically but the edit), and the benchmark rows passed on the 8-core approximation, not the reference box. Both stay open as follow-ups for v0.2.x.
- **2026-10-11 check scripts:** a check could fail on a match. Each `check` ran `section | grep -q` under `pipefail`: `grep -q` exits at the first match, the section's writer then dies of SIGPIPE, and the pipeline fails. It showed in `check_styles.sh`, whose format section is long (one run in three); all eight scripts with that helper now let grep read the whole section.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-7:** copy and paste carry formats inside Bricks. The copy job keeps the block's cell styles (`CsvTable::copy_styles`, a `StyleBlock` of style ids, for up to `PASTE_MAX_CELLS` cells when the sheet has any styles) beside FML-12's formulas, keyed by the exact clipboard text; a paste of that text adds `CsvTable::paste_styles` (an `Edit::Styles` for the cells written, rows a paste adds at the end included) to the same `Batch`, so one Ctrl+Z takes text and styles back. A plain cell pasted over a styled one makes it plain, as in Excel. Text from anywhere else pastes as before and leaves the cells' formats alone. The HTML other programs read now carries each cell's look (`copy_range_styled`: bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, text colour, solid fill as inline CSS).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/commands/tests/paste_styles.rs` (a bold, filled B2 pasted at D5 gives D5 its style id and text, undo restores both, redo again; a plain copy clears italic cells it lands on; a paste past the last row adds a styled row; the HTML has `font-weight:bold;color:#c00000;background-color:#ffff00;`). `scripts/check_format.sh` FORMAT OK with step 5: the bold Data!A2 copied onto D5 turns D5's Bold on, one Ctrl+Z turns it off, and `wl-copy` text pasted on a bold D6 keeps it bold; the STYLE-6 binary fails the pasted-bold check.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-6:** editing formats (built in a parallel worktree). `CsvTable` holds the user's style layers (cell runs per column, row runs, column styles, by row and column ids) and `Edit::Styles` changes them with an exact inverse. `CsvTable::format_edit` turns a `StylePatch` into one undo step by cutting the selection at every layer boundary, so a whole column is a column style plus the cells that have their own style, as in Excel; an entry equal to what the layers beneath give is dropped, and equal pool records are reused (un-bolding returns a cell to the file's own style id). Whole rows and columns on sheets with more than 16,384 style runs are worked out in a job (bolding all 8 columns of styled_1m took 13 ms). The format bar (font, size, B/I/U/S, text and fill colour, borders, alignment, wrap, number formats with Custom…, merge, unmerge, clear), a right-click Format submenu, and Excel's shortcuts (Ctrl+B/I/U/5/1, Ctrl+Shift+1/3/4/5/~; none were bound) all go through it. A patched save rewrites only the restyled cells' `s` (rich text and shared strings survive); BRICKSJ3 journals carry formats, merges, and new style records (BRICKSJ2 and BRICKSJ1 still restore); a formatted CSV saves only as .xlsx. Border presets on whole columns treat the first and last rows as top and bottom.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_format.sh` FORMAT OK (Ctrl+B on Data!A2 then Ctrl+S gives A2 a bold font in the saved XML; Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y; a formatted CSV's Save As says it can't keep formatting and the CSV is untouched; kill -9 and Restore brings the bold back); the STYLE-4 binary fails 8 of 9. `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/tests/format.rs` (ignored LibreOffice part passes): bold, a fill, a column style, and a merge read back from both a patched and a fresh save. `apps/bricks/crates/commands/tests/format.rs`: 22 patches x 3 targets apply, undo, and redo exactly. `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/format.rs` (9) and journal tests (BRICKSJ3 round trip, a BRICKSJ2 fixture). On landing: check_xlsx, check_styles, check_format all OK on the integrated build. Scroll p99: styled_1m 7.22 ms, formulas 5.44 ms.
  - Follow-ups: no separate "No fill" button (Clear Formatting removes fills); shortcuts do nothing while the cell editor is open.
- **2026-10-11 BENCH-3:** three styled-XLSX rows in apps/bricks/docs/BENCHMARKS.md, measured by `cargo bench` on `corpus/styled_1m.xlsx` (`gen_corpus_xlsx --styled`: bold header on a fill, number formats per column, an italic theme-coloured column, a bold total row with a fill and a border every 100 rows): open 4.12 s, save after one edit 7.67 s (patched), scroll p99 11.1 ms, all within target on the 8-core/16 GB approximation (Ryzen 9 5900, not the reference box). "Save 1,048,576-row workbook as XLSX" now measures what the app does since STYLE-5: one edit, then a patched save (6.28 s). These runs shared the cores with a release build in another worktree, which shows in the scroll rows (formula scroll p99 9.2 ms vs 6.05 ms alone); the 1 GB scroll run timed out waiting for frames (a hidden window, as BENCH-2 noted; run alone it gives 7.32 ms p99, 60 fps). "Peak RAM after open" reads 1.24x (98 MiB anonymous, 31 MiB at BENCH-2): the STYLE-2 and STYLE-3 binaries give the same 93 MiB today, so it isn't these stories; watch it on the reference box.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-4:** merged cells (built in a parallel worktree, landed after STYLE-5). `CsvTable` holds the merged ranges (all-rows positions, sorted, from the file) and `Edit::Merges` replaces the list. Merge and Unmerge (new right-click entries, `grid.merge`/`grid.unmerge`) and the merges that follow row and column inserts and deletes (Excel's rules via `adjusted_merges`, in the structural edit's own undo step on both the formula and no-formula paths) are each one undoable operation. Merging asks before it empties other cells' text, absorbs merges it touches, waits for file order, and reads at most 65,536 cells (it reads on the UI thread). Sorting rows a merge covers is refused ("Unmerge cells before sorting"). `grid::Merges` keeps navigation toolkit-free: the cursor sits on a merge's anchor, keys step out from its far edge, and ranges cover merges whole. The grid draws a merge as one cell with the anchor's fill, text (a centred title centres across it), and borders, and the editor covers it. On landing, `xlsx::save` writes `CsvTable::merges()` (the table's current list) instead of mapping the file's.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/merges.rs` (12), `apps/bricks/crates/commands/tests/merges.rs` (2), five new grid navigation tests; `scripts/check_styles.sh` STYLES OK incl. step 5 (Right from Styles!A1 lands on E1, F8 selects E8, the title centres across A1:D1 at 226 per mille within 151..302, a row inserted above moves the merge); the STYLE-3 binary fails all four. `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/tests/patch.rs` saves the merge moved by a deleted row (E7:G8), now through `merges_after` as the app does. Scroll p99: styled_1m.xlsx 7.51 ms, formulas.csv 6.05 ms.
  - Follow-up: the recovery journal doesn't carry merges yet (STYLE-6 adds BRICKSJ3).
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-5:** saving an .xlsx patches the package it was read from (ADR 0010). Import copies the file to the cache directory (`xlsx::Package`, kept by the session, removed with it) and reads from the copy. `xlsx::save` (new `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/src/save/`) copies every part no change touches as compressed bytes; writes changed sheets by streaming the file's part (in file order: unchanged `<c>` elements copied as written, edited and formula cells written anew, rows the file lacks added; after inserts, deletes, or a sort: every row written from the model with its styles and row specs, and `sqref`/`ref`/`activeCell` ranges moved to where their rows and columns are); regenerates `<dimension>`, `<cols>` (file specs, column styles, user widths), and `<mergeCells>`; splices records the pool gained into `styles.xml` (save adds a `yyyy-mm-dd` format for a CSV date cell); rewrites the workbook (sheet list keeping chart sheets and attributes, defined names with `localSheetId` remapped and `_xlnm.` names following renames and deletions, `fullCalcOnLoad`), its relationships and content types (new and deleted sheets, calcChain dropped), and drops `docProps/app.xml` sheet titles when sheets changed. `.xlsm` keeps its macros under an `.xlsm` name (Ctrl+S now saves over it; Save As suggests `.xlsm`) and loses them, with the bin default and macro content type, as `.xlsx`. A workbook with no package is written from a minimal one by the same code. rust_xlsxwriter left the save path (it stays a dev-dependency of the corpus example); `export.rs`, `losses.rs`, the losses bar, and the save-over prompt are gone. LibreOffice can't read zips streamed with data descriptors, so `file_format::save_atomic` now hands writers a seekable file (`WriteSeek`). `GridView::user_sizes` gives the sizes that differ from the file's, in Excel's units.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/tests/patch.rs`: the fixture with edits on Data and Q1 Sales, Styles' row 4 deleted, Summary renamed Report, and a new sheet saves; its parts are the original's plus the new sheet; the chart, comments, drawings, theme, styles, and shared strings are byte-identical; values read back; styles and merges on Styles moved up a row (A1:D1, E7:G8); the conditional format, validation, and hyperlink ranges moved (`C7:C14`, `D7:D14`, `A17`); an unedited save copies every sheet part; an .xlsm keeps `vbaProject.bin` and an .xlsx drops it. Ignored LibreOffice test (passes): `lo_xlsx.py styles` and `dump` of the saved file match the original's with the deleted row gone and the edits. CSV-to-xlsx round trips of `rows_10mb.csv` and `corpus/nasty/*` stay cell-identical. `save_1m` with one edit: 6.75 s (target < 30 s); `import_1m` 3.82 s. The user's real workbook saved after one edit in 116 ms, and LibreOffice reads its first 60 rows' styles, merges, widths, and heights identically and its values identically but the edit. `scripts/check_xlsx.sh` XLSX OK (Ctrl+S saves without asking; the chart and comments stay); the STYLE-3 binary fails the save and restore steps.
  - Follow-ups: conditional-format and validation formulas, chart series references, table parts' ranges, and defined names don't follow row inserts and deletes; rich text in a sheet whose rows were reordered is written as plain text; shared formulas are written expanded in rewritten sheets.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-3:** the grid draws cell styles. `apps/bricks/src/looks.rs` resolves each style once into a `Look` (Pango font and decorations, text colour, fill, four border lines, alignment, wrap, indent, number format code); the body draws fills, then text, then borders, so text can overflow into empty neighbours (from visible columns only, as left, right, or centred text needs) and borders sit on top. Layouts are cached by font key, wrap width, and text; formatted text by code and text; both for two frames like the existing text layouts. A workbook's default font (cell format 0's) draws in Bricks' UI font; other fonts change family by name and scale size by points over the default's. General alignment right-aligns numbers and dates on every sheet, CSV included, and centres booleans and errors. A `*x` number-format fill splits the text to the two edges (the accounting format's padding). Explicit text colours and `[Red]`-style tags under 3:1 contrast against the cell's background fall back to the theme's colour; automatic text on a fill is black or white by the fill's luminance. Sizes: the most common file row height becomes the sheet's row height (Excel's default row maps to Bricks' 22 px), the file's default column width the sheet's column width (Excel's character width formula with Bricks' digit in place of 7 px), others by row and column id; `Sizes::set_all` lays them out in one pass. The accessibility description of the cursor cell shows its formatted text. `--bench-scroll` waits for an .xlsx import.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_styles.sh` STYLES OK: in a screenshot a number sits 105 px right of where text sits in the same column, long text spans 234 px across an empty neighbour, the fixture's five fills (incl. three theme tints) and red and blue text are drawn, and B8, B9, B10, B13 read `1,234.50`, `12.50%`, `(1,234)`, `Dec 9, 2025`; the STYLE-2 binary fails all 13 checks. `apps/bricks/tests/styled_scroll.rs` on `corpus/styled_1m.xlsx` (new `gen_corpus_xlsx --styled`): frame CPU p99 8.88 ms, 60.0 fps, 0 frames late (target < 16 ms). CSV scroll is unchanged: `rows_1024mb.csv` p99 6.73 ms vs 6.53 ms for the STYLE-2 binary on the same machine state (both higher than BENCH-2's 2.0 ms today: the machine, not the change). The user's real workbook renders its bold headers, accounting numbers right-aligned, and white sheet fill in light and dark themes.
  - Follow-ups: text rotation, shrink-to-fit, and superscript/subscript are kept but not drawn; merged cells draw as separate cells until STYLE-4; hidden rows and columns still show.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-2:** one Excel number format engine in `formula::numfmt` (built in a parallel worktree), used by the grid (`NumberFormat::parse` once, `display` per cell, about 180 ns) and TEXT (the old `functions/format.rs` is gone). Sections, conditions, the minus sign, and colour tags follow Excel's rules (only the first section adds a minus sign). `*x` fill comes back as `Formatted.fill` for the grid to spread (TEXT drops it, as LibreOffice does), `_x` is one space, and `?` pads with a plain space (LibreOffice uses U+2007). A value a format can't show (a date past 9999-12-31) fills the cell with `#`, as Excel does. 50 Excel overrides of LibreOffice: times round to the unit shown and carry (seven older TEXT cases changed this way), booleans show TRUE/FALSE under any format, `A/P` keeps its case, `%%` multiplies twice, `[$-F400]` is `h:mm:ss AM/PM`, and codes with bare letters, unclosed quotes or brackets, five sections, or `[Color57]` are refused. Still LibreOffice where Excel may differ: `#.##` of 12 shows `12`, `-0.001` under `0` shows `0`, unknown tags like `[DBNum1]` are ignored, serials before 1900-03-01 use the 1899-12-30 epoch.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/formula/tests/formats.rs`, 1,051 cases in `formats.tsv` recorded from LibreOffice by `scripts/lo_formats.py` (text and section colour), all matching; `--test libreoffice` still passes. A throwaway fuzz run (300k random codes x 12 values) found no panics. The user's accounting code shows ` 1,234,567 `, ` (42)`, ` -   ` with the fill after the first char.
- **2026-10-11 STYLE-1:** XLSX import reads styles. `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/src/styles.rs` parses `xl/styles.xml` (number formats, fonts, fills, borders, `cellXfs`, indexed colours; `cellStyleXfs` and `dxfs` are not cell formats) and the theme's colour scheme into `data_model::StylePool` under the file's indices. `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/src/layout.rs` streams each sheet part a second time with quick-xml 0.41 (new direct dependency, already in the tree via calamine), on its own thread beside calamine's value pass, reading only `<c s>`, `<row>`, `<col>`, `<sheetFormatPr>`, and `<mergeCell>` into `FileStyles` (cell runs per column, row styles, column styles; cells whose style equals their row's or column's aren't stored) and `FileLayout` (column specs, row specs as runs, merges). `CsvTable::style_of` looks styles up by id, so they follow sorts and inserts. Theme tints use float HLS, which matches LibreOffice exactly (Excel's palette differs by one or two per channel); LibreOffice maps indexed colour 22 to white where Excel's palette has C0C0C0, so indexed colours are tested against Excel's palette in a unit test only.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/tests/styles.rs` compares every used cell of the four fixture sheets (now with a `Styles` sheet built by `scripts/lo_xlsx.py`, post-processed to use theme colours with tints) to `fixture-styles-libreoffice.txt` from the new `lo_xlsx.py styles` dump: font name, size, bold, italic, underline, strike, colour; solid fill; four border sides; alignment, wrap, rotation, indent; number format (normalized for LibreOffice's locale tags and escapes). Merges, hidden rows and columns, row heights (pt vs 1/100 mm within 0.02 mm), and column widths (as multiples of the default width, within 1%) match. A wrong theme index mapping fails it. `import_1m`: 4.07 s (was 3.33 s; target < 10 s), peak RSS 60 MiB. The user's real workbook (12,571 rows) reads 13 cell formats, 55 runs, 3 row-spec runs, 4+ merges in 81 ms. `check_xlsx.sh` still XLSX OK with the fourth sheet. Nothing visible changes yet (STYLE-3 draws them).
- **2026-10-11 decision:** XLSX styles are in M5 before v0.2.0 (user decision): keep them on save, show them in the grid, and edit them. Save patches the original package instead of rewriting it, so charts, images, comments, conditional formats, validation, pivots, and macros survive. Design in ADRs 0009 (style pool, per-column runs, Excel's cell/row/column layering, display rules incl. dark-mode contrast and General right-alignment) and 0010 (patching, built-in template for CSV workbooks, rust_xlsxwriter removed). Supersedes XLSX-2's behaviour: STYLE-5 removes the losses bar and the save-over prompt, since nothing is dropped any more. Triggered by a real workbook (Arial 16 bold title, merges, accounting number format) that v0.1.1 couldn't open at all.
- **2026-10-10 BENCH-2:** six XLSX and formula rows in apps/bricks/docs/BENCHMARKS.md, each measured by `cargo bench` (`apps/bricks/benches/corpus.rs`): the XLSX open runs `bricks corpus/rows_1m.xlsx --bench-open`, whose OPENED line now waits for the import (`!is_loading()`) and reports `"sheets"`; the formula scroll runs `--bench-scroll 600` on `corpus/formulas.csv`; save, SUM, running total, and VLOOKUP run in-process as the app's jobs do. Missing `formulas.csv` or `rows_1m.xlsx` prints which generator to run. Under `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=16G taskset -c 0-7` (Ryzen 9 5900, not the reference box), all six pass: open XLSX 3.69 s, save XLSX 8.19 s, formula scroll p99 2.1 ms, SUM over 1 GB 3.77 s, running total 3.75 s, 10,000 VLOOKUPs 0.24 s.
  - The running total first measured 10.98 s: every single-cell read rescanned up to 63 rows from its index checkpoint. Formula evaluation now reads cells through a per-sheet `SpanCache` (256 direct-mapped blocks of 64 row spans, cleared with the other results on each revision), and `CsvTable::cell_cached` takes spans from it; 3.75 s after.
  - Regression note: "Save with 1,000 edits" measured 2.93 s and then 1.14 s in these runs against 0.93 s recorded at BENCH-1 (the CSV save path is unchanged in M5, and the two runs differ 2.6×, which points at btrfs fsync variance; still far under 15 s). Re-measure on the reference box.
- **2026-10-10 FML-12:** copy and paste formulas inside Bricks. The copy job, besides the TSV of results, collects the block's formula cells (raw text, offset in the block, position among all rows) when the block has at most `PASTE_MAX_CELLS` cells; `GridView::formula_clip` keeps them with the exact text copied. A paste whose clipboard text equals that text puts each formula, shifted by `formula::shift_formula` by how far it moved (rows by position among all rows, so sorted or filtered views shift by file rows), into the same paste batch: one undo step. Any other clipboard text pastes as values, so text copied into LibreOffice gets results. A reference shifted above row 1 or left of column A becomes `#REF!`; `$` parts stay.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/formula/tests/shift.rs` (=A2*2 → =A3*2, `$A$2` stays, off the top → `=#REF!`); `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/paste_libreoffice.rs` ignored case: a copied formula cell pastes into LibreOffice as `value:` with its result (pass, with `--test-threads=1`); `scripts/check_formula_bar.sh` FORMULA BAR OK with step 7: B2 (=A2*2) copied and pasted on B3 shows `=A3*2` in the formula bar; the FML-11 binary fails it (it pastes the value 4).
- **2026-10-10 FML-11:** sort, filter, find, replace, type inference, and copy read formula results. Every such job gets its table through `GridView::job_table`: a snapshot with the sheet's `Computed` results, reused while the workbook revision and sheet match, else computed at the start of the job itself (no extra round trip or busy state; the sort, filter, search, or replace job already holds edits back). Inference does the same, so it never blocks edits. Replace never edits formula cells: replace all skips them and the find bar says "Replaced N cells · skipped K formula cells"; Replace on a formula cell goes on to the next match with "Formula cells aren't replaced · 3 of 7". `Matches::replace_all` returns the skipped count (built in a parallel worktree).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/formulas.rs` (`jobs_see_results_sort_filter_infer`: a `=10-B{n}` column sorts by result, a `> 5` number filter keeps rows with results 9, 8, 7, 6, inference says Integer; `find_copy_and_replace_see_results`: "8" finds the result cell, copy's TSV holds results, replace all changes 2 value cells and skips 1 formula, only-formula matches give none and 1 skipped). `scripts/check_formulas.sh` FORMULAS OK with step 6: Ctrl+F 4 lands on B2 (`=A2*2`, result 4); the FML-10 binary fails it.
- **2026-10-10 FML-10:** formulas follow inserted and deleted rows and columns, and sorted rows. `Workbook::structure_rewrites` and `CsvTable::sort_rewrites` were built in a parallel worktree; the grid runs a structure edit with formulas anywhere as "Updating formulas" on a snapshot, then makes the edit and its rewrites one step with the same label as before (`Reshape::label_of`); without formulas the path is the old synchronous one. A sort computes its rewrites in the sort job and applies them in the same "Sort" batch. Rewrites above 2,097,152 cells are refused with the reason.
  - Semantics: references address positions among all rows (ADR 0006), so an insert shifts every reference at or past it, a range grows when the insert falls inside it and shrinks on a delete, a reference into a deleted row is `#REF!`, and references from other sheets to the edited one follow too. A filtered delete (several ranges) applies highest range first. Sorting shifts the moved formula's relative row references by how far its row moved, as Excel does; references from other rows or sheets aren't rewritten.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/structure.rs` (insert at position 4: `=SUM(A1:A10)` → `=SUM(A1:A11)`, `=A5` → `=A6`, `=$A$5` → `=$A$6`, `=A3` stays, Sheet2's `=Sheet1!A5` follows; delete: `=A5` → `=#REF!`, `=SUM(A1:A10)` → `=SUM(A1:A9)`; columns mirror rows; a filtered two-range delete; a 1,000-row sort keeps each `=B{n}*2` reading its own row and the inverses restore every text). `scripts/check_structure.sh` STRUCTURE OK (insert above row 2: `=SUM(A1:A4)`, the moved `=A3*10` still shows 20; one Ctrl+Z restores; delete row 2: `=SUM(A1:A2)` and `=#REF!`, Ctrl+Z restores); the FML-9 binary fails the five rewrite checks.
- **2026-10-10 FML-9:** a formula bar and name box over the grid (`apps/bricks/src/formula_bar.rs`). The name box shows the selection as references count rows (`B2`, `B2:D9`, `A:C`, `2:5`) and goes to what is typed there, switching sheets for `Sheet2!B3`; the bar shows the cursor cell's raw text (its formula), and Enter sets the cell through the same path as the in-cell editor (`GridView::apply_text`: formula check, edge-row growth, one undo step). A note under them says why a formula is refused ("Formula error at character N: …", which replaces FML-4's alert, also for the in-cell editor), "No sheet named that", "Not a cell reference", or "Result from the file: Bricks doesn't compute BAHTTEXT yet" for an unedited XLSX formula shown from its cached result (`Workbook::uses_cached`). Ctrl+G and F5 focus the name box; Escape in either entry goes back to the grid. New GridView signal `changed` (after edits and sheet switches) keeps the bar current.
  - Decisions: rows a reference names beyond the editable edge row go to the edge row; columns aren't cut (after a sheet switch the loaded width is unknown until the next frame). A row hidden by a filter, or the header row, is refused with "That row isn't shown".
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_formula_bar.sh` FORMULA BAR OK: B2 shows `=A2*2` in the bar and `B2` in the name box; Ctrl+G A2 goes there; `=A2*5` in the bar makes B2 10 and one Ctrl+Z makes it 4; `=A2+` is refused at character 5; `Nope!A1` says "No sheet named that"; `t!B2` from another sheet goes back to B2. `scripts/check_xlsx.sh` gains: Summary!A3 (`=BAHTTEXT(1)`) shows LibreOffice's cached text and the note naming BAHTTEXT. The SHEET-2 binary fails every check but the refused formula, whose message FML-4's alert already showed. `scripts/check_formulas.sh` still FORMULAS OK with the message in the note.
- **2026-10-10 SHEET-2:** renaming a sheet rewrites the references to it (`='Q1 Sales'!B2` → `='Q2'!B2`; a name that reads as a cell is quoted, as Excel writes it), and deleting one turns them into `#REF!`; each is one undo step with the sheet change. `Workbook::sheet_rewrites` (built in a parallel worktree) walks every sheet's formula cells; the grid runs it as an "Updating formulas" job on a workbook snapshot (edits wait for it, as for a sort) and makes the change when it is done, or at once when no sheet has formulas. More than 2,097,152 rewritten cells is refused with "This would rewrite N formulas; Bricks rewrites at most 2,097,152 at once." (new `refused` signal).
  - Decision: tab actions don't wait for a running formula scan; the rewrite job scans the snapshot's unscanned sheets itself, so `busy()` doesn't grow to cover scans (edits stay possible while a large file is scanned).
  - Not rewritten: defined names (the workbook's list, not table edits). Undo of a rename shows the renamed sheet (the step's sheet, ADR 0008).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/sheets.rs` (a cross-sheet reference reads its cell; rename rewrites it and still shows 7, case-insensitively, unqualified `=B2` untouched; delete gives `=#REF!` showing #REF!; the inverses restore sheet and text; cancel stops). `scripts/check_sheets.sh` section 6: `=t!A2*10` shows 20, renaming t to Data keeps 20, Ctrl+Z gives back t and the formula still shows 20; the SHEET-1 binary fails "renaming keeps the reference working".
- **2026-10-10 XLSX-2:** an imported workbook that holds what Bricks drops on save shows a bar over the grid ("Bricks shows this workbook's values, formulas, and sheets. Saving will drop: cell formatting, 1 chart, comments, merged cells."), and saving over it (Ctrl+S, the close prompt's Save, or Save As onto the same file) asks first: Save (not asked again for this file until it is read again), Save As Copy…, or Cancel. An .xlsm file is never saved over: Ctrl+S opens Save As set to .xlsx with "Bricks can't keep macros: save as .xlsx", and choosing the .xlsm itself is refused. Built after SHEET-1 rather than after SHEET-2 (it depends only on XLSX-1).
  - `xlsx::losses::scan` (built in a parallel worktree) reads part names and streams worksheet XML through `memmem` with a carry-over between 64 KiB reads. Rules beyond the plan: LibreOffice writes bold as `<b val="true"/>`, so `val="true"` forms of b, i, and strike count; number formats `General` and `@` don't (LibreOffice declares them in every file).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/tests/losses.rs` (the LibreOffice fixture gives exactly cell formatting, 1 chart, comments, merged cells; a workbook Bricks saved from `rows_10mb.csv` gives none; a marker split across reads is found). `scripts/check_xlsx.sh` XLSX OK with the new checks: the bar names all four, Ctrl+S asks "Save over “f.xlsx”?", Cancel leaves the file's mtime and size unchanged, Save then writes it. The SHEET-1 binary fails all six new checks.
- **2026-10-10 SHEET-1:** add, rename, move, and delete sheets, each one undoable step (`GridView::add_sheet`, `rename_sheet`, `move_sheet`, `delete_sheet`, each a `SheetEdit`). The tab strip ends with an Add Sheet button (Shift+F11 too); right-clicking a tab offers Rename…, Move Left, Move Right, Delete (Delete off for the only sheet, Move off at the ends); double-click or F2 on a tab renames it in a popover that names Excel's rule a name breaks and stays open.
  - Decisions: the grid tracks whose cursor, scroll, and sizes it holds (`ui_sheet`), so an undo that adds or removes a sheet shows the active one as it was (`follow_active`); undo first shows the step's sheet if it exists, and a redone Add shows the sheet it brings back. A removed sheet's view state is kept for its undo. Renaming a CSV file's only sheet counts as an unsaved change but isn't saved (a CSV file has no sheet names).
  - Keyboard: Ctrl+Tab moves the focus from the grid to the tabs (GTK's focus chain), arrows between them. GTK 4's AT-SPI has no GrabFocus, so the check drives this path rather than focusing through the accessibility tree.
  - Follow-up (pre-existing, not changed): committing an empty editor on the edge row adds an empty row (SAVE-2's paste path).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/commands/tests/sheets.rs` (add, edit, rename, move, delete; each undo and redo gives back the sheet list and every sheet's edits exactly; the deleted sheet's edits return with it; 17 name-rule cases with their messages). `scripts/check_sheets.sh` SHEETS OK: Shift+F11 twice gives tabs t, Sheet1, Sheet2; F2 renames Sheet1 to Q1 Sales; Ctrl+Z renames it back and Ctrl+Z again takes Sheet2 away; each sheet keeps its own cursor; Ctrl+S with two sheets opens Save As saying "A CSV file holds one sheet" and saves t.xlsx with both sheets (a CSV saves as .xlsx through the app). The XLSX-1 binary fails every positive check.
- **2026-10-10 XLSX-1:** .xlsx open and save, sheet tabs, and workbook crash recovery. The `xlsx` crate (calamine 0.36, rust_xlsxwriter 0.99 with `constant_memory`, zip 8 with deflate only) and journal v2 were built in parallel worktrees and cherry-picked; the app side ties them in.
  - Import streams each worksheet's cells (`next_cell_with_formula`) into a comma/LF CSV copy in the cache directory (`csv_engine::scratch_path`), mapped and unlinked; formulas are normalized through parse/render (no `_xlfn.`), and the file's cached results kept for functions Bricks lacks. Cells out of row-major order fall back to calamine's dense reader up to 50,000,000 cells. Export writes numbers, booleans, and dates only when their text is canonical, so import(export(raw)) == raw; `YYYY-MM-DD 00:00:00` stays text (a midnight date-time would read back as a date), and dates go through `ExcelDateTime::from_ymd` so those before 1900-03-01 match calamine. `render_xlsx` also prefixes BAHTTEXT (LibreOffice reads the bare name as #NAME?). `file_format::save_atomic`'s writer is `dyn Write + Send`; `SaveProgress::add_written` counts rows for .xlsx saves.
  - App: `open_session` sniffs the file (`xlsx::kind`): .xls, encrypted, and .ods files are refused with what to do; an .xlsx window says "Opening…" while the import job runs (status "Opening N%", Esc cancels and closes). Tabs under the grid (one per sheet; Ctrl+Page Down/Up). Each sheet keeps its cursor, scroll, and sizes (`SheetUi`); types and formula scans follow the sheet they ran on. Save As gains Format (CSV or Excel workbook); delimiter and encoding rows show for CSV only. An .xlsx save computes every sheet's results for the file's cached values, then reads the saved file back ("Checking the saved file…") and shows it, keeping the active sheet, each sheet's header row and column types by position, and the active sheet's filters (other sheets' filters go: not undoable state, like after a CSV save). The delimiter dropdown shows for CSV sheets only; the malformed-file check runs on CSV sheets only; the status bar names "Excel workbook".
  - Journal v2 (`BRICKSJ2`): every sheet's id, name, origin, header row, and edits; `restore_plan` turns it into one "Restore unsaved edits" step (inserted, removed, renamed, moved sheets and their edits). v0.1.x journals (`BRICKSJ1`) still restore (committed fixture `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/data/v1.journal`).
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/xlsx/tests/import_export.rs`: the LibreOffice fixture (`scripts/lo_xlsx.py fixture`) matches LibreOffice's dump (sheet names, values, text, 16 formulas, results, BAHTTEXT through its cached result); edit, save, re-import keeps every cell; `rows_10mb.csv` and every `corpus/nasty` file round-trip as .xlsx with identical cell text (a CSV saves as .xlsx); ignored `libreoffice_reads_save`: LibreOffice reads the save back identically. `scripts/check_xlsx.sh` XLSX OK (tabs Data, Q1 Sales, Summary; Ctrl+Page Down shows `A1: 25.5`; 42 saved into sheet2.xml and shown after the read-back; kill -9 with edits on two sheets restores both); the FML-8 binary fails all four. `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/journal.rs` (3-sheet round trip, v1 journal, corrupt journals). Perf (release, this machine): `import_1m` 1,048,576 rows in 3.33 s, peak RSS 361 MiB (target < 10 s); `save_1m` 6.23 s (target < 30 s).
- **2026-10-10 FML-8:** date and time functions (`functions/dates.rs`). A `Date` whose integer part is 0 shows as `HH:MM:SS`; `Date + Date` is a `Date` (`DATE(2024,1,1)+TIME(6,0,0)` shows `2024-01-01 06:00:00`). DATEVALUE/TIMEVALUE read ISO dates, M/D/Y, D-Mon-Y, and "Mon D, YYYY", with times and AM/PM. Built in a parallel worktree from FML-3 and rebased after FML-7.
  - Bricks follows LibreOffice: years 0–49 are 2000–2049 and 50–99 are 1950–1999 (Excel adds 1900); years from 100 up to 1582-10-15 are #VALUE!; years up to 32767; serials before 1900-03-01 use the 1899-12-30 epoch (`DATE(1900,1,1)` is 2); TIME keeps fractional arguments; HOUR/MINUTE/SECOND of a negative serial use its fraction; EDATE/EOMONTH truncate the start; DAYS keeps fractions; DATEDIF "MD" can go negative; NETWORKDAYS skips text in a holidays range.
  - Bricks follows Excel (fixture overrides): LibreOffice's Err:502 (bad DATE/TIME arguments, WEEKDAY/WEEKNUM types, DATEDIF order or unit, unreadable DATEVALUE/TIMEVALUE text, text holidays in WORKDAY) is #NUM! or #VALUE!.
  - Acceptance: 135 FML-8 rows match LibreOffice or their Excel override; TODAY and NOW by properties in `tests/volatile_dates.rs`.
- **2026-10-10 FML-7:** lookup and reference functions (`functions/lookup.rs`) and `Context::exact_index` (default `None`; the workbook builds an `ExactIndex` per sheet, area, and direction once per revision and caches it in `Calc`). Built in a parallel worktree from FML-3 and rebased after FML-6.
  - Decisions: exact matches use exact numeric equality (LibreOffice's `MATCH(0.1+0.2,{0.1,0.3},0)` is #N/A); approximate matches binary-search over values of the target's type; an empty argument means FALSE or 0 (`VLOOKUP(x,t,2,)` is exact), as in LibreOffice.
  - Bricks follows Excel (fixture overrides): an index past the table is #REF!, below 1 #VALUE!; `"5"` never matches the number 5; wildcards apply to array constants in VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP; MATCH over a 2-D area is #N/A; XLOOKUP/XMATCH shape and mode errors are #VALUE!; CHOOSE and INDEX out of range are #VALUE!. As the plan says, INDEX with row or column 0 and XLOOKUP binary-search modes ±2 are #VALUE!; a multi-column XLOOKUP result is #VALUE! (no spilling).
  - Acceptance: 147 FML-7 rows match LibreOffice or their Excel override. `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/lookup_speed.rs` (release): one VLOOKUP over 1,000,000 rows 219.76 ms (target < 1 s), 10,000 exact VLOOKUPs into 1,000,000 rows 251.64 ms (target < 2 s); this machine, not the reference box.
- **2026-10-10 FML-6:** text functions and TEXT format codes (`functions/text.rs`, `functions/format.rs` with `format::format(v, code)`). Built in a parallel worktree from FML-3 and rebased after FML-5.
  - Bricks follows Excel (fixture overrides): `CONCAT("a",TRUE)` is `aTRUE`; CHAR/CODE use Windows-1252 for 128–159 and Unicode otherwise (LibreOffice on Linux uses UTF-8, so `CHAR(233)` is U+FFFD there); `CHAR(0)` is #VALUE!; REPT stops at Excel's 32,767 characters; `TEXT(1E20,"General")` is `1E+20`; TEXTJOIN needs 3 arguments; LibreOffice's Err:502 for bad counts and positions is #VALUE!.
  - Bricks follows LibreOffice: FIND/SEARCH with empty find text are #VALUE!; `CODE("")` is 0; CLEAN also removes 127–159; CONCAT() of nothing is ""; `TEXT(12,"#.##")` is `12`; TEXT cuts seconds after 15-digit rounding and writes AM/PM in upper case; `TEXT(-1,"yyyy")` is 1899.
  - Acceptance: 328 FML-6 rows (130 for TEXT, covering every token) match LibreOffice or their Excel override.
- **2026-10-10 FML-5:** math, statistics, logic, and information functions (`functions/math.rs`, `stats.rs`, `logic.rs`, `info.rs`, shared `criteria.rs` and `wildcard.rs`). Built in a parallel worktree from FML-3 and rebased after FML-4.
  - Decisions: SUMPRODUCT evaluates element-wise (`SUMPRODUCT((A1:A3>1)*B1:B3)` works). Criteria cover every position of the range, so `""` and `"<>x"` count cells the area never visits. ROUND, INT, TRUNC, CEILING, FLOOR, MROUND, QUOTIENT, and MOD round to 15 significant digits first, as LibreOffice does (`ROUND(2.675,2)` is 2.68).
  - Bricks follows Excel (fixture overrides): booleans in references are skipped by SUM, COUNT, SMALL, and match only boolean criteria; number text and booleans typed as arguments are coerced (`SUM("3",TRUE)` is 4); `ISNUMBER(TRUE)` is FALSE; CEILING/FLOOR are Excel's (LibreOffice's CEILING.XCL/FLOOR.XCL), so `FLOOR(5,0)` is #DIV/0!; LibreOffice's Err:502/504 become #NUM!, #DIV/0!, or #VALUE! as Excel gives them.
  - Bricks follows LibreOffice: MEDIAN with no numbers, MODE with no repeat, and LARGE/SMALL out of range are #VALUE!; `MROUND(10,-3)` is 9; `FACT(171)` is #VALUE!; RANDBETWEEN rounds both bounds up; LARGE/SMALL round a fractional k up; RANK needs an exact match.
  - Acceptance: 389 FML-5 rows in `apps/bricks/crates/formula/tests/data/cases.tsv` match LibreOffice (or their Excel override); RAND and RANDBETWEEN by properties in `tests/volatile.rs`.
- **2026-10-10 FML-4:** the grid owns a `Workbook` and an `UndoStack<Workbook>` (ADR 0008); table commands run through `commands::OnSheet`, and `commands::SheetEdit` runs `BookEdit`s (used from XLSX-1 on). `Command` gains `sheet()`, `UndoStack` gains `peek_undo`/`peek_redo`. Drawing reads through `ShownSheet`; cells over the frame's budget show `…` and a "Calculating" job fills them. A typed formula that doesn't parse stays in the editor and the window says "Formula error at character N: …" (FML-9 moves this to the formula bar); leaving the editor by clicking elsewhere keeps it there quietly, so the alert can't loop through focus changes. After indexing, a "Scanning for formulas" job finds the file's formula cells.
  - The row gutter shows file row numbers (the header row is 1, drawn in the corner), so `A1` is what the user sees (ADR 0006). Under a filter it shows each row's position among all rows, not a renumbering.
  - Accessibility: the grid's role is Grid (GTK's generic role carries no description) and its description is the cursor cell and what it shows (`B2: 4`); `scripts/a11y_helpers.py` reads descriptions and clears AT-SPI's cache first, since descriptions change as the cursor moves.
  - `corpus/formulas.csv` (1,000,000 rows: `=B{n}*C{n}`, a running total, `=IF(...)`) from `scripts/gen_corpus.py`; its `band` column shows `#NAME?` until FML-5's IF.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_formulas.sh` FORMULAS OK (B2 shows 4; typed `=A2*3` shows 6; Ctrl+Z empties it; `=A2+` is refused "at character 5"; the saved file keeps `=A2*2`); the FML-3 binary fails all five. `apps/bricks/tests/formula_scroll.rs`: frame CPU p99 2.75 ms over 600 frames scrolling from row 500,000 (target < 16 ms; this machine, not the reference box). The first frame mid-file spends the 200,000-visit budget (max 354 ms) before the job takes over.
- **2026-10-10 FML-3:** data-model gains `Workbook` (sheets, active sheet, defined names, the clock, the calculation cache), `BookEdit`, the calculation driver (`value_of`, `evaluate_all`, `store_values`, `computed_for`), `ShownSheet` (the active sheet with results, under a 200,000-visit budget), `FormulaCells` (`scan_formulas`), and `Computed` (results on a job's snapshot, read by `file_cell`, `read_source`, `read_inserted`, sort, filter, and search; never by `cell_value`, save, or the journal).
  - `SUM` is implemented here, with FML-5's aggregate rules and Neumaier summation, because this story's acceptance needs it; FML-5 adds the rest.
  - Decisions: `Context::size` gives a sheet 16,384 columns (Excel's width) for whole-row references; areas visit only cells that exist. The driver knows each formula's position from where it was reached (grid row, reference, or one `row_positions` pass for job cell lists), because `position_of` is O(rows) on a sorted table. `RAND()` is seeded per cell and revision. `set_now` drops cached results.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/data-model/tests/formulas.rs`: `=B2*2` and `=SUM(B2:B3)` show 4 and 7, an edit makes them 6 and 8, the save keeps the raw formulas; parse errors show as text; over-budget cells show `…` and a job's `evaluate_all` fills them; `scan_formulas` matches a naive scan on `corpus/nasty/*` and a 20,000-row generated file; `computed_for` makes `sort_rows` order by result; a 200,000-row running total caches every row; cycles show #CIRC!. Ignored `sum_1gb`: `=SUM(rows!D:D)` over `rows_1024mb.csv` (19,094,579 rows) in 3.71 s (target < 5 s; this machine, not the reference box).
- **2026-10-10 FML-2:** `formula` gains values (`Value`, `literal`, `result_text`), coercion and comparison (`coerce.rs`), `evaluate` over a `Context`, and the function registry (`ROW`, `COLUMN`, `TRUE`, `FALSE` so far).
  - Oracle: `scripts/lo_functions.py` fills each case's inputs on a fresh sheet of a headless Calc (IgnoreCase on, wildcards on, as Excel compares) and records G1's result in `apps/bricks/crates/formula/tests/data/cases-libreoffice.tsv`; it also carries the inputs, so the test needs only that file. 261 `ops` cases.
  - Decision: `Context::area` visits only non-empty cells, and its visitor returns false to stop (lookups end at the first match).
  - Where Bricks follows LibreOffice rather than Excel: `0^0` is 1 and `0^-1` is #NUM!; a negative base takes odd roots (`(-8)^(1/3)` is -2); a power that underflows to 0 is #NUM!; `+`/`-` cancel to exactly 0 within 2^-48 and `=` compares to 2^-48 relative (`0.1+0.2=0.3` is TRUE, `0.1+0.2-0.3` is 0); text in arithmetic reads as LibreOffice reads it (`" 1 "`, `"1,000.5"`, `"$5"`, `"(5)"`, `"5-"`, `"- 5"`, `"12:30"`, `"25:00"`, ISO dates).
  - Where Bricks follows Excel (cases with an `excel` column naming the result and why; the test fails if LibreOffice starts agreeing): booleans are their own type, so `TRUE()&""` is `TRUE`, `TRUE()=1` is FALSE, TRUE sorts after text, and `"TRUE"+0` is #VALUE! (LibreOffice treats booleans as 1 and 0); numbers as text use Excel's General format (`1E+15`, `1E-10`; LibreOffice writes `1000000000000000`, `1E+020`); `{#N/A,1}+1` is #N/A (LibreOffice rejects error literals in arrays); `ROW(1)` is #VALUE! (LibreOffice's Err:504 has no Excel value).
  - Acceptance: `cargo test -p formula --test libreoffice` (261 cases), `--test deep` (a 1,000,000-row `=A{n}+1` chain through a stack-based driver on an 8 MiB thread, 4.5 s in debug; a self-reference and a 500-cell cycle give #CIRC!).
- **2026-10-10 FML-1:** new crate `apps/bricks/crates/formula` (no dependencies): `parse`, `render`/`render_xlsx`, the AST, references (`column_name` moved here from the app, `column_index`, `parse_reference`, `format_reference`, `adjust`, `names_sheet`), and `scalar` (the number/ISO date grammars moved from `data-model/src/infer.rs`, plus serial dates on LibreOffice's 1899-12-30 epoch).
  - Decisions: a range is stored with its corners ordered (`B5:A1` renders `A1:B5`, as Excel stores it); `A1:A1` stays a range. Sheet names that read as a cell (`Q2`, `A1`) are quoted when rendered (`'Q2'!B2`), as Excel writes them; unquoted they still parse. Numbers of magnitude ≥ 1e16 or < 1e-6 render with an exponent (`1E+20`). "unclosed string" and "unclosed parenthesis" point at the opening quote or parenthesis.
  - Acceptance: `apps/bricks/crates/formula/tests/grammar.rs`: 95 parse→render→parse cases, 25 error cases with their character and message, adjustment cases for every `Change` rule, column names 0..20,000 round-trip.
- **2026-10-10 decision:** M5 is XLSX, formulas, and multiple sheets (user decision), replacing invariant 8's V0.1 non-goals for those three. Design in ADRs 0006 (workbook model), 0007 (formula engine), 0008 (sheets and undo). User choices: own engine on Bricks' model; XLSX save writes values, formulas, sheets and drops the rest with warnings; CSV cells starting with = evaluate; a CSV with 2+ sheets saves as .xlsx; core ~120 functions; formula bar with name box.
- **2026-10-10 PKG-4:** the app is Bricks now: command `bricks`, the window title, the app menu entry, dialogs ("Bricks closed unexpectedly…"), `--version`, and log lines (`bricks: …`).
  - Decided with the user: a full rename (not just the command), shipped as v0.1.1.
  - App ID `dev.bricks.Spreadsheet` → `dev.bricks.Bricks`. The desktop entry and icon take the app ID's name (`dev.bricks.Bricks.desktop` and `.svg`), so Wayland docks match the window to its entry. The old `spreadsheet.desktop` never matched `dev.bricks.Spreadsheet`. `GenericName=Spreadsheet` keeps it findable in app menus.
  - Cargo package and binary: `bricks` (`cargo run -p bricks`). Tarball: `bricks-X.Y.Z-x86_64-linux.tar.gz`. Installer override: `BRICKS_DOWNLOAD_URL`.
  - State folder: `~/.local/state/bricks`. `apps/bricks/src/state.rs` resolves it once for recent files and recovery journals; on first use it moves `~/.local/state/spreadsheet` over if only the old one exists, so recent files and unrestored crash edits survive the upgrade. If both exist, the bricks one wins and the old one is left alone.
  - Installer: installing or uninstalling removes v0.1.0's binary, desktop entry, icon, and `text/csv=spreadsheet.desktop` defaults. It only does so when `spreadsheet.desktop` is the one it wrote (`Exec=~/.local/bin/spreadsheet`), so another program called `spreadsheet` is left alone. An upgrade reports "Updated Bricks 0.1.0 -> X".
  - Not renamed: the R2 bucket `spreadsheet-releases` and the Pages project `spreadsheet` (`wrangler.toml`). These are Cloudflare names, and renaming the project would change its pages.dev address under the bricks.cronkite.cc domain. The word "spreadsheet" stays where it means the kind of program.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_install.sh` gains an upgrade scenario, run in GTK containers on Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora (INSTALL OK on all three):
    - The real v0.1.0 is installed with its own installer (from the `v0.1.0` tag) and opens a CSV.
    - The new installer then reports "Updated Bricks 0.1.0 -> 9.9.9". The old binary, entry, icon, and CSV defaults are gone. `text/csv` opens `dev.bricks.Bricks.desktop`.
    - `gio open` starts Bricks. Its recent list holds the new file and v0.1.0's, and `~/.local/state` holds only `bricks`.
    - Negative control: the old installer fails 13 of the 15 checks. The 2 it passes are the v0.1.0 setup.
  - `apps/bricks/src/state.rs` unit tests: the old folder moves over with its contents; an existing bricks folder wins and the old one is left alone.
- **2026-10-10 APP-8:** clear errors for malformed files. A dialog names the line and shows its text.
  - Decided with the user: report a quote that never closes, text after a closing quote (`"ab"c`), and binary data. A file with quote problems opens as read, then warns; a binary file is refused.
  - `data-model/src/check.rs`: `CsvTable::check_file` runs once a reading is indexed, on the job pool, with cancel. It parses rows by the same RFC 4180 rules as the index.
    - Only rows holding a quote or a zero byte are parsed (one `memchr2` per 65,536-row chunk). A clean 1 GB file takes 83 ms (`checking_1gb`, ignored, release).
    - Results come in file order: the first 5 problems with line number, file row and excerpt, plus a total count. Line numbers count every newline, so they match a text editor even after quoted newlines.
    - Excerpts run from the line's start, are cut at 60 characters, and show control characters escaped (`\0`, `\t`, `\x03`).
  - `CsvTable::binary_at_start` looks for a zero byte in the first 64 KiB. `open_session` refuses such files with "Cannot open …: It holds binary data, not text. Line 1 has a zero byte: “PK\x03\x04\0\0…”". Re-reads (save, delimiter change) don't refuse.
  - `apps/bricks/src/malformed.rs` shows the dialog: "“x.csv” has malformed lines", the first 3 problems as "Line 70,002: text follows a closing quote:" with the line's text under it, then "…and 4 more problems", and "The file is shown as read. Rows you don't edit are saved unchanged."
    - Buttons: Go to Line N (`GridView::go_to_file_row`, which follows sorts and filters) and Close (the default).
    - It runs once per window for each delimiter and encoding. A save re-reads the file and keeps unedited rows byte for byte, so it would only repeat itself. A delimiter change checks again.
    - It waits while APP-7's restore offer is pending.
  - Fixed along the way: a file named on the command line that couldn't be opened printed to stderr and exited with no window, so a file manager's double-click showed nothing. The start window now opens with the "Cannot open" dialog. Benchmark runs still exit with failure.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_malformed.sh`, in an Arch container under headless sway. It reads the dialogs' text through the accessibility tree (`scripts/a11y_helpers.py`, AT-SPI), as GTK shows it. 12 checks pass:
    1. An unclosed quote after a quoted newline: "Line 5" and `3,"Lee, K,never closed`.
    2. 200k rows with 7 bad lines across chunks: lines 12, 70,002 and 70,003 listed, "…and 4 more problems". Go to Line 12, then typing and Ctrl+S, changes line 12 and nothing else. Saving doesn't ask again.
    3. A zip-like file is refused with the line and the bytes, on the start window.
    4. A well-formed file opens with no dialog.
  - Negative control: the APP-7 binary fails 9 of the 12 checks. The 3 it passes check for things that must not happen.
  - Data tests (`data-model/tests/malformed.rs`):
    - All 13 nasty-corpus files are clean (odd but well-formed).
    - Lines are placed after quoted newlines.
    - 7 problems in 200k rows are counted, with the first 5 kept in order across chunks.
    - A zero byte is found at the start and past the sample.
    - A cancelled check gives nothing.
    - Unit tests cover escaped quotes, a quote inside an unquoted field, empty quoted fields, a BOM before a quote, and excerpt cutting and escaping.
  - Not covered: rows with more fields than the header (ragged) are valid CSV for this app and aren't reported. Problems in the part of a file after an unclosed quote can't be seen, because the quote swallows it.
- **2026-10-10 APP-7:** crash recovery. After a crash, a relaunch offers the unsaved edits back.
  - Decided with the user:
    - The journal is written 5 seconds after the oldest unwritten change, so at most every 5 s while editing goes on.
    - Recovery is offered both on the start window and when the file is reopened.
    - Untitled tables are recoverable too.
  - What is journaled: the table's edit state, not the undo history (its memory budget trims old steps). That is the cell edits, the row order (sorts, inserted and deleted rows), the column order, cleared blocks, and the insert counters (`data_model::EditState`).
  - `data-model/src/journal.rs` holds the binary format: magic `BRICKSJ1`, a header, the state, and an FNV-1a hash of the rest.
    - Header: file path (none if untitled), file size and modification time, delimiter, header row, encoding, time, count of unsaved edits, rows, writer's pid.
    - Writing is deterministic: edits are sorted, and the row order goes in one write.
    - Reading refuses bad magic, a bad checksum, impossible counts, short or trailing bytes, and non-UTF-8 values.
  - `CsvTable::restore_edit` checks the state against the file before it goes in: rows and columns must exist, rows may appear once in the order, and cleared blocks must lie within the file. It needs the file fully indexed.
    - The state goes back as `Edit::Restore`, whose inverse is the state before, so a restore is one undo step.
    - Insert counters only grow, so new rows after an undone restore can't reuse ids.
  - `apps/bricks/src/recovery.rs` (no GTK) keeps journals in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/spreadsheet/recovery/<pid>-<n>.journal`, written atomically (temporary file, fsync, rename).
    - Each process holds `owner-<pid>.lock` locked (`File::lock`) while it runs. A journal whose owner's lock can be taken belongs to a process that died; another running instance's journals are left alone. Lock files go with their process's last journal.
  - `apps/bricks/src/crash.rs`:
    - The status tick writes the window's journal on the job pool.
    - The journal is deleted when nothing is unsaved (saved or all undone) and when the window closes. So one still there after the process ended means it crashed (or was killed).
    - Benchmark runs neither write journals nor offer them.
  - The offer comes once the file is indexed.
    - The file must have the size and modification time it had when the journal was written. If not: "“x.csv” changed after the crash", Keep for Later (default) or Discard.
    - The file is re-read with the journal's delimiter and encoding if they differ, and its header row set as then.
    - Reopening asks "Restore unsaved edits?" (Discard / Restore; Esc keeps it for next time). The start window's "Unsaved edits from a crash" list restores at once; its first Restore button is the window's default and focus.
    - The journal is read on the job pool; the restore goes in as "Restore unsaved edits".
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_recovery.sh`, in an Arch container under headless sway, with real key presses (`wtype`) and a real crash (`kill -9` after the journal is written). All four scenarios pass:
    1. Start window: crash; relaunch without a file; Enter (on Restore); Ctrl+S. The file holds the edit and no journal is left.
    2. Reopen: crash; relaunch on the file; the dialog; Enter; Ctrl+S. The file holds the edit.
    3. Changed file: crash; append a line; relaunch. The edits stay off it, and the journal is kept.
    4. Untitled: Ctrl+N, type, crash, restore. The crashed journal goes, and the restored window journals the value again.
  - Harness finding: with no real keyboard, each `wtype` call adds a virtual one. Focus arrived with the key in the same 4 ms (seen in `WAYLAND_DEBUG`), and GTK dropped it, so `keys()` pauses 400 ms before and after.
  - Data tests (`commands/tests/journal.rs`): every kind of edit journaled, read into a fresh table, and restored saves the same bytes. One undo takes it all back and redo re-applies it, with fresh ids afterwards. Journals are deterministic.
    - Damaged (flipped byte), cut short, trailing bytes, not a journal, and a 5-row journal on a 2-row file are all refused with nothing applied. An untitled table round-trips.
    - `app` unit tests: only gone processes' journals count as crashed; a failed write leaves the earlier journal whole.
  - 1 GB, sorted (the biggest journal: 19.1M-row order): 76.4 MB, written in 107 ms and read in 94 ms on the job pool; checked and applied in 71 ms on the UI thread (`a_sorted_1gb_table_journals_and_restores`, ignored).
  - Fixed along the way: the start window focused Open by calling `grab_focus` before the window existed. Enter now acts on the window's default widget (Restore after a crash, else Open).
  - Not covered: a journal is per window, so two windows on the same file in different processes keep two journals, and both are offered. Filters, type overrides, and the undo history are not journaled; a restore is one step.
- **2026-10-10 APP-6:** follow the desktop's dark/light preference, live; crisp text at 1.5x and 2x.
  - The grid already took its colors from the theme's foreground color, so it follows whatever GTK shows. The gap was GTK itself: before 4.20 it reads only the theme name. Desktops that signal dark mode only through the settings portal's `org.freedesktop.appearance color-scheme` (GNOME 42+ defaults, KDE, wlroots desktops via xdg-desktop-portal-gtk) left it light.
  - `apps/bricks/src/theme.rs`: at startup, read `color-scheme` from the portal (`ReadOne`, falling back to `Read` for older portals), and follow `SettingChanged`. 1 is dark; 0 and 2 are light.
    - Apply it through `gtk-interface-color-scheme` when GTK has it (4.20+, found by name at run time since the app builds against 4.14's API), else `gtk-application-prefer-dark-theme`. Setting the old switch on GTK 4.24 printed a deprecation warning on every launch.
    - The D-Bus proxy lives in a thread-local for the app's life: the first version dropped it, which silently ended live updates (the check caught it).
  - High-DPI needed no code change: GTK renders the grid's own drawing (Pango layouts in snapshots) at the output's scale. This story proves it.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_look.sh`, in Arch (GTK 4.2x, the new setting) and Ubuntu 24.04 (GTK 4.14, the old switch) containers.
    - Theme: a stand-in settings portal (`scripts/look_helpers.py portal`) says dark, then light, then dark again while the app runs. Screenshots measure grid background and text: 0.18 and 1.00, then 1.00 and 0.00, then 0.18 and 1.00, on both distros.
    - Scale: headless sway switches its output 1x, 1.5x, 2x live with the app open; Xvfb runs at `GDK_SCALE` 1 and 2. Measure: of the pixels half-way to ink, the share that is solid ink. Text must be at least 95% as crisp as at 1x, and at least twice as crisp as a 1x screenshot scaled up (what a blurry window looks like); 1x itself must measure as text (>= 0.3).
    - Results: Arch 1.5x 0.65, 2x 0.75, X11 2x 0.74 (1x 0.48 to 0.51; scaled-up 1x 0.03 to 0.09). Ubuntu with the GL renderer: 0.65, 0.72, 0.70 (1x 0.41; scaled-up 0.03 to 0.12). Two runs gave the same numbers.
    - Negative control: the binary built before this story stayed light when the portal said dark.
    - The check also fails if the app prints any GTK warning.
  - Smoke on this desktop (Hyprland; portal `color-scheme` = 1), through Broadway: the app came up dark even with `GTK_THEME=Adwaita` forcing the light theme name.
  - Known limit, GTK's: Ubuntu 24.04's GTK 4.14 on a system without a GPU falls back to the cairo renderer, which draws 1.5x at 2x for the compositor to shrink. Text there measured 0.32 to 0.42 (1x 0.41 to 0.46): softer, as in every GTK 4.14 app. With any GPU (GL or Vulkan renderer) it is crisp. Newer GTK (Arch) is crisp even in software.
  - Container note: Ubuntu's sway 1.9 refuses to start when the host has the Nvidia kernel module loaded, so it runs with `--unsupported-gpu` (the headless software output never uses a GPU).
- **2026-10-10 APP-5:** drag and drop to open. Files dropped on the start window or any file window open as Open does: each in its own window (or brought forward if already open), recorded in recent files; a non-local URI or an unreadable file shows the usual error.
  - `accept_file_drops` adds a `gtk::DropTarget` for `gdk::FileList` (GTK fills it from `text/uri-list` on both Wayland and X11) to every window. The windows open from an idle callback after the drop completes, because opening a file closes the start window, which may be the drop's own target. The start window's hint mentions dropping.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_drop.sh`. In an Arch container, headless sway (Wayland) and Xvfb (X11) each run the app on its start window, with a GTK drag-source window (`scripts/dnd_helpers.py source`) offering a file as `text/uri-list`, as file managers do.
    - The pointer is real input to each display server: the wlr-virtual-pointer protocol via pywayland on sway, XTEST via xdotool on X11.
    - It drags first.csv onto the start window, then second.csv onto first.csv's window. Both displays end with windows titled first.csv and second.csv, and recent files "second, first".
    - Negative control: the release binary built before this story fails the same check on both (the start window stays).
  - Container note: Arch's sway binary carries the `cap_sys_nice` file capability, so the container needs `--cap-add SYS_NICE` to exec it.
  - Not tested: a drag from a real file manager (Nautilus, Dolphin). They offer the same `text/uri-list`, plus a portal format for Flatpak senders, which GTK also reads.
- **2026-10-10 decision:** Flatpak (PKG-2) is deferred; the curl installer is the M4 distribution (user decision). PKG-2 stays P2 and todo, so it doesn't hold the M4 gate.
  - The work is parked, unmerged, on branch `story/pkg-2-flatpak` (ea4b959). Its manifest builds, installs, and runs in Flathub's own CI image (GNOME 51), checked by `scripts/check_flatpak.sh`.
  - Before a submission it needs: an app ID on a domain we control (or `io.github.<user>.<repo>`), a homepage URL, hosted screenshots, and either a Flathub exception for home-folder access or a move to portals.
- **2026-10-10 PKG-1:** curl installer. `curl -fsSL <page>/install.sh | sh` installs or updates for the user, without sudo; `| sh -s -- --uninstall` removes it.
  - `site/install.sh` (POSIX sh, since it is piped into `sh`): reads `latest.json` from the download bucket (`ship.sh site` fills in `DOWNLOAD_BASE_URL`; `SPREADSHEET_DOWNLOAD_URL` overrides it for tests). It downloads with curl or wget, checks the sha256, and installs nothing on a mismatch.
    - The binary goes to `~/.local/bin` by copy and rename, so a running copy isn't written into. The icon (new `packaging/spreadsheet.svg`, now in the tarball) goes to `~/.local/share/icons/hicolor/scalable/apps`.
    - The desktop entry goes to `~/.local/share/applications`, with `Exec=` the absolute path, so a launcher starts it even when `~/.local/bin` isn't on its PATH.
  - `text/csv` and `text/tab-separated-values` defaults go into `~/.config/mimeapps.list` directly (`xdg-mime` isn't always installed).
    - Every other line and section stays. Uninstall removes only the entries naming `spreadsheet.desktop`, so another app's default for CSV survives.
    - Two bugs found while reviewing before the container runs: uninstall dropped every CSV default, and the function reused the `tmp` variable the download's cleanup trap needed.
  - Then it runs `spreadsheet --version`. If the loader can't find GTK 4, it says so with the distro's command from `/etc/os-release` (`pacman -S gtk4`, `apt install libgtk-4-1`, `dnf install gtk4`, `zypper install libgtk-4-1`, else generic) and exits 2. It also says when `~/.local/bin` isn't on PATH.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/check_install.sh` (also run by `ship.sh verify`). A throwaway site on localhost serves the tarball, a tampered copy, and latest.json; Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora containers run as a normal user:
    - Without GTK: installs, then prints the right package command; exit 2. With a tampered tarball: "checksum mismatch … nothing installed", and nothing is.
    - With GTK, and another app's `image/png` and `text/csv` defaults already there: installs; `gio mime text/csv` says spreadsheet.desktop.
    - `gio open /tmp/t.csv`, which is what a file manager's double-click calls, starts the app on the file: its recent-files list (APP-4) then holds `/tmp/t.csv`.
    - Reinstall updates. Uninstall leaves no binary or desktop entry and keeps the other app's `image/png` default.
    - All three ok. `VERBOSE=1` and `ONLY=<distro>` help when one fails.
  - Also changed: the download page shows the curl command for its own address, plus the uninstall line, and the AUR sentence is gone. The README has an Install section.
  - The ship-it skill now checks after deploy that the live `install.sh` names the download URL.
  - Not yet proven: the M4 gate asks for VMs with a desktop and a real double-click in a file manager. Containers prove the same path (`gio open` uses the same defaults a file manager does) but not a file manager itself.
- **2026-10-10 PKG-3:** the release binary is built in an Ubuntu 24.04 container and checked on clean Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora.
  - `packaging/build.Dockerfile`: Ubuntu 24.04 (glibc 2.39, GTK 4.14, the oldest we support), `libgtk-4-dev`, and stable Rust with the components `rust-toolchain.toml` names.
    - `scripts/ship.sh build` runs cargo in it as the calling user, with its own `CARGO_HOME` and target dir under `target/container`, so host builds are untouched.
    - Preflight now checks for docker. `ship.sh` was mode 644 in git although the ship-it skill runs it directly; it is executable now.
  - Acceptance: `scripts/ship.sh verify` (`scripts/check_release.sh`). For each distro a fresh container installs only GTK 4's runtime from its own packages (`gtk4`; `libgtk-4-1` and `libgtk-4-bin` on Ubuntu). The binary then opens a CSV under Broadway with `--bench-open` and must print its first frame and quit. That loads every linked library and draws a window.
    - It also fails if any glibc symbol is newer than 2.39.
    - Result: Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora all ok; the newest glibc symbol is GLIBC_2.34; tarball 873 KB.
  - Finding: a binary built on this Arch host passes the same check today. Rust's std targets old glibc symbols, and gtk-rs's `v4_14` feature keeps to GTK 4.14's API. My replan note said otherwise, and is corrected.
    - The container makes it a guarantee rather than luck: a newer toolchain or a -sys crate can't raise the floor unnoticed, and `verify` would catch it if they did.
  - `--version` added (`spreadsheet 0.0.1`) for the installer and the skill's build check. `packaging/aur/PKGBUILD` removed (no AUR).
- **2026-10-10 APP-4:** recent files: the last 10 files opened or saved, newest first, kept across restarts.
  - `apps/bricks/src/recent.rs` (no GTK): one absolute path per line in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/spreadsheet/recent` (default `~/.local/state/spreadsheet/recent`), raw bytes, so any file name works except one with a newline.
    - Each change reads the file again first, because every launch is its own process (`NON_UNIQUE`), then writes a temporary file and renames it over the list.
    - Opening a file again moves it to the top, once.
  - Recorded: files opened from the dialog, the list, or the command line, and the target of a Save As. Runs with a `--bench-*` flag are left off, so tests and `cargo bench` don't fill the list with corpus files.
  - UI:
    - The start window lists them under "Recent" (name, then the folder with `~` for home; the full path as tooltip).
    - Each window's header has a ▾ menu next to Open (`app.open-recent` with the path, read again each time it opens), plus "Clear Recent Files".
    - A file that can't be opened shows the error and leaves the list. A path that isn't UTF-8 stays on the start window but can't go in the menu (action targets are strings).
  - Acceptance: `recent::tests::the_last_ten_files_survive_a_restart`. 12 files added, then a fresh load reads the newest 10 in order; opening one again moves it to the top without a duplicate.
    - Also tested: two windows' writes merge; remove and clear; relative and odd names; labels.
  - Smoke test via Broadway with `XDG_STATE_HOME` in /tmp:
    - Launches with alpha.csv, then beta.csv, wrote "beta, alpha". A launch with no file showed both on the start window.
    - Clicking alpha opened it and moved it to the top. The header ▾ listed alpha, beta, and Clear Recent Files.
    - With beta.csv deleted, choosing it showed "Cannot open /tmp/app4/beta.csv: No such file or directory" and left only alpha on the list.
- **2026-10-10 decision:** no AUR (user decision: it is dead). M4 ships a curl installer instead: `curl -fsSL https://bricks.cronkite.cc/install.sh | sh` on the Cloudflare download site.
  - It reads `latest.json`, downloads the tarball, and checks its sha256. It installs per user, without sudo: the binary in `~/.local/bin`, the .desktop file and icon in `~/.local/share`, and the text/csv default via `xdg-mime`.
  - Rerunning it updates; `--uninstall` removes it. It can't install GTK 4, so it prints the distro's install command when GTK 4 is missing.
  - The gate needs clean Arch, Ubuntu 24.04, and Fedora VMs (user decision). New story PKG-3 builds the release against Ubuntu 24.04's libraries, so it starts on the oldest system we support. PKG-1 is now the installer and depends on it.
- **2026-10-10 FILT-4:** edits and paste respect the filtered view, and the FILT-1 refusals are gone.
  - Typing, Delete, copy, and paste already reached only the rows shown (FILT-1). This story proves paste and lifts the refusals.
  - Decided with the user:
    - A paste that runs past the last row shown adds rows at the end of the file, as when unfiltered. They pass every filter until it is applied again, so they stay shown.
    - Deleting rows while filtered deletes only the selected rows shown, as one undo step; hidden rows between them stay. Inserting puts empty rows next to the row shown, and they stay shown too. Excel and Calc behave this way.
  - Data model:
    - `CsvTable::insert_rows(row, n)` puts rows before shown row `row`'s position, or at the end of the file when `row == row_count()`.
    - `delete_rows(row, n)` maps the shown rows to positions (`view_ranges`). One range is a plain `Edit::DeleteRows`. Several are the new `Edit::DeleteRowRanges(Vec<Range>)`, whose inverse is `Edit::InsertRowRanges(Vec<(at, runs)>)`.
    - `RowOrder::remove_ranges`/`insert_ranges` take all the ranges in one step: one copy pass for a sorted order (a delete per range would copy the whole permutation each time), one treap remove per range for runs. `Reshape` labels and focuses the new variants.
  - Removed: `PasteError::Filtered`, `ClipView::Filtered` and its status message, `GridView::refused_while_filtered`, and the filtered-only refusal in `insert_rows`/`delete_rows`.
    - The grid's edge row (type below the last row to add one) is back while filtered. `shown_rows` had dropped it; the smoke test found that.
  - **Bug fixed from EDIT-3 (row-map treap):** cutting a run in `split` gave the new right half a fresh random priority. That could outrank the ancestors it lands under, which breaks the heap order. Split after split, the tree became a chain: depth 7,230 for 80,000 runs, quadratic time, then a stack overflow when deleting the 1.36M rows shown in the 1 GB file.
    - The new half now draws a priority at or below the split node's (`node_below`). The same 80,000 runs: depth 47, 9 ms instead of 4.1 s.
    - `the_tree_stays_shallow_through_many_splits` (200,000 runs, depth < 120) would have failed before the fix.
    - `insert_1gb` is unchanged (insert and delete at row 1M in 0.03 ms).
  - Acceptance: `commands/tests/filter_edits.rs` `paste_into_a_filtered_range_touches_visible_rows_only`. A 3×2 paste over Denver rows 1, 3, and 5 changes those rows only; rows 2, 4, and 6 are saved unchanged, and undo restores the file byte for byte.
    - Also tested: a paste past the end adds rows at the end of the file, shown, and one undo removes them.
    - Deleting all shown rows is one step with hidden rows kept, in file order and in a sorted order; undo puts them back in place. Neighbours with nothing hidden between them are a plain `DeleteRows`.
    - Inserting puts rows next to the row shown and at the end.
    - `RowOrder` ranges round-trip against a flat vector.
    - The old FILT-1 assertions that pastes, inserts, and deletes are refused are gone.
  - 1 GB (`commands/tests/filter_1gb.rs` `deleting_the_rows_shown_on_1gb`, ignored): deleting the 1,362,719 rows shown (Portland, revenue > 50,000) takes 352 ms in file order (undo 505 ms) and 95 ms sorted (undo 100 ms), on the UI thread.
  - Smoke test via Broadway on a /tmp copy of the 10 MB file, filtered to Denver (27,431 of 193,311):
    - Rows 3–5 (ids 3, 10, 15, with hidden rows between them) deleted with Ctrl+- (27,428 of 193,308); Ctrl+Z brought them back.
    - Ctrl++ inserted an empty row, shown above id 3.
    - On a fresh copy, typing 999999 into the edge row added a shown row. After Ctrl+S, `diff` showed only `999999,,,,,,,` appended at the end of the file. The filter, applied again on the reopened file, then hid it (city is empty), as filters do.
  - Follow-up: deleting or undoing a million shown rows in file order takes 0.35–0.5 s on the UI thread (one treap operation per range). It could move to a job, or the runs could be rebuilt in one pass like the sorted order.
- **2026-10-09 TYPE-2:** each header shows its column's type, and the user can override it.
  - Decided with the user:
    - The type shows as a dim tag right of the title, left of ▾: 123, 1.5, abc, date, T/F. It is blue when overridden, and narrow columns drop it before the button.
    - The ▾ popover gets a "Type" dropdown: "Automatic (whole numbers)" (the inferred type, named), Text, Number, Date, True/false.
    - In a Number column, detected or overridden, number filter conditions read every cell that parses as a number, zero-padded included: "00999" > 500 passes. In other columns they take only plain numbers, as before.
    - The popover hides the number conditions for columns that aren't numbers. A number filter already on keeps them listed.
  - My calls:
    - Overrides aren't undoable, like filters: they change no data.
    - They stay with their column through header flips and new inferences.
    - A save gives them back by column position, before the filters, since filters read them.
    - Changing a column's type re-applies a number filter on that column. Text filters don't depend on the type.
  - `data_model`:
    - `CsvTable::type_overrides` (by `ColId`) sit apart from the inferred `types`. `column_type(col)` (what sort and filter use) returns the override first, then the inferred type.
    - Accessors: `inferred_type(col)`, `type_override(col)`, `set_type_override(col, Option<InferredType>)`.
    - The filter's `Matcher` takes the column's numberness from `type_of(filter.col)`. Its numeric reading accepts only digits, sign, point, and exponent: no "inf", "NaN", or "1,234".
  - Acceptance: `file-format/tests/type_override.rs`, on a file whose `code` column ("00999", 10, 9, abc, blank) is inferred Text.
    - Sort goes from 00999, 10, 9, abc to 9, 10, 00999, abc with Number. A number column read as Text sorts as text.
    - "> 500" goes from no rows to "00999".
    - Values, edits (none), and the saved file are unchanged (the saved bytes equal the source). Automatic brings the inferred order back.
    - A second test: an override survives a header flip and a new inference.
